**CPMN**

CarePlanMaster for Nurses

**Jwad Waadallah Basheer**

College of Nursing, University of Mosul

# Preface

Most nursing students meet the care plan for the first time in clinical training, and most of them get stuck at the same step: they have pages of patient data, but they cannot see how it turns into a nursing diagnosis, and from there into interventions that someone can actually measure. I wrote this book for those students, and for ward nurses who want a quick, reliable reference beside them on a busy shift.

Every condition gets one page, and every page looks the same: assessment (subjective and objective data), nursing diagnosis, plan and expected outcome, interventions, and evaluation. On each page you will find two actual diagnoses and one risk diagnosis. I chose them because they are the priorities you will meet most often with that condition. Keeping one layout from start to finish means you can find what you need in seconds, whether you are studying at home or standing at the bedside.

Please remember that these plans describe the typical patient, and your patient is never exactly typical. Use them as a starting point, then adjust the diagnoses, outcomes and interventions to the person in front of you, to the doctor's orders and to your hospital's policies. Diagnostic labels follow NANDA International terminology.

If you find a mistake or have an idea that would make the next edition better, I would be glad to hear it from colleagues and students alike.

**Jwad Waadallah Basheer**

College of Nursing, University of Mosul

**PART 1**

# Foundations of Care Planning

## 1.1  How to Use This Book

Each care plan fits on one page and is set out in five columns, one for each step of the nursing process. Read across a row from left to right. The assessment data in the first column are the evidence for the diagnosis beside them, and the outcome, interventions and evaluation in that same row all belong to that one diagnosis.

- Assessment: subjective data (what the patient or family tells you) and objective data (what you observe or measure yourself, or find in the records and investigation results).

- Nursing diagnosis: two actual diagnoses and one risk diagnosis, marked ACTUAL and RISK.

- Plan / expected outcome: a patient outcome you can measure, with a time limit attached.

- Nursing interventions: what the nurse does, whether that is monitoring, hands-on care, working with other staff or teaching.

- Evaluation: how you decide whether the outcome was reached, and what to do next if it was not.

Remember that these are common presentations. Figures such as a target pain score, a urine output or a time frame are typical values only; change them to suit your patient, the physician's orders and hospital policy.

## 1.2  The Nursing Process

The nursing process is simply an organised way of planning and giving care around the patient. It has five steps, and they do not happen once and stop; you go round them again and again for as long as the patient is in your care.

- Assessment: gathering patient data, checking that it is accurate and putting it in order.

- Diagnosis: looking at that data to find actual and potential health problems that a nurse can treat independently.

- Planning: deciding which problem comes first and writing outcomes that can be measured.

- Implementation: doing the planned interventions and writing down what was done.

- Evaluation: checking the patient's response against the expected outcome and changing the plan if needed.

**Setting priorities**

Anything that threatens life comes first. A practical order to follow is airway, then breathing, then circulation; after those come other physical needs, then safety, and then psychosocial needs. In general an actual problem is dealt with before a risk problem, but a serious risk, such as aspiration or a fall, may need action straight away.

## 1.3  Assessment Data

Subjective data are what the patient says about their own symptoms, feelings and worries, for example "I feel short of breath when I walk." Write these down in the patient's own words whenever you can. Objective data are the things you can see or measure: vital signs, examination findings, intake and output, lab results and imaging reports.

Only record data that are relevant to the diagnosis. One finding on its own is weak evidence; a group of related cues pointing the same way is much stronger.

## 1.4  Writing Nursing Diagnoses

**Actual (problem-focused) diagnosis**

An actual diagnosis names a problem the patient has right now, at the time you assess them. It has three parts, often called the PES format: the problem (the diagnostic label), the etiology (related factors), and the signs and symptoms (defining characteristics).

Example: Acute pain related to surgical tissue trauma as evidenced by pain score 7/10, guarding of the incision and tachycardia.

**Risk diagnosis**

A risk diagnosis is about a problem that has not happened yet but probably will, given the risk factors you have found. Because the problem does not exist yet, there are no signs and symptoms to list. It has only two parts: the label and the risk factors.

Example: Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: surgical incision, peripheral IV cannula and diabetes mellitus.

**Common errors**

- Putting a medical diagnosis in the etiology, such as "related to pneumonia". Write the cause a nurse can act on instead, such as "related to retained secretions".

- Writing a nursing action or a need as the problem, such as "needs suctioning".

- Adding signs and symptoms to a risk diagnosis.

## 1.5  Planning and Expected Outcomes

An expected outcome describes how the patient will change. It does not describe what the nurse will do. A good outcome is specific, measurable, achievable, relevant and has a time limit. The patient is always the subject of the sentence, and the verb is something you can observe.

Correct: The patient will report pain of 3/10 or less within 1 hour of analgesia.

Incorrect: The nurse will give analgesia every 6 hours. (That sentence is an intervention, not an outcome.)

## 1.6  Nursing Interventions

Interventions are the actions you take to reach the outcome. Write them clearly enough that a nurse coming on the next shift could carry them out without asking you.

- Independent interventions: things the nurse starts on the basis of nursing knowledge, for example positioning, deep-breathing exercises and patient teaching.

- Dependent interventions: things that need a physician's order, for example giving medications or starting IV fluids.

- Collaborative interventions: things done together with other members of the team, for example the physiotherapist, dietitian or pharmacist.

## 1.7  Evaluation

In evaluation you compare how the patient actually responded with the outcome you expected. Record the result as goal met, goal partially met or goal not met, and back it up with the data you collected. If the goal was not met, go back through each step of the plan. Were the data complete? Was the diagnosis right? Was the outcome realistic? Were the interventions done, and were they the right ones? Then revise the plan.

**PART 2**

# General Care Plans

## 2.1  Pre-operative Care

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I am afraid I will not wake up after the operation." Says he slept badly the night before surgery **Objective** HR 104/min, BP 138/88 mmHg Restless, keeps asking questions Hands trembling | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to anticipated surgery and unfamiliar environment as evidenced by verbalized fear, restlessness and tachycardia. | He tells you he feels less anxious before he goes to theatre. HR back to his baseline within 2 hours. | Ask how anxious he feels and what exactly frightens him. Walk him through what happens before, during and after surgery, in plain words. Give him time to ask questions and say how he feels. If policy allows, let a relative stay with him until transfer. Show him how to breathe slowly and deeply. Give the prescribed pre-medication and note how he responds. | He says he feels calmer and HR is at baseline: goal met. Still very anxious? Tell the surgeon or anesthetist. |
| **Subjective** "I do not know what will happen after surgery." Asks if he is allowed to eat in the morning **Objective** Cannot explain the fasting instructions Cannot show you deep breathing or coughing | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to lack of exposure to perioperative routines as evidenced by questions and inaccurate statements about preparation. | Before surgery he can tell you his fasting time and the pre-op instructions. He can show you deep breathing, coughing and leg exercises. | Find out what he already knows and how he learns best. Tell him when to stop eating, and to take off jewellery, dentures and nail polish. Teach deep breathing, coughing while splinting the incision, and leg exercises, then watch him do them back. Explain the pain scale, and tell him analgesia will be there for him after the operation. Go through the pre-op checklist and make sure the consent is signed. | Repeats the instructions correctly and does the exercises: goal met. Teaching not finished? Go over it again and bring the family in. |
| **Subjective** Diabetic for 8 years Smokes 15 cigarettes a day **Objective** Blood glucose 190 mg/dL Booked for abdominal surgery BMI 32 kg/m² | **RISK** Risk for surgical site infection as evidenced by risk factors: diabetes mellitus, smoking, obesity and planned surgical incision. | No SSI: wound clean, no redness or discharge, normal temperature for 30 days after surgery. | Check blood glucose as ordered and report anything above target. Ask him to shower with antiseptic soap, following policy. Do not shave the skin; if hair has to come off, use clippers. Make sure the prophylactic antibiotic is given within the ordered time before incision. Encourage him to stop smoking, both before and after the operation. | No sign of infection while he is in hospital: goal met. Fever, redness or discharge from the wound should be reported straight away. |

## 2.2  Post-operative Care

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The pain near the wound is severe." Gives his pain 8/10 **Objective** Guards the incision Grimacing HR 110/min, BP 145/90 mmHg | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgical tissue trauma as evidenced by pain score 8/10, guarding and tachycardia. | Pain down to 3/10 or less within 1 hour of analgesia. Able to deep breathe and get up early, with pain he can tolerate. | Score pain on a numeric scale every 2–4 hours and again after each intervention. Give analgesics on time; if he is on opioids, keep an eye on sedation and respiratory rate. Help him into a comfortable position, and have him hold a pillow over the wound when he coughs. Time activities so they come after his analgesia. Add non-drug measures as well, such as relaxation and a quiet room. | Pain 3/10 or less when rechecked: goal met. Pain still not controlled? Go over the analgesic plan with the physician. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot cough because it hurts." **Objective** Coarse crackles at both bases Cough weak, not clearing anything SpO₂ 92% on room air RR 24/min | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to effects of anesthesia and pain on coughing as evidenced by crackles, weak cough and reduced SpO₂. | Chest clear on auscultation and SpO₂ 95% or more within 24 hours. | Listen to the chest and check SpO₂ every 4 hours. Raise the head of the bed to 30–45 degrees. Every 1–2 hours, get him to breathe deeply and cough with the incision splinted. While he is awake he should use the incentive spirometer 10 times an hour. Get him out of bed early, as ordered. Give oxygen as prescribed. | Chest clear, SpO₂ 96%: goal met. Secretions still there? Think about chest physiotherapy and let the physician know. |
| **Subjective** Feels dizzy when he sits up **Objective** Abdominal surgery 4 hours ago Wound drain in situ On prophylactic anticoagulant | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: recent surgery, surgical drain and anticoagulant therapy. | Vital signs stable, dressing dry, drain output within the expected range. | Vital signs every 15 minutes for the first hour, then as per policy. Look at the dressing and the drain; mark the level and record what drains. Follow Hb and coagulation results. Report HR over 100/min, a falling BP, or a drain that is filling fast. Stay with him the first time he walks, because postural hypotension can make him fall. | Vital signs stable and very little drainage: goal met. Active bleeding goes to the surgeon immediately. |

## 2.3  Acute Pain

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The pain is sharp and does not go away." Scores the pain 7/10 **Objective** Guards the painful area Restless and grimacing HR 106/min, RR 22/min | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to physical injury agent as evidenced by pain score 7/10, guarding and increased HR. | Pain 3/10 or less within 1 hour of the intervention. Able to rest and join in with his care. | Ask where the pain is, how bad it is, what it feels like and how long it lasts. Give analgesics as prescribed and check again after 30–60 minutes. Support the painful area in a comfortable position. Use cold or warm packs if ordered. Teach relaxation and slow breathing. Take the patient's word for the pain, and write it down. | Pain at 3/10 or less: goal met. If not, go over the analgesic regimen with the physician. |
| **Subjective** "I wake up because of the pain." Says he sleeps about 3 hours a night **Objective** Yawning, dark circles under the eyes Irritable | **ACTUAL** Disturbed sleep pattern related to pain as evidenced by frequent waking and reported short sleep. | Sleeping at least 5 hours a night within 2 nights. | Give analgesia before bed. Keep noise and lights down at night. Bunch care together so he gets longer stretches of rest. Let him keep his usual bedtime routine. | Says he slept 5–6 hours: goal met. If not, look again at how well his pain is controlled at night. |
| **Subjective** No bowel movement for 2 days **Objective** On opioid analgesics Moving less than usual | **RISK** Risk for constipation as evidenced by risk factors: opioid use and reduced physical activity. | Passes soft stool at least every 2–3 days. | Chart bowel movements every day. Unless fluids are restricted, aim for 2–2.5 L/day. Encourage fibre in the diet, and as much movement as he can manage. Give the prescribed stool softener or laxative alongside the opioid. | Soft stool every 2 days: goal met. No stool for 3 days, or a distended abdomen, needs reporting. |

## 2.4  Chronic Pain

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My back has hurt for more than a year." Puts the pain at 6/10 most days **Objective** Posture and gait have changed Doing much less than before Has used analgesics for a long time | **ACTUAL** Chronic pain related to chronic musculoskeletal condition as evidenced by pain for more than 3 months and reduced activity. | Within 1 week, pain is at a level that lets her get through daily activities. She can use two pain relief methods that do not involve drugs. | Find out the pattern of the pain, what sets it off and how it affects her day. Give analgesics on a regular schedule, as prescribed. Teach heat application, relaxation and how to pace her activities. Encourage gentle exercise along the lines the physiotherapist recommends. Talk about the risks of taking too much analgesia. | Manages daily activities with pain at 4/10 or less: goal met. If not, refer her to the pain clinic. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot fall asleep because of the pain." **Objective** Tired, finds it hard to concentrate Sleeps less than 4 hours a night | **ACTUAL** Insomnia related to persistent pain as evidenced by difficulty falling asleep and fatigue. | Says her sleep is better within 1 week. | Ask about her sleep pattern. Suggest going to bed at the same time each night and no caffeine in the evening. Give analgesia before bed, as prescribed. Teach a relaxation routine to use before sleep. | Sleeping 6 hours or more: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Nothing I do helps." **Objective** Has been ill for a long time Can no longer do her usual role at work and at home | **RISK** Risk for powerlessness as evidenced by risk factors: chronic illness and loss of control over daily life. | She takes part in decisions about her pain plan. | Let her help set the goals. Give her choices about when care and activities happen. Point out progress, however small. Refer to a support group or counselling when it seems right. | Joins in planning and sets herself realistic goals: goal met. |

## 2.5  Immobility

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot move my legs well." **Objective** Limited range of movement in both legs Needs help to turn in bed Muscle strength 3/5 | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to neuromuscular impairment as evidenced by limited ROM and inability to turn without help. | Within 1 week, joint ROM is kept and he moves with assistance. | Check muscle strength and ROM every day. Do passive or active ROM exercises 3 times a day. Turn him every 2 hours and support him with pillows. Agree a mobility plan with the physiotherapist. Bring in assistive devices where they help. | No contractures, movement getting better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I need help to wash." **Objective** Cannot reach the lower half of his body Needs a hand with bathing | **ACTUAL** Bathing self-care deficit related to impaired mobility as evidenced by inability to wash body parts. | Within 3 days he takes part in his own bath as far as he is able. | Find out how much he can do on his own. Put soap, towel and basin where he can reach them. Let him do what he can, and help with the rest. Keep him covered and protect his dignity. | Washes his face, arms and chest himself: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Says his calves feel heavy **Objective** On bed rest for over 3 days Age 68 years Had surgery recently | **RISK** Risk for thrombosis as evidenced by risk factors: immobility, older age and recent surgery. | No DVT: no pain, swelling or redness in the calves. | Check both calves every day for pain, swelling and warmth. While awake, he should do ankle pumps and leg exercises every hour. Put on compression stockings or devices if ordered. Give the prophylactic anticoagulant as prescribed. Keep him well hydrated. | No signs of DVT during the admission: goal met. Swelling of one leg or sudden breathlessness must be reported at once. |

## 2.6  Risk for Falls

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel unsteady when I walk." **Objective** Shuffling gait Holds on to furniture to walk Muscle weakness | **ACTUAL** Impaired walking related to muscle weakness as evidenced by unsteady gait and need for support. | Within 3 days she walks safely with a walking aid. | Check her gait and balance. Give her a walker or cane and show her how to use it properly. Walk with her until she is steady. Work with the physiotherapist. | Walks with the aid and keeps her balance: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I did not know I should call for help." **Objective** Gets out of bed by herself at night Does not realise she could fall | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to fall prevention as evidenced by unsafe behaviour and statements. | Within 24 hours, patient and family can name three ways to prevent a fall. | Explain why she is at risk and why the precautions matter. Show her the call bell and teach her to get up slowly. Ask the family to keep the floor clear. Check understanding with teach-back. | Rings for help before getting up: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Feels dizzy when she stands Fell once in the past year **Objective** Age 75 years On an antihypertensive and a sedative Nocturia | **RISK** Risk for adult falls as evidenced by risk factors: history of falls, older age, medications and nocturia. | No falls during her stay in hospital. | Score her fall risk on admission and every day with a validated scale. Bed low, wheels locked, side rails as per policy. Call bell and personal things within easy reach. Non-slip footwear and a night light. Offer the toilet every 2 hours and before she settles for the night. Look for orthostatic hypotension. | No falls while in hospital: goal met. If she does fall, check her for injury and report it as policy requires. |

## 2.7  Pressure Injury Prevention

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "It is hard for me to change position." **Objective** Cannot turn without help Lies in the same position for long periods | **ACTUAL** Impaired bed mobility related to weakness as evidenced by inability to reposition independently. | Repositioned at least every 2 hours, with no pressure redness. | Turn every 2 hours, following a turning chart. Put pillows between bony prominences so they do not touch. Use slide sheets so the skin is not dragged. Ask the patient to help with turning where he can. | Skin intact, no redness that stays: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot reach the toilet in time." **Objective** Bed linen wet Cannot walk to the toilet alone | **ACTUAL** Functional urinary incontinence related to impaired mobility as evidenced by urine leakage before reaching toilet. | Within 3 days his skin is dry and he is incontinent less often. | Offer a bedpan or urinal every 2 hours. Clean and dry the skin quickly after every episode. Put on barrier cream. Do not pile layers of pads and sheets under him. | Skin dry and intact: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Says he has no appetite **Objective** Braden score 12 Thin, with bony prominences standing out Albumin low | **RISK** Risk for adult pressure injury as evidenced by risk factors: immobility, moisture, poor nutrition and low Braden score. | No pressure injury develops during his stay. | Check the skin over bony areas every shift. Nurse him on a pressure-redistributing mattress. Unless there is a reason not to, keep the head of the bed at 30 degrees or less. Refer to the dietitian and encourage protein. Score the Braden scale again every day. | No pressure injury: goal met. Any redness that does not blanch must be reported and documented. |

## 2.8  Infection and Isolation Precautions

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel lonely in this room." **Objective** In a single room under contact isolation Few visitors Quiet and withdrawn | **ACTUAL** Social isolation related to infection control precautions as evidenced by expressed loneliness and withdrawal. | Within 48 hours, says she feels less lonely. | Explain why she is isolated and roughly how long it will last. Drop in and chat with her regularly, not only when a procedure is due. Encourage phone calls with the family. Show visitors how to wear protective equipment properly so they can keep coming. | Says she feels less alone: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Why do the staff wear gowns?" "Can my children visit?" **Objective** Takes the mask off at the door Hand hygiene done wrongly | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to transmission precautions as evidenced by questions and incorrect practice. | Within 24 hours, patient and family show correct hand hygiene and precautions. | Explain how this organism spreads. Demonstrate hand hygiene, and how to put on a mask and gown. Give written instructions. Watch them do it back. | Doing it correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Culture grows a multidrug-resistant organism Has an IV cannula and a urinary catheter Long hospital stay | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: invasive devices and prolonged hospitalization. | No new infection: temperature and WBC normal, device sites clean. | Hand hygiene before and after every contact. Keep equipment for this patient only. Check device sites daily and take out any device that is no longer needed. Watch temperature and WBC. | No new infection: goal met. |

## 2.9  Fever

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel hot and then cold." **Objective** Temperature 39.2 °C Skin flushed and warm HR 112/min | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to infectious process as evidenced by temperature 39.2 °C, warm skin and tachycardia. | Temperature below 38 °C within 4 hours. | Take the temperature every 2–4 hours. Give the antipyretic as prescribed and recheck 1 hour later. Take off extra clothes and covers; tepid sponge if ordered. Take blood cultures before the antibiotics go in, as ordered. Give antibiotics on time. | Temperature down to 37.6 °C: goal met. Report fever that will not settle, or rigors. |
| **Subjective** "I have body aches and chills." **Objective** Shivering Restless | **ACTUAL** Impaired comfort related to fever as evidenced by chills and body aches. | Says she feels more comfortable within 2 hours. | Dress her in light, dry clothes and change damp sheets quickly. Keep the room at a comfortable temperature and let air move. Give the prescribed antipyretic and analgesic for the aches. Give mouth care and sips of fluid. Plan care so she can rest without being disturbed. | Says she is comfortable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Thirsty, drinking less than usual **Objective** Sweating Temperature 39 °C Lips dry | **RISK** Risk for deficient fluid volume as evidenced by risk factors: increased insensible loss and reduced oral intake. | Urine output at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour and mucous membranes moist. | Unless restricted, encourage 2–3 L/day of oral fluids. Keep an I&O chart. Give IV fluids as ordered. Watch the urine colour and specific gravity. | Urine output adequate: goal met. |

## 2.10  Deficient Fluid Volume

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel thirsty and dizzy." **Objective** BP 95/60 mmHg, HR 115/min Urine output 20 mL/hour, urine dark Skin turgor poor | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to active fluid loss as evidenced by hypotension, tachycardia and low urine output. | Within 8 hours, urine output is at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour and vital signs are stable. | Vital signs every hour until stable. Give IV fluids as prescribed and keep checking the rate. Record urine output every hour, along with I&O. Weigh daily, at the same time each day. Follow serum electrolytes, urea and creatinine. Once allowed, encourage drinking. | BP and HR normal, urine output adequate: goal met. If not, ask the physician to review the fluid plan. |
| **Subjective** "My mouth is very dry." **Objective** Lips dry and cracked Dry tongue | **ACTUAL** Impaired oral mucous membrane integrity related to dehydration as evidenced by dry, cracked lips and tongue. | Mouth moist again within 24 hours. | Mouth care every 4 hours. Put moisturiser on the lips. If allowed, give small sips of water often. | Mouth moist: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Still vomiting **Objective** Fluid loss continuing BP dropping | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: hypovolemia. | MAP stays above 65 mmHg and level of consciousness stays normal. | Check level of consciousness and capillary refill. Report a systolic BP below 90 mmHg. Keep a large-bore IV line in place. Be ready for fluid resuscitation as ordered. | No signs of shock: goal met. |

## 2.11  Excess Fluid Volume

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My legs are swollen." **Objective** Pitting edema +3, both legs Put on 2 kg in 2 days Taking in more than he passes | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to compromised regulatory mechanisms as evidenced by edema, weight gain and positive fluid balance. | Within 3 days the edema is less and his weight is coming down toward baseline. | Weigh every day, same time, same scale. Keep a strict I&O chart. Limit fluids and sodium as prescribed. Give diuretics as ordered, and watch the potassium. Raise the swollen limbs. | Less edema, weight falling: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I get short of breath when lying flat." **Objective** Crackles at lung bases SpO₂ 91% RR 26/min | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to pulmonary congestion as evidenced by crackles, dyspnea and low SpO₂. | SpO₂ 94% or more within 12 hours. | Sit him upright or semi-Fowler. Give oxygen as prescribed. Listen to the lungs every 4 hours. Check ABG as ordered. | SpO₂ 95%, less breathless: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Edema all over Moving less | **RISK** Risk for impaired skin integrity as evidenced by risk factors: edema and immobility. | Skin over the swollen areas stays intact for the whole admission. | Look at swollen areas and bony prominences every shift. Change his position every 2 hours and raise the swollen limbs. No tight clothes, tape or restraints on swollen skin. Keep the skin clean, dry and moisturised. | Skin intact: goal met. |

## 2.12  Electrolyte Imbalances

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Complains of palpitations and weak muscles **Objective** Serum K⁺ 2.9 mmol/L (or above 5.5 mmol/L) Irregular pulse ECG changes | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to altered heart rhythm secondary to potassium imbalance as evidenced by irregular pulse and ECG changes. | Within 24 hours the rhythm is regular and serum K⁺ is 3.5–5.0 mmol/L. | Continuous ECG monitoring, as ordered. Give potassium replacement, or treatment to lower it, as prescribed. Potassium is never given as an IV push; always use an infusion pump. Recheck serum K⁺ as ordered. | Rhythm and K⁺ back to normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family says she has become confused **Objective** Serum Na⁺ 124 mmol/L Does not know the time or place | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to sodium imbalance as evidenced by disorientation and abnormal serum sodium. | Oriented to time, place and person as the sodium comes back to normal. | Check level of consciousness and orientation every 4 hours. Correct the sodium slowly, as prescribed. Restrict fluids if ordered. Reorient her and keep the surroundings calm. | Oriented: goal met. Seizures or worse confusion must be reported. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Muscle weakness Confusion On diuretics | **RISK** Risk for adult falls as evidenced by risk factors: muscle weakness, confusion and medications. | No falls. | Put fall precautions in place. Help her when she moves. Keep the call bell where she can reach it. | No falls: goal met. |

## 2.13  Acid–Base Imbalances

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot get enough air." **Objective** ABG: pH 7.28, PaCO₂ 58 mmHg Drowsy SpO₂ 88% | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to alveolar hypoventilation as evidenced by respiratory acidosis, hypoxemia and drowsiness. | ABG values moving back toward normal within 12 hours. | Follow ABG, SpO₂ and level of consciousness as ordered. Sit him upright so he can ventilate better. Give controlled oxygen to the prescribed target. No sedatives unless they are ordered. If pH or PaCO₂ gets worse, be ready for non-invasive ventilation. | pH and PaCO₂ improving, patient alert: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Feels sick and weak **Objective** Breathing deep and fast ABG: pH 7.25, HCO₃⁻ 14 mmol/L | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to compensation for metabolic acidosis as evidenced by deep, rapid respirations. | RR 12–20/min as the acidosis is corrected. | Watch the pattern and rate of breathing. Treat the cause as prescribed (insulin and fluids, for example). Bicarbonate only if it is ordered. Keep a close eye on potassium. | Breathing pattern normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Abnormal pH Hypoxemia | **RISK** Risk for acute confusion as evidenced by risk factors: acid–base imbalance and hypoxemia. | Stays oriented. | Check orientation every 4 hours. Keep safety measures in place. Report any change in mental state. | Oriented: goal met. |

## 2.14  Blood Transfusion

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I get tired after walking a few steps." **Objective** Hb 6.8 g/dL Pale HR 110/min on exertion | **ACTUAL** Activity intolerance related to reduced oxygen-carrying capacity as evidenced by fatigue on exertion and low Hb. | After the transfusion, manages self-care without becoming breathless. | Plan rest between activities. Give blood as prescribed. Check HR and SpO₂ while she is active. Recheck Hb after the transfusion, as ordered. | Does her ADL without tiring: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am worried about receiving blood." **Objective** Face looks tense Keeps asking if it is safe | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to unfamiliar procedure as evidenced by worry and repeated questions. | Before the transfusion starts, she says she understands and feels less anxious. | Explain what will happen, how long it takes and what checks are done. Tell her which symptoms she must report. Stay with her for the first 15 minutes. | Calm and cooperative: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Receiving a blood product Has had transfusions before | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: possible transfusion reaction or incompatibility. | Transfusion finished with no reaction. | At the bedside, check the patient's identity and the blood unit with a second nurse. Take baseline vital signs. Run it slowly at first and watch closely for the first 15 minutes. Vital signs as per policy. If there is a reaction: stop the transfusion, keep the line open with normal saline, call the physician and send the unit back. Finish it within 4 hours of the unit leaving storage. | No reaction: goal met. |

## 2.15  IV Therapy and Central Venous Lines

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The area around the cannula is sore." **Objective** Cannula site red and swollen Skin around the site cool and pale Drip running slower | **ACTUAL** Impaired tissue integrity related to infiltration or phlebitis as evidenced by swelling, redness and pain at site. | Within 48 hours the site has healed, with no redness or swelling. | Stop the infusion and take the cannula out. Raise the limb and apply a compress as per policy. If a new cannula is needed, put it in the other limb. Grade the infiltration or phlebitis and document it. | Site healing: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "What should I do if the line pulls?" **Objective** PICC line just inserted Cannot explain how to look after the line | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to care of vascular access device as evidenced by questions about line care. | Before discharge she can name the warning signs and explain how to protect the line. | Teach her to keep the dressing dry and intact, and to cover it in the shower. Teach the signs of infection (redness, discharge, fever) and of a blocked line. Tell her not to pull or bend the line, and not to let anyone else use it. Give written instructions and a number to call. | Explains line care correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Central venous catheter On IV therapy long term | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: invasive vascular device. | No CLABSI. | Hand hygiene and aseptic technique every time the line is accessed. Scrub the hub before each access. Change the dressing as per policy, or sooner if it is soiled. Look at the site every day and ask whether the line is still needed. Watch the temperature. | No signs of infection: goal met. |

## 2.16  Enteral Tube Feeding

Check where the tube is. Measure gastric residual volume if ordered. If he vomits or coughs, stop the feed. | Chest sounds clear: goal met. |

## 2.17  Total Parenteral Nutrition

Give insulin as prescribed. Report signs of hypo- or hyperglycemia. | Glucose within target: goal met. |

## 2.18  Nausea and Vomiting

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel sick all the time." **Objective** Has vomited 4 times in 6 hours Pale and sweaty | **ACTUAL** Nausea related to (cause, for example medication or gastric irritation) as evidenced by reported nausea and vomiting. | Within 2 hours she says the nausea is less. | Give the antiemetic as prescribed. Get rid of smells and let in fresh air. Once she can manage it, offer small amounts of clear fluid. Give mouth care after she vomits. | Less nausea, no more vomiting: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Thirsty **Objective** Mouth dry Passing less urine HR 108/min | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to vomiting as evidenced by dry mucosa and reduced urine output. | Urine output adequate within 8 hours. | Record I&O and urine output every 4 hours. Give IV fluids as prescribed. Watch vital signs and capillary refill. When the vomiting settles, offer small sips of clear fluid. | Urine output adequate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Vomiting over and over | **RISK** Risk for electrolyte imbalance as evidenced by risk factors: vomiting. | Electrolytes stay normal. | Follow serum electrolytes. Report any abnormal result. Replace as ordered. | Electrolytes normal: goal met. |

## 2.19  Altered Bowel Elimination: Constipation and Diarrhea

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have not opened my bowels for 4 days." **Objective** Stool hard and dry Abdomen distended | **ACTUAL** Constipation related to reduced activity and fluid intake as evidenced by no stool for 4 days and hard stool. | Passes soft stool within 48 hours. | Encourage fluids and fibre. Get her moving. Give the laxative as prescribed. Give her privacy and a regular time for the toilet. | Soft stool passed: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I have frequent watery stools." **Objective** Liquid stools 6 times a day Hyperactive bowel sounds | **ACTUAL** Diarrhea related to infection or medication as evidenced by frequent liquid stools. | Within 72 hours, fewer stools and the stool is formed. | Record how often stools come and what they look like. Send a stool sample if ordered. Keep him hydrated. Protect the skin around the anus. | Formed stools: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Diarrhea continuing | **RISK** Risk for deficient fluid volume as evidenced by risk factors: excessive fluid loss through stool. | Fluid balance maintained. | Keep track of I&O and weight. Encourage oral rehydration. Give IV fluids as ordered. | I&O balanced: goal met. |

## 2.20  Urinary Catheter Care

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot pass urine." **Objective** Bladder distended Bladder scan volume 600 mL | **ACTUAL** Impaired urinary elimination related to urinary retention as evidenced by inability to void and distended bladder. | Bladder drained, with urine output of at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour. | Insert the catheter with aseptic technique, as ordered. Record urine output. Keep the drainage bag lower than the bladder. Plan to take it out early. | Draining well: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "The catheter feels uncomfortable." **Objective** Restless Tugging at the catheter | **ACTUAL** Impaired comfort related to indwelling catheter as evidenced by reported discomfort. | Says the discomfort is less within 24 hours. | Fix the catheter to his thigh. Check there are no kinks and nothing is pulling on it. Give perineal care. | Says he is comfortable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has an indwelling urinary catheter | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: indwelling catheter. | No CAUTI. | Keep the drainage system closed. Perineal care every day. Encourage him to drink. Take the catheter out as soon as it is not needed. | No signs of UTI: goal met. |

## 2.21  Sleep Disturbance in Hospital

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The noise wakes me up." **Objective** Awake through the night Sleepy in the day | **ACTUAL** Disturbed sleep pattern related to environmental factors as evidenced by frequent waking. | Within 2 nights she sleeps at least 5 hours. | Keep noise and lights down at night. Group care activities together. Offer an eye mask or earplugs. | Sleeping better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel tired all day." **Objective** Lethargic Finds it hard to concentrate | **ACTUAL** Fatigue related to sleep deprivation as evidenced by reported tiredness and lethargy. | Within 3 days she feels more energetic and does some activity during the day. | Daytime naps no longer than 30–45 minutes. Encourage light activity and sitting out of bed in the day. Open the curtains so she gets daylight. Look for other reasons for the tiredness, such as anemia or pain. | Says she has more energy: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Elderly Not sleeping | **RISK** Risk for acute confusion as evidenced by risk factors: sleep deprivation and older age. | Stays oriented. | Check orientation. Put a clock and calendar where she can see them. Encourage the family to be with her. | Oriented: goal met. |

## 2.22  Anxiety in the Hospitalized Patient

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I keep worrying about my illness." **Objective** Restless HR 102/min Cannot concentrate | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to change in health status as evidenced by worry, restlessness and tachycardia. | Says he is less anxious within 24 hours. | Gauge how anxious he is. Give him clear, honest information. Teach relaxation breathing. Encourage him to talk about how he feels. | Looks calm: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am scared of the procedure tomorrow." **Objective** Tearful Wants the procedure cancelled | **ACTUAL** Fear related to unfamiliar procedure as evidenced by expressed fear and tearfulness. | Before the procedure she says she is less afraid. | Explain the procedure and what she will feel. Arrange for the physician to talk with her. Let the family be there to support her. | Agrees to go ahead and is calm: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Body exposed during care Depends on staff for most things | **RISK** Risk for compromised human dignity as evidenced by risk factors: loss of privacy and dependence. | Says she feels respected during care. | Keep her covered and private during care. Call her by the name she prefers. Always explain before you touch her. | Says she feels respected: goal met. |

## 2.23  Use of Physical Restraints

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Family says he does not know where he is **Objective** Disoriented Pulls at his lines and tubes | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to (cause, for example delirium) as evidenced by disorientation and pulling at devices. | Kept safe using the least restrictive measures. | Try other options first: reorientation, a family member at the bedside, close observation. Use a restraint only with a physician's order and as policy allows. Explain to the family what is happening and why. | Safe, and restraint off as soon as possible: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Asks to be able to move his arms **Objective** Wrist restraints on Can hardly move his arms | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to restraint use as evidenced by limited movement. | Circulation and joint movement kept normal. | Every 2 hours, release the restraint and put the joints through ROM. Every hour, check circulation, skin and sensation. Offer drinks and the toilet. | Circulation and skin normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Restraint on Confusion | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: physical restraint and agitation. | No injury. | Use the right size of restraint and tie it to the bed frame, never the side rails. Write down why it is on, every check made and how he responds. Keep asking whether it is still needed. | No injury: goal met. |

## 2.24  Discharge Planning

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I am not sure how to take my new medicines." **Objective** Cannot explain when to take each medicine New prescriptions on discharge | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to new treatment regimen as evidenced by inaccurate statements about medications. | Before discharge, can give the name, dose, timing and main side effects of every medicine. | Find out what she knows, how well she reads and which language she prefers. Teach one medicine at a time in simple language, with a written schedule. Use teach-back to check she has understood. Include the family member who helps her with medicines. | Explains her medicines correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I often forget my medicines at home." **Objective** Has been readmitted before Often misses doses | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to complex regimen as evidenced by missed doses and readmissions. | Can describe how she will manage her treatment at home. | Ask the physician whether the regimen can be made simpler. Suggest a pill organiser and reminders. Book a follow-up appointment. | Gives a plan that is realistic: goal met. |
| **Subjective** A relative says "I don't know how I will manage." **Objective** Needs help with all ADL Only one person to care for him | **RISK** Risk for caregiver role strain as evidenced by risk factors: patient dependence and lack of support. | Before discharge, the caregiver can name where support will come from. | Find out what the caregiver can manage and what they need. Teach the care skills needed. Refer to community or social services. | Caregiver names sources of support: goal met. |

## 2.25  Palliative Care

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I am afraid of dying in pain." **Objective** Advanced illness Withdrawn and tearful | **ACTUAL** Death anxiety related to awareness of terminal illness as evidenced by expressed fear of dying. | Talks about how he feels and says he is less afraid. | Let him talk about his fears. Reassure him that his symptoms will be controlled. Bring in family and spiritual support, as he wishes. | Says he feels calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel short of breath and uncomfortable." **Objective** Breathless Restless | **ACTUAL** Impaired comfort related to disease symptoms as evidenced by dyspnea and restlessness. | Says he is more comfortable within 24 hours. | Give the prescribed symptom-relief medication. Sit him upright and use a fan. Give mouth care and skin care. | Looks comfortable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Why is this happening to me?" **Objective** Terminally ill | **RISK** Risk for spiritual distress as evidenced by risk factors: terminal illness. | Expresses a sense of peace. | Respect his religious practices. If he asks, arrange a visit from a religious leader. Listen without judging. | Says he feels at peace: goal met. |

## 2.26  Death and the Grieving Family

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot believe he is gone." **Objective** Crying Shock | **ACTUAL** Grieving related to death of a family member as evidenced by crying and expressions of disbelief. | The family can grieve in a supportive place. | Give them a private room. Let them spend time with the person who has died. Call the deceased by name. Respect cultural and religious practices. | Family express their feelings openly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "We do not know what to do now." **Objective** Unsure about the procedures Keep asking what they should do | **ACTUAL** Interrupted family processes related to death of a family member as evidenced by confusion about next steps. | Before leaving, the family can say what happens next. | Explain how the body is released and how the death certificate is issued. Give written information and phone numbers. Call the social worker if needed. Respect religious requirements about the timing of burial. | Family understand the next steps: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Death was sudden Little family support | **RISK** Risk for maladaptive grieving as evidenced by risk factors: unexpected death and lack of support. | Before leaving, the family name at least two sources of support. | Find out what support the family has. Tell them about bereavement support. Encourage them to call the unit later with any questions. | Family name their sources of support: goal met. |

**PART 3**

# Respiratory Care Plans

## 3.1  Pneumonia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cough a lot but cannot bring up the phlegm." **Objective** Coughing up thick yellow sputum Crackles heard over the right lower lobe RR 26/min | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to retained thick secretions as evidenced by crackles, ineffective cough and tachypnea. | Within 48 hours he coughs up secretions effectively and breath sounds are better. | Listen to the lungs every 4 hours. Unless fluids are restricted, encourage 2–3 L/day by mouth to loosen the secretions. Teach controlled coughing and deep breathing. Give the nebulised bronchodilator or mucolytic as prescribed. Do chest physiotherapy if ordered. If he cannot clear the secretions himself, suction him. | Fewer crackles, cough working well: goal met. If not, reassess and let the physician know. |
| **Subjective** "I get short of breath when I talk." **Objective** SpO₂ 89% on room air Using his accessory muscles Restless | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to alveolar consolidation as evidenced by hypoxemia, dyspnea and restlessness. | SpO₂ kept at 94% or above within 24 hours. | Watch SpO₂, RR and level of consciousness. Sit him in high Fowler, or when he lies on his side, put the good lung down (unaffected side down). Give oxygen as prescribed and check the device is delivering it properly. Spread activities out with rest in between. Check ABG as ordered. | SpO₂ 95%, less breathless: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Complains of chills **Objective** Temperature 38.8 °C WBC 16 × 10⁹/L Age 72 years | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: infection and possible progression to sepsis. | BP, HR and urine output stay stable. | Vital signs every 4 hours, more often if he is unstable. Once cultures are taken, give the antibiotics on time. Keep a record of urine output. Report systolic BP below 90 mmHg, HR above 120/min, or any new confusion. | Vital signs stable: goal met. |

## 3.2  Bronchial Asthma

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My chest feels tight and I cannot breathe out." **Objective** Wheezing on breathing out Expiration is prolonged RR 28/min, SpO₂ 91% | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to bronchoconstriction as evidenced by wheeze, prolonged expiration and tachypnea. | Within 2 hours, RR is 12–20/min and SpO₂ is 94% or more. | Sit her upright; leaning forward may feel easier. Give the inhaled short-acting bronchodilator as prescribed and see how she responds. Give oxygen to keep SpO₂ on target. Give the corticosteroid as prescribed. Stay with her and keep calm yourself. | Less wheeze, SpO₂ on target: goal met. No better after treatment? Report it at once. |
| **Subjective** "I use the inhaler only when I feel bad." **Objective** Uses the inhaler the wrong way Many attacks this year | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to insufficient knowledge of treatment regimen as evidenced by incorrect inhaler use and frequent exacerbations. | Before discharge she uses the inhaler correctly and can explain how a reliever differs from a preventer. | Teach inhaler and spacer technique, then watch her do it. Explain what the reliever does and what the preventer does. Help her work out her own triggers (dust, smoke, cold air). Give her a written asthma action plan. Tell her to rinse her mouth after the steroid inhaler. | Technique correct and explanation accurate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I wait until the attack is bad before going to hospital." **Objective** Needs the reliever more than 3 times a week Has had severe attacks before | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: delayed treatment of exacerbations and overuse of reliever inhaler. | Can tell you when to follow the action plan and when to go to emergency. | Explain the warning signs: needing the reliever more often, waking at night, struggling to talk. Go through the steps of the asthma action plan with her. If the reliever does not help, she should go to emergency. | Names the warning signs and what to do: goal met. |

## 3.3  Status Asthmaticus

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Cannot finish a sentence **Objective** SpO₂ 86% Very little wheeze ("silent chest") HR 130/min, RR 34/min PaCO₂ rising on ABG | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to severe bronchospasm and air trapping as evidenced by hypoxemia, silent chest and rising PaCO₂. | Within 4 hours, SpO₂ 94% or more and ABG getting better. | Call for urgent medical help. Give high-flow oxygen and continuous nebulised bronchodilators as prescribed. Give IV corticosteroid and IV magnesium sulfate as ordered. Monitor SpO₂, HR, RR and level of consciousness continuously. Get the intubation equipment ready. | SpO₂ on target and able to talk: goal met. If she becomes drowsy or exhausted, prepare for ICU and ventilation. |
| **Subjective** "I am too tired to breathe." **Objective** Using accessory muscles Sweating heavily Exhausted | **ACTUAL** Fatigue related to increased work of breathing as evidenced by exhaustion and accessory muscle use. | Within 4 hours she is working less hard to breathe. | Keep her sitting up and well supported. Do all her care for her; cut out any activity that is not needed. Watch for signs that her breathing muscles are tiring. | Using accessory muscles less: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On high-dose beta-agonists On steroids | **RISK** Risk for electrolyte imbalance as evidenced by risk factors: high-dose bronchodilators (hypokalemia) and corticosteroids (hyperglycemia). | Potassium and glucose stay within the target range. | Check serum potassium and blood glucose. Watch the ECG for dysrhythmia. Replace as ordered. | Values in range: goal met. |

## 3.4  Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease (COPD)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot climb stairs without stopping." **Objective** Barrel chest Breathes out through pursed lips SpO₂ 88%, PaCO₂ 52 mmHg | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to alveolar destruction and air trapping as evidenced by hypoxemia, hypercapnia and dyspnea. | SpO₂ 88–92% (or the prescribed target) within 24 hours. | Give controlled oxygen to the prescribed target; avoid high concentrations. Watch ABG, SpO₂ and level of consciousness. Sit him upright or leaning forward. Teach pursed-lip and diaphragmatic breathing. Give bronchodilators and steroids as prescribed. | SpO₂ on target and alert: goal met. Report drowsiness (he may be retaining CO₂). |
| **Subjective** "I get tired when I wash or dress." **Objective** Breathless with very little effort HR goes up by 25/min with activity | **ACTUAL** Activity intolerance related to imbalance between oxygen supply and demand as evidenced by exertional dyspnea and fatigue. | Manages his ADL with rests, keeping SpO₂ on target. | Plan rest periods between activities. Teach ways to save energy (sit down to wash, keep things close by). Encourage pulmonary rehabilitation. Check SpO₂ while he is active. | Gets through ADL with breathlessness he can tolerate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Smokes, 40 pack-years **Objective** Secretions thick On steroids Admitted often | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: chronic lung disease, retained secretions and corticosteroid therapy. | No new chest infection. | Encourage influenza and pneumococcal vaccines. Teach the early signs of infection (sputum changing colour or amount, fever). Help him stop smoking. Encourage fluids and clearing secretions. | No signs of infection: goal met. |

## 3.5  Bronchiectasis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cough up large amounts of phlegm every morning." **Objective** Large amounts of purulent sputum Coarse crackles Clubbed fingers | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to excessive bronchial secretions as evidenced by copious sputum and coarse crackles. | Clears secretions effectively and can show you the airway clearance techniques. | Teach postural drainage and the active cycle of breathing, as ordered. Encourage fluids to thin the sputum. Give nebulised saline or a mucolytic as prescribed. Write down the amount, colour and smell of the sputum. | Less sputum, clearer breath sounds: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel weak and have lost weight." **Objective** Lost 4 kg in 2 months No appetite | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to increased energy expenditure and poor appetite as evidenced by weight loss. | Weight holds steady within 2 weeks. | Give mouth care before meals. Offer small, frequent meals high in protein. Refer to the dietitian. Weigh once a week. | Weight steady: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Secretions retained long term Keeps getting infections | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: stasis of secretions and damaged airways. | Reports signs of infection early and stays out of hospital. | Teach him to report fever, more sputum, or coughing blood. Send sputum for culture as ordered. Encourage vaccination. | Names the warning signs: goal met. |

## 3.6  Pulmonary Tuberculosis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Can I stop the tablets when I feel better?" "I have had a cough for 2 months." **Objective** Sputum AFB smear positive Just started anti-TB treatment Cannot name his medicines | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to complexity of long-term treatment as evidenced by intention to stop treatment early and inability to name medications. | Before discharge, can give the name, schedule and length of his anti-TB treatment. | Explain that treatment lasts at least 6 months and must not be stopped early. Support directly observed therapy (DOT). Teach the common side effects: orange urine with rifampicin, jaundice, changes in vision, numbness. Book follow-up sputum tests. | Explains his regimen correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I have no appetite." **Objective** Lost 5 kg BMI 17.5 kg/m² | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to anorexia and increased metabolic demand as evidenced by weight loss and low BMI. | Gains weight steadily during treatment. | Give a high-calorie, high-protein diet. Give pyridoxine with isoniazid as prescribed. Weigh once a week. | Weight going up: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Lives with a family of 7 **Objective** Smear-positive TB Home is crowded | **RISK** Risk for infection (to others) as evidenced by risk factors: airborne transmission and close household contacts. | No new cases among contacts; he follows airborne precautions. | Keep him in airborne isolation until he is no longer infectious. Teach him to cover his mouth when coughing and to get rid of sputum safely. Encourage opening windows to air the rooms at home. Send household contacts for screening. | Follows precautions and contacts have been screened: goal met. |

## 3.7  Lung Cancer

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I get breathless and cough blood sometimes." **Objective** SpO₂ 90% Coughing up blood Less air entry on one side | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to airway obstruction by tumour as evidenced by hypoxemia and dyspnea. | SpO₂ on the prescribed target and he says he is less breathless. | Position him so the lungs can expand as much as possible. Give oxygen as prescribed. Keep track of how much blood he coughs up and report any increase. Teach breathing control. | SpO₂ on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I worry about what will happen to my family." **Objective** Tearful Not sleeping | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to diagnosis of cancer and uncertain future as evidenced by worry and insomnia. | Talks about his feelings and says he is less anxious. | Give him time to talk. Give accurate information about the treatment plan. Bring in the family and psychological support. | Says he is less anxious: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Tumour growing into blood vessels May be on an anticoagulant | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: tumour erosion of blood vessels. | No massive hemoptysis; any bleeding is caught early. | Watch and record any hemoptysis. Follow the Hb. Have suction ready at the bedside. Report any increase in bleeding at once. | No major bleeding: goal met. |

## 3.8  Pleural Effusion

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot take a deep breath." **Objective** Affected side moves less with breathing Dull to percussion No breath sounds at the base SpO₂ 91% | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to reduced lung expansion from pleural fluid as evidenced by dyspnea and reduced breath sounds. | After drainage, RR 12–20/min and SpO₂ 94% or more. | Sit her upright. Give oxygen as prescribed. Get her ready for thoracentesis: explain it, then sit her up leaning forward over a table. Watch vital signs and SpO₂ during the procedure and afterwards. Record how much fluid comes off and what it looks like. | Breathing easier after drainage: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "The side of my chest hurts when I breathe." **Objective** Guarding Breathing shallow | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to pleural inflammation as evidenced by pain on breathing and shallow respiration. | Pain 3/10 or less within 1 hour of analgesia. | Give analgesia as prescribed and check again after 30–60 minutes. Have her hold a pillow against her chest when she coughs. If lying on the affected side eases the pain, position her that way. Once the analgesia works, teach slow deep breathing. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has had a thoracentesis | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: invasive procedure (pneumothorax, bleeding, re-expansion pulmonary edema). | No complications after the thoracentesis. | After the procedure, watch for sudden breathlessness, chest pain or a drop in SpO₂. Arrange a chest X-ray as ordered. Check the puncture site for bleeding. | No complications: goal met. |

## 3.9  Pneumothorax and Chest Drain

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I felt sudden chest pain and could not breathe." **Objective** No breath sounds on one side Chest moves unevenly SpO₂ 88%, RR 30/min | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to lung collapse as evidenced by dyspnea, absent breath sounds and hypoxemia. | Within 24 hours the lung has re-expanded and SpO₂ is 94% or more. | Give oxygen as prescribed. Assist with putting in the chest drain. Keep the drainage system below chest level and the tubing free of kinks. Look for swinging (tidaling) and bubbling in the water seal. Tracheal deviation, low BP or worsening breathlessness must be reported at once (tension pneumothorax). | Lung re-expanded on X-ray, SpO₂ on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "The tube hurts when I move." **Objective** Guards the drain site Does not want to move | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to chest drain and pleural irritation as evidenced by guarding and reported pain. | Pain 3/10 or less and doing his breathing exercises. | Give analgesia regularly as prescribed. Support the tube so it does not pull. Encourage deep breathing and exercises for the arm on the affected side. | Does the exercises with pain he can tolerate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Chest drain in situ | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: accidental disconnection or dislodgement of chest drain. | Drain stays secure and working. | Make sure every connection is secure. Never clamp the drain unless it is ordered. Keep sterile gauze and clamps at the bedside. If the tube falls out, cover the site with an occlusive dressing and call the physician immediately. | Drain not dislodged: goal met. |

## 3.10  Hemothorax

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel dizzy and short of breath." **Objective** Blood coming out of the chest tube BP 92/58 mmHg, HR 118/min Skin pale and cold | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to blood loss into the pleural space as evidenced by hypotension, tachycardia and bloody chest drainage. | Within 12 hours, vital signs stable and drainage falling. | Vital signs every 15–30 minutes until stable. Record chest drain output every hour. Report drainage above the surgeon's limit (often more than 200 mL/hour). Give IV fluids and blood as prescribed. Follow the Hb. | Vital signs stable, drainage less: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot breathe deeply." **Objective** Breath sounds reduced on the affected side SpO₂ 90% | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to lung compression by blood as evidenced by hypoxemia and reduced breath sounds. | SpO₂ kept at 94% or more. | Give oxygen as prescribed. Sit him semi-Fowler. Keep the chest drain patent. | SpO₂ on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Still bleeding | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: hypovolemia from continuing blood loss. | Perfusion adequate: MAP above 65 mmHg, urine output at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour. | Put in two large-bore IV cannulas as ordered. Record urine output every hour. If bleeding goes on, get him ready for thoracotomy. | Perfusion adequate: goal met. |

## 3.11  Pulmonary Embolism

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I suddenly could not breathe and my chest hurts." **Objective** SpO₂ 87% HR 124/min, RR 32/min Anxious and sweating | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to ventilation–perfusion mismatch as evidenced by sudden dyspnea, hypoxemia and tachycardia. | SpO₂ 94% or more within 6 hours. | Give oxygen as prescribed. Sit him in high Fowler. Continuous monitoring of SpO₂, ECG and vital signs. Give the anticoagulant or thrombolytic as prescribed. Get him ready for CT pulmonary angiography. | SpO₂ on target, less breathless: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel I am going to die." **Objective** Restless Shaking | **ACTUAL** Fear related to sudden breathlessness and chest pain as evidenced by expressed fear and restlessness. | Says he is less frightened within 2 hours. | Stay with him. Explain each procedure briefly and calmly. Give analgesia as prescribed. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On heparin or another anticoagulant | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: anticoagulant or thrombolytic therapy. | No bleeding, and clotting results in the therapeutic range. | Check aPTT or INR as ordered. Look for bleeding from the gums, urine, stool and puncture sites. No IM injections. Keep the antidote available as policy requires. | No bleeding: goal met. |

## 3.12  Acute Respiratory Distress Syndrome (ARDS)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Cannot speak in full sentences **Objective** SpO₂ 85% even on high-flow oxygen Bilateral infiltrates on chest X-ray Low PaO₂/FiO₂ ratio | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to alveolar-capillary membrane damage as evidenced by refractory hypoxemia and bilateral infiltrates. | Reaches the prescribed SpO₂ or PaO₂ target on ventilatory support. | Watch ABG, SpO₂ and the ventilator settings. Assist with intubation and mechanical ventilation. Use prone positioning according to protocol. Keep PEEP as prescribed and avoid disconnecting the circuit unless necessary. Give sedation as ordered. | Oxygenation on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family say he was very anxious **Objective** Fluid balance 3 L positive Crackles all through both lungs | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to increased capillary permeability as evidenced by positive balance and crackles. | Fluid balance neutral or negative, as ordered. | Strict I&O and a daily weight. Give diuretics as prescribed. Follow a conservative fluid strategy as ordered. | Fluid balance on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On a ventilator Immobile Lying prone | **RISK** Risk for adult pressure injury as evidenced by risk factors: immobility, sedation and prone positioning. | Skin and pressure areas stay intact while ventilated and proned. | When prone, pad the face, chest, knees and any other pressure areas. Move the head and arms as the prone protocol says. Every shift, check the skin and the areas under devices (tube ties, masks). Nurse him on a pressure-redistributing mattress. | Skin intact: goal met. |

## 3.13  Acute Respiratory Failure

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Confused, does not answer questions **Objective** PaO₂ 50 mmHg, PaCO₂ 65 mmHg, pH 7.24 RR 8/min and shallow | **ACTUAL** Impaired spontaneous ventilation related to respiratory muscle fatigue as evidenced by hypercapnia, acidosis and bradypnea. | Within 2 hours ventilation is adequate and ABG is improving. | Call for urgent medical help. Open the airway and support breathing with a bag-valve-mask if needed. Get ready for non-invasive ventilation or intubation. Watch ABG, SpO₂, HR and level of consciousness. | pH and PaCO₂ getting better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family say "He is not making sense." **Objective** GCS 10 Restless and disoriented | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to hypoxemia and hypercapnia as evidenced by disorientation and reduced GCS. | Becomes oriented as gas exchange improves. | GCS and orientation every hour. Bed low, padded side rails as policy allows. Reorient him often, using simple words. Correct the hypoxemia and hypercapnia as prescribed. | Oriented: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Not fully conscious Cough is weak | **RISK** Risk for aspiration as evidenced by risk factors: decreased level of consciousness and impaired cough. | Airway stays clear. | Head of the bed up at 30–45 degrees. Have suction ready. Nothing by mouth until it is safe for him to swallow. | No aspiration: goal met. |

## 3.14  Mechanical Ventilation

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** ET tube in situ Secretions in the tube High-pressure alarm going off Coarse crackles | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to presence of artificial airway and inability to cough as evidenced by secretions and high airway pressure. | Airway stays patent and breath sounds are clear. | Listen to the lungs every 2–4 hours. Suction when needed with sterile technique; pre-oxygenate first. Every shift, check the tube position (marking at the lips) and the cuff pressure. Answer ventilator alarms straight away. Humidify the gas he breathes in. | Breath sounds clear, pressures normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Nods but cannot talk **Objective** Intubated Gestures in frustration | **ACTUAL** Impaired verbal communication related to endotracheal tube as evidenced by inability to speak. | Gets his needs across by other means. | Ask questions he can answer yes or no. Give him a writing board or a picture chart. Explain every procedure to him. | Communicates his needs: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Intubated for more than 48 hours Lying flat Sedated | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: invasive airway and impaired cough (ventilator-associated pneumonia). | No ventilator-associated pneumonia. | Head of the bed at 30–45 degrees. Oral care with antiseptic, following protocol. Every day, check whether he is ready for a sedation break and weaning. Drain condensate away from the patient. Use closed suction systems as policy says. | No new infiltrates, no fever: goal met. |

## 3.15  Tracheostomy Care

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Points at his throat to show it is uncomfortable **Objective** Thick secretions in the trach tube Noisy breathing | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to increased secretions and artificial airway as evidenced by noisy breathing and visible secretions. | Tracheostomy stays patent and the airway clear. | Suction when needed, using sterile technique. Clean or change the inner cannula as policy says. Humidify the air. Keep a spare tube, the obturator and a tracheal dilator at the bedside. | Airway clear: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I do not want people to see my neck." **Objective** Will not look at the stoma Hides his neck with his hand when visitors come | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to tracheostomy as evidenced by avoidance of looking at stoma. | Looks at the stoma and starts to help care for it. | Let him talk about how he feels. Bring him into his care little by little. Suggest a scarf or stoma cover for when he goes out. | Helps with stoma care: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Surgical stoma Secretions around the site | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: open stoma and secretions. | Stoma stays clean, with no signs of infection. | Clean the stoma and change the dressing as policy says. Change the ties when soiled, leaving room for one finger. Look for redness or pus. | Stoma clean: goal met. |

## 3.16  Thoracic Surgery (Lobectomy and Pneumonectomy)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "It hurts to breathe." **Objective** Breathing shallow Less air entry at the base SpO₂ 92% | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to incisional pain and reduced lung volume as evidenced by shallow breathing and low SpO₂. | SpO₂ 94% or more and using the incentive spirometer. | Give analgesia regularly (epidural or PCA as ordered). Every hour, encourage deep breathing and incentive spirometry. Get him moving early. Position as ordered (after pneumonectomy, do not turn him fully onto the non-operated side). | SpO₂ on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Puts the pain at 8/10 **Objective** Guarding Moves the arm on the operated side less | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to thoracotomy incision as evidenced by pain score and guarding. | Pain 3/10 or less and doing his arm exercises. | Check pain regularly. Teach shoulder ROM exercises. Have him hold a pillow over the incision when he coughs. | Pain controlled, arm moving fully: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Major thoracic surgery Chest drains | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: major surgery and chest drains. | Vital signs stable and drain output within the expected limits. | In the first hours, record chest drain output every hour. Follow vital signs and Hb. Report any drainage that is too much. | No excessive bleeding: goal met. |

## 3.17  Obstructive Sleep Apnea

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
