| **Subjective** His wife says he snores loudly and stops breathing at times "I feel sleepy all day." **Objective** BMI 36 kg/m² Thick neck SpO₂ falls while he sleeps | **ACTUAL** Disturbed sleep pattern related to upper airway obstruction during sleep as evidenced by snoring, apneas and daytime sleepiness. | Within 2 weeks he uses CPAP and says he sleeps better. | Show him how to use the CPAP mask and look after it. Encourage him to lose weight. Tell him to avoid alcohol and sedatives before bed. Suggest he sleeps on his side. | Uses CPAP every night: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I fell asleep while driving." **Objective** Sleepy in the day Dozes off during the interview | **ACTUAL** Fatigue related to poor sleep quality as evidenced by daytime sleepiness. | Within 2 weeks of using CPAP, says he feels more alert in the day. | Until he is treated, he should not drive or use machinery when sleepy. Encourage a regular sleep routine. Tell him to avoid sedatives and alcohol in the evening. Ask about his alertness at follow-up. | Says he is more alert: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Hypoxemic again and again at night Obesity | **RISK** Risk for decreased cardiac tissue perfusion as evidenced by risk factors: nocturnal hypoxemia and hypertension. | BP stays on target and he uses CPAP every night. | Check BP at every visit. Encourage nightly CPAP and taking his antihypertensives. Encourage weight loss and regular exercise. Teach him to report chest pain or palpitations. | BP controlled: goal met. |

**PART 4**

# Cardiovascular Care Plans

## 4.1  Hypertension

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I stop my tablets when I feel well." Gets a headache now and then **Objective** BP 168/102 mmHg on two readings BMI 31 kg/m² Eats a lot of salt | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to insufficient knowledge of disease and treatment as evidenced by stopping medication and uncontrolled BP. | Can explain why he needs treatment and takes his tablets every day; BP close to target within 4 weeks. | Explain that high blood pressure usually causes no symptoms and that treatment is for the long term. Teach him when to take his medicine and that he must not stop it without advice. Teach cutting down on salt, losing weight and exercising regularly. Show him how to check and record his BP at home. Book follow-up. | Explains his treatment, BP coming down: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I do not know what foods have salt." **Objective** Diet history: pickles, processed foods, salted bread Adds salt at the table | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to dietary sodium as evidenced by high-salt diet and questions. | Before discharge, can pick out salty foods and plan a low-salt diet. | Explain where hidden salt comes from. Give a list of low-salt swaps. Refer to the dietitian. | Plans a low-salt menu: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** BP not controlled Smokes Diabetic | **RISK** Risk for ineffective cerebral tissue perfusion as evidenced by risk factors: uncontrolled hypertension and smoking. | No neurological deficit; BP kept under control. | Teach the warning signs of stroke and that they need urgent care. Help him stop smoking. Check BP at every visit. | Names the warning signs: goal met. |

## 4.2  Hypertensive Crisis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have a severe headache and blurred vision." **Objective** BP 210/130 mmHg Nausea Confused | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to severely elevated blood pressure as evidenced by severe headache, blurred vision and nausea. | BP brought down gradually as prescribed (usually no more than 25% in the first hour), with no neurological worsening. | Put her in a quiet room with the head of the bed raised. While IV therapy runs, check BP every 5–15 minutes. Give the IV antihypertensive through an infusion pump as prescribed. Check neurological status every hour. Chest pain, more confusion or seizures must be reported. | BP down as prescribed, neurological status stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am frightened." **Objective** Restless HR 112/min | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to sudden severe symptoms as evidenced by fear and restlessness. | Within 1 hour she says she feels calmer and rests quietly. | Explain the procedures and monitoring in simple words. Stay with her through the acute phase. Keep visitors few, noise low and lights dim. Teach slow breathing. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Very high BP Kidneys may be affected | **RISK** Risk for decreased cardiac output as evidenced by risk factors: increased afterload. | Urine output stays above 0.5 mL/kg/hour, and no chest pain. | Follow urine output and renal function. Watch the ECG. Report chest pain or breathlessness. | Output adequate, no chest pain: goal met. |

## 4.3  Angina Pectoris

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I get chest pressure when I walk fast; it goes when I rest." **Objective** Pain lasts 5 minutes Eased by nitroglycerin ST depression on ECG during pain | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to myocardial ischemia as evidenced by exertional chest pressure relieved by rest. | Chest pain relieved within 5–15 minutes of treatment. | Stop what he is doing and have him sit or lie down. Give sublingual nitroglycerin as prescribed, checking BP before each dose. If SpO₂ is below target, give oxygen. Take a 12-lead ECG while the pain is present. Pain still there after 3 doses? Report it (this may be an MI). | Pain relieved: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I do not know how much activity is safe." **Objective** Newly diagnosed Cannot explain how to use nitroglycerin | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to new diagnosis as evidenced by questions about activity and medication. | Can explain how to use nitroglycerin and when to get help. | Teach him to carry nitroglycerin, keep it in a dark container and replace it as directed. Teach him to call emergency services if the pain is still there after the first dose, following local instructions. Teach him to build up exercise slowly and not to eat a heavy meal before exertion. | Explains correct use: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Coronary artery disease Smokes Cholesterol high | **RISK** Risk for decreased cardiac tissue perfusion as evidenced by risk factors: coronary artery disease, smoking and hyperlipidemia. | Commits to cutting down his risk factors. | Help him stop smoking. Teach a low-fat diet. Encourage him to keep taking his statin and antiplatelet. | Describes his plan to reduce risk: goal met. |

## 4.4  Myocardial Infarction

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The chest pain is crushing and goes to my left arm." Gives the pain 9/10 **Objective** Sweaty and pale ST elevation on ECG Troponin raised | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to myocardial ischemia and necrosis as evidenced by crushing chest pain, diaphoresis and ECG changes. | Pain relieved within 30 minutes of treatment. | Assess the pain and take an ECG straight away. Give aspirin, nitroglycerin and IV morphine as prescribed. If SpO₂ is below target, give oxygen. Get ready for reperfusion (PCI or thrombolysis) as ordered. Keep him on bed rest somewhere calm. | Pain relieved: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel weak and dizzy." **Objective** BP 94/60 mmHg, HR 112/min Skin cool Passing less urine | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to impaired contractility as evidenced by hypotension, tachycardia and cool skin. | Systolic BP stays above 90 mmHg and urine output is at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour. | Continuous ECG, and vital signs every 15 minutes at first. Record urine output every hour. Listen to the heart and lungs for any new murmur or crackles. Give medications as prescribed. | Hemodynamics stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Acute myocardial injury | **RISK** Risk for decreased cardiac output as evidenced by risk factors: dysrhythmias after myocardial infarction. | Any dysrhythmia is picked up and treated quickly. | Have the defibrillator and emergency drugs ready. Follow electrolytes, potassium and magnesium in particular. Report any new dysrhythmia at once. | No untreated dysrhythmia: goal met. |

## 4.5  Heart Failure

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I wake up at night unable to breathe." **Objective** Crackles at both bases Edema in both legs Put on 3 kg in 1 week | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to reduced cardiac pumping as evidenced by edema, crackles and weight gain. | Within 3 days the extra weight is coming off and the edema is less. | Weigh every day before breakfast. Keep a strict I&O chart. Limit fluid and sodium as prescribed. Give diuretics as ordered and watch potassium and creatinine. Sit him upright. | Weight falling, less edema: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I get tired walking to the bathroom." **Objective** Breathless with the smallest effort HR goes up by 30/min with activity | **ACTUAL** Activity intolerance related to reduced cardiac output as evidenced by fatigue and dyspnea on exertion. | Within 1 week, manages his own care with breathlessness he can accept. | Plan rest between activities. Build up activity slowly. Check HR, BP and SpO₂ while he is active. | Copes with self-care: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I do not weigh myself at home." **Objective** Readmitted often | **RISK** Risk for ineffective health self-management as evidenced by risk factors: complex regimen and lack of self-monitoring. | Can say when he should call the doctor (for example a 2 kg weight gain in 3 days). | Teach him to weigh himself every day and write it down. Teach a low-salt diet and his fluid limit. Teach the signs that his heart failure is getting worse. | Describes his self-monitoring plan: goal met. |

## 4.6  Acute Pulmonary Edema

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I am drowning." **Objective** Pink frothy sputum Crackles all through the lungs SpO₂ 84%, RR 36/min | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to fluid in alveoli as evidenced by hypoxemia, frothy sputum and crackles. | SpO₂ 94% or more within 1 hour. | Sit him bolt upright with his legs hanging down. Give high-flow oxygen and be ready for CPAP as ordered. Give the IV diuretic and nitrates as prescribed. Continuous monitoring of SpO₂, BP and ECG. If ordered, put in a urinary catheter to measure output. | SpO₂ on target, less breathless: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Very frightened **Objective** Agitated Sweaty | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to severe breathlessness as evidenced by agitation and fear. | Looks calmer as his breathing settles. | Stay with him. Speak calmly and tell him what you are doing. Give the prescribed medication. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On high-dose IV diuretic | **RISK** Risk for electrolyte imbalance as evidenced by risk factors: rapid diuresis. | Potassium and sodium stay normal during the diuresis. | Check potassium and sodium as ordered. Watch the ECG for dysrhythmia. Replace potassium as ordered. While the IV diuretic is running, record urine output every hour. | Electrolytes normal: goal met. |

## 4.7  Cardiomyopathy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel tired all the time and get breathless." **Objective** Echo shows a reduced ejection fraction S3 heard Edema | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to impaired contractility as evidenced by reduced ejection fraction, fatigue and edema. | Vital signs stable and symptoms easing. | Keep track of vital signs, weight and edema. Give the heart failure medicines as prescribed. Limit sodium as ordered. Watch the ECG for dysrhythmias. | Symptoms less: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot work as before." **Objective** Breathless on exertion HR goes up by 25/min with activity | **ACTUAL** Activity intolerance related to reduced cardiac reserve as evidenced by exertional dyspnea. | Within 1 week, plans his day within his limits and is less breathless on effort. | Teach energy saving: sit down for tasks, rest between activities. He should avoid heavy lifting and hard exercise, as the cardiologist advises. Check HR, BP and SpO₂ while he is active. Refer to cardiac rehabilitation. | Sticks to his activity plan: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Complains of palpitations **Objective** Heart dilated | **RISK** Risk for decreased cardiac output as evidenced by risk factors: dysrhythmias and thromboembolism related to dilated chambers. | No serious dysrhythmia, no embolic events. | Monitor the rhythm. Give the anticoagulant as prescribed. Teach the signs of stroke. | No events: goal met. |

## 4.8  Cardiac Dysrhythmias

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My heart is racing and I feel dizzy." **Objective** Pulse irregular at 140/min (atrial fibrillation) BP 100/64 mmHg | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to altered heart rate and rhythm as evidenced by irregular tachycardia, dizziness and low BP. | Within 24 hours, ventricular rate controlled and BP stable. | Continuous ECG monitoring. Take the apical and radial pulse at the same time. Give rate- or rhythm-control drugs as prescribed. If ordered, prepare for cardioversion. Follow electrolytes. | HR controlled, BP stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "What is wrong with my heart?" **Objective** Looks anxious Keeps glancing at the monitor | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to irregular heartbeat as evidenced by worry and questions. | Says she is less anxious within 24 hours. | Explain her rhythm and its treatment in simple words. Explain why she is monitored and what the alarms mean. Invite her questions. Teach slow breathing. | Calm: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Atrial fibrillation | **RISK** Risk for ineffective cerebral tissue perfusion as evidenced by risk factors: atrial fibrillation and risk of embolism. | No stroke. | Give the anticoagulant as prescribed. Check neurological status. Teach the warning signs of stroke. | No neurological change: goal met. |

## 4.9  Permanent Pacemaker

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I am afraid to move my arm." **Objective** New pacemaker in the left chest Keeps the arm completely still | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to care after pacemaker insertion as evidenced by questions and fear of moving. | Before discharge, can state his activity limits and pacemaker precautions. | For the period advised, he should not lift the arm above shoulder level. Teach him to check his pulse and always carry his pacemaker card. Tell him to keep mobile phones away from the device and to tell staff before an MRI or a security check. Book device follow-up. | States the precautions: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives pain at the insertion site 5/10 **Objective** Site slightly swollen Guards his left shoulder | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgical insertion as evidenced by reported pain. | Pain 3/10 or less. | Give analgesia as prescribed. If ordered, put a cold pack on the site. Support the arm in a comfortable position. Check the site for a hematoma. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Implanted device and incision | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: surgical incision and implanted device. | Site heals with no infection. | Keep the dressing dry. Watch for redness, swelling, discharge and fever. Give the prophylactic antibiotic as ordered. | Site clean: goal met. |

## 4.10  Infective Endocarditis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have fever and sweats for 2 weeks." **Objective** Temperature 38.6 °C New murmur heard Blood cultures positive | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to infection of heart valves as evidenced by fever and positive blood cultures. | Afebrile within 72 hours of starting antibiotics. | Take blood cultures before the antibiotics, as ordered. Give IV antibiotics on time and for the full course. Temperature every 4 hours. Look after the long-term IV access. | Afebrile: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I get tired quickly." **Objective** Tired Anemic | **ACTUAL** Activity intolerance related to infection and anemia as evidenced by fatigue. | Within 1 week, manages ADL with rests and stable vital signs. | Plan rest periods between activities. As the fever settles, increase activity bit by bit. Check HR and BP while he is active. Follow the Hb. | Copes with ADL: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Vegetations on the valve Echo shows a large vegetation | **RISK** Risk for ineffective cerebral tissue perfusion as evidenced by risk factors: embolization of vegetations. | No stroke or other embolic event. | Check neurological status and the limbs, and test the urine for blood. Report sudden neurological signs, pain in a limb, or chest pain. | No emboli: goal met. |

## 4.11  Pericarditis and Cardiac Tamponade

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The chest pain is sharp and worse when I lie down." **Objective** Friction rub heard Leaning forward eases the pain | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to pericardial inflammation as evidenced by sharp positional chest pain and friction rub. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Sit him up, leaning forward. Give the anti-inflammatory as prescribed. Check pain regularly. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel breathless and faint." **Objective** Low BP, distended neck veins, muffled heart sounds Pulsus paradoxus | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to compression of the heart by pericardial fluid as evidenced by hypotension, distended neck veins and muffled heart sounds. | BP back up after pericardiocentesis. | Report signs of tamponade at once. Get ready for urgent pericardiocentesis. Continuous monitoring of vital signs and ECG. | BP restored: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Echo shows a pericardial effusion HR climbing | **RISK** Risk for decreased cardiac output as evidenced by risk factors: increasing pericardial effusion. | Any sign of tamponade is picked up early and reported straight away. | Check BP, HR and neck veins every 1–2 hours. Check for pulsus paradoxus the way you were taught. Keep the pericardiocentesis set ready. A falling BP, rising HR or muffled heart sounds must be reported at once. | No tamponade: goal met. |

## 4.12  Valvular Heart Disease and Valve Replacement

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I get breathless on exertion." **Objective** Murmur Cannot exercise as much as before Crackles | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to valve dysfunction as evidenced by dyspnea and murmur. | Hemodynamics stable after the valve surgery. | Follow vital signs, ECG and urine output. Watch for signs of heart failure. Give the prescribed medications. | Hemodynamics stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "How long will I take warfarin?" **Objective** Has a mechanical valve Just starting warfarin | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to lifelong anticoagulation as evidenced by questions. | Can tell you the INR target, what to watch in the diet, and the signs of bleeding. | Explain that warfarin is lifelong with a mechanical valve. Teach the need for regular INR checks. Tell her not to suddenly change how many green leafy vegetables she eats, and to check before starting any new medicine. Tell her to let the dentist know before any procedure. | Explains her anticoagulation: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has a prosthetic valve Teeth and gums poorly cared for | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: prosthetic valve (endocarditis). | Can explain that she needs antibiotic prophylaxis as advised and good oral hygiene. | Teach daily care of teeth and gums and regular dental check-ups. Before any procedure, she should tell the dentist or doctor about the valve. Tell her to report a fever quickly. | States the precautions: goal met. |

## 4.13  Coronary Artery Bypass Graft (CABG)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Gives sternal pain 7/10 **Objective** Guarding Breathing shallow | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to sternotomy as evidenced by pain score and shallow breathing. | Pain 3/10 or less and coughing effectively. | Give analgesia regularly. Teach him to hug a pillow against his chest when he coughs. Encourage deep breathing and the incentive spirometer. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Chest drains draining BP low after surgery Urine output low | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to post-operative myocardial depression as evidenced by low BP and urine output. | MAP on target and urine output at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour. | Follow hemodynamics, ECG and chest drain output. Give fluids and inotropes as prescribed. Watch potassium. | Hemodynamics stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Wounds on the sternum and leg Diabetic | **RISK** Risk for surgical site infection as evidenced by risk factors: sternotomy, leg graft site and diabetes. | Wounds heal without infection. | Keep blood glucose under control as ordered. Look at the wounds every day. Teach sternal precautions (no heavy lifting, no pushing up with the arms). | Wounds healing: goal met. |

## 4.14  Cardiac Catheterization and PCI

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "What will happen during the procedure?" **Objective** Never had one before Restless, full of questions | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to invasive procedure as evidenced by questions and worry. | Says he understands the procedure and feels less anxious. | Explain the procedure, what he will feel, and the aftercare. Ask about allergy to contrast and iodine. Before the procedure, check renal function and anticoagulant status. Leave time for his questions. | Calm and informed: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My leg feels heavy and I want to bend it." **Objective** Femoral puncture On ordered bed rest | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to bed rest after femoral access as evidenced by prescribed immobility. | Keeps the leg straight for the ordered time, with no complications. | Keep the leg on the puncture side straight, as ordered. Check the site, distal pulses, colour and warmth regularly. | Pulses normal, site dry: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Arterial puncture On antiplatelet and anticoagulant drugs | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: arterial puncture and antithrombotic drugs. | No hematoma or bleeding at the puncture site. | Check the site each time you do vital signs, per protocol. If it bleeds, press on it and report at once. Look out for back pain or low BP (retroperitoneal bleed). | No bleeding: goal met. |

## 4.15  Rheumatic Heart Disease

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I had sore throats as a child." Says she gets breathless **Objective** Mitral murmur Breathless on exertion | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to valve damage as evidenced by murmur and exertional dyspnea. | Within 1 week, vital signs stable and less breathless. | Watch vital signs and look for signs of heart failure. Give the prescribed medications. Plan activity within what she can tolerate. Watch for an irregular pulse (atrial fibrillation). | Symptoms controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Why do I need monthly injections?" **Objective** On penicillin prophylaxis Has missed two doses | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to long-term antibiotic prophylaxis as evidenced by questions. | Can explain why she needs prophylaxis and when it is due. | Explain that it stops rheumatic fever from coming back. Give her a card with the schedule. | Keeps to the schedule: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Damaged valves Teeth and gums poorly cared for | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: damaged heart valves (endocarditis). | Keeps her teeth and gums healthy and reports fever. | Teach oral hygiene and regular dental review. Tell her to report a fever. Tell her to let the dentist know she has rheumatic heart disease. | States the precautions: goal met. |

## 4.16  Peripheral Arterial Disease

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My calf hurts when I walk and stops when I rest." **Objective** Weak pedal pulses Skin on the legs cool, shiny and hairless Capillary refill slow | **ACTUAL** Ineffective peripheral tissue perfusion related to arterial narrowing as evidenced by claudication, weak pulses and skin changes. | Within 4 weeks, walks further before the pain starts. | Check pulses, colour and temperature of the legs. Encourage a graded walking programme. Keep the legs down or level; do not raise them. Help him stop smoking. Give the antiplatelet and statin as prescribed. | Walking further: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Leg pain 6/10 when walking **Objective** Claudication comes on after 100 metres Weak pedal pulses | **ACTUAL** Chronic pain related to ischemia as evidenced by claudication. | Says the pain during activity is manageable. | Teach him to stop when the pain starts, then carry on walking. Give analgesia as prescribed. Encourage a supervised walking programme. Help him stop smoking. | Pain manageable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Circulation poor Diabetic | **RISK** Risk for impaired skin integrity as evidenced by risk factors: impaired circulation. | Skin on the feet stays intact. | Teach him to inspect his feet every day. Advise proper footwear. No heating pads on the legs. | Skin intact: goal met. |

## 4.17  Deep Vein Thrombosis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My left calf is swollen and painful." **Objective** Only one calf swollen Warm and red Doppler positive | **ACTUAL** Ineffective peripheral tissue perfusion related to venous obstruction as evidenced by swelling, warmth and pain in the affected limb. | Within 3–5 days, swelling and pain are less. | Give the anticoagulant as prescribed. Raise the affected leg. Measure the calf every day. Never massage the leg. Encourage walking as allowed. | Swelling down: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives the pain 6/10 **Objective** Calf tender Guards the leg | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to venous inflammation as evidenced by tender calf and reported pain. | Pain 3/10 or less. | Give analgesia as prescribed; no NSAIDs with anticoagulants unless they are ordered. Raise the leg. Put on a compression stocking when ordered. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Clot in a vein | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: anticoagulant therapy. | No bleeding and no sign of pulmonary embolism. | Check clotting results as ordered. Look for bleeding from the gums, in the urine and in the stool. Teach her to report sudden breathlessness or chest pain (could be an embolism). Watch SpO₂ and RR. | No bleeding, no sign of embolism: goal met. |

## 4.18  Varicose Veins

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My legs ache and feel heavy at the end of the day." **Objective** Veins swollen and twisted Ankles swollen | **ACTUAL** Chronic pain related to venous congestion as evidenced by aching and heaviness. | Within 2 weeks she says the discomfort is less. | Teach her to put her legs up when resting. Show her how to use compression stockings. Encourage walking and avoiding long periods of standing. | Less discomfort: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I do not like the look of my legs." **Objective** Keeps her legs covered Will not wear short clothing | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to visible veins as evidenced by covering legs. | Talks about how she feels and about her treatment options. | Listen to her worries. Explain the treatment options. Give advice on support stockings and cosmetic options. | Discusses the options: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Venous stasis Skin over the veins dry and itchy | **RISK** Risk for impaired skin integrity as evidenced by risk factors: venous stasis. | Skin stays intact. | Teach skin care and moisturising. Tell her to avoid knocks and scratching. Teach leg raising and walking. | Skin intact: goal met. |

## 4.19  Abdominal Aortic Aneurysm

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel a pulsing in my abdomen." **Objective** Pulsating mass in the abdomen Aneurysm seen on ultrasound | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to diagnosis and planned surgery as evidenced by questions. | Can name the warning signs of rupture and describe his plan of care. | Explain the condition and the surgical plan. Tell him not to lift heavy things or strain. Teach him to go to emergency straight away for sudden back or abdominal pain. | Names the warning signs: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am afraid of the operation." **Objective** Anxious Slept badly before surgery | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to major surgery as evidenced by expressed fear. | Says he feels less anxious. | Tell him about the operation and what recovery will be like. Encourage questions. Bring the family into the pre-op teaching. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Aneurysm Hypertension | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: aneurysm rupture. | BP stays on target, and rupture is recognised early. | Control BP as prescribed. Watch for sudden pain, low BP and a fast heart rate. Report straight away. | Condition stable: goal met. |

**PART 5**

# Neurological Care Plans

## 5.1  Stroke (Ischemic and Hemorrhagic)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Family say his right side went weak and his speech slurred 2 hours ago **Objective** Weak on the right, muscle strength 2/5 Face droops on one side Cannot turn in bed by himself | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to neuromuscular impairment as evidenced by hemiparesis and inability to turn independently. | Within 1 week, joint ROM kept without contractures and he has started moving with help. | Check neurological status and GCS as ordered (often every hour in the acute phase). Keep the affected limbs in neutral alignment and support the weak shoulder. Turn him every 2 hours. Passive ROM 3 times a day. Start rehabilitation early with the physiotherapist. | No contractures, movement getting better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Tries to talk but the words do not come out clearly **Objective** Expressive aphasia Frustrated | **ACTUAL** Impaired verbal communication related to cerebral injury as evidenced by inability to form words. | Within 3 days he can make his basic needs known by other means. | Speak slowly, in short simple sentences. Give him time to answer and do not finish his sentences for him. Use picture boards and gestures. Refer to the speech therapist. | Gets his basic needs across: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Coughs when he drinks **Objective** Face weak Gag reflex reduced | **RISK** Risk for aspiration as evidenced by risk factors: impaired swallowing and reduced level of consciousness. | No aspiration. | Nothing by mouth until the swallow screen is done. Sit him upright for meals and for 30 minutes afterwards. Give thickened fluids as recommended. Keep suction available. | Breath sounds clear: goal met. |

## 5.2  Transient Ischemic Attack

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My arm was numb for 20 minutes, then it went away." **Objective** Neurological exam normal now BP 158/94 mmHg Smokes | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to significance of TIA as evidenced by statement that symptoms are gone. | Before discharge, can explain that a TIA warns of a stroke and can list his risk factors. | Explain that a TIA is a warning sign. Teach the warning signs of stroke and that they need emergency care. Stress taking his medicines every day (antiplatelet, statin, antihypertensive). Book follow-up. | Explains what a TIA means: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I smoke and eat salty food." **Objective** Hypertensive High lipids | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to unhealthy lifestyle as evidenced by smoking and uncontrolled risk factors. | Commits to two changes in his lifestyle. | Help him stop smoking. Teach a diet low in salt and fat. Encourage regular exercise. | Describes his plan: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** TIA recently Hypertensive | **RISK** Risk for ineffective cerebral tissue perfusion as evidenced by risk factors: recent TIA and hypertension. | No stroke. | Check BP. Give the antiplatelet as prescribed. Check neurological status. | No neurological deficit: goal met. |

## 5.3  Traumatic Brain Injury

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Family say he fell and hit his head **Objective** GCS 11 Pupils unequal Vomiting Headache | **ACTUAL** Decreased intracranial adaptive capacity related to brain injury as evidenced by reduced GCS, pupil changes and vomiting. | GCS stays stable or gets better, with no signs of raised ICP. | Check GCS, pupils and vital signs as ordered (often every hour). Head of the bed up to 30 degrees, head kept in the midline. Do not let the neck flex, and keep tube ties loose. Keep him well oxygenated with a normal CO₂. Report a falling GCS, new pupil changes, or a rising BP with a slowing HR. | GCS stable or better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My head is pounding." **Objective** Grimacing Pain 7/10 | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to head trauma as evidenced by headache and grimacing. | Headache 3/10 or less within 1 hour of analgesia. | Give the prescribed analgesia, choosing one that will not hide neurological signs. Dim the lights and cut the noise. Keep the head of the bed at 30 degrees. Recheck pain each time you do neuro obs. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Head injury Restless | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: altered level of consciousness and possible seizures. | No injury. | Padded side rails, as policy allows. Bed kept low. Watch for seizures. | No injury: goal met. |

## 5.4  Increased Intracranial Pressure

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Says his headache is getting worse **Objective** GCS falling Projectile vomiting BP 180/70 mmHg, HR 52/min (pulse pressure widening) | **ACTUAL** Decreased intracranial adaptive capacity related to increased ICP as evidenced by decreasing GCS, vomiting and Cushing response. | ICP and cerebral perfusion pressure stay within the prescribed limits. | Report signs of rising ICP at once. Head of the bed at 30 degrees, head in the midline. Avoid anything that pushes ICP up: suctioning longer than needed, coughing, straining. Give osmotic therapy as prescribed. Keep temperature and blood glucose normal. | Neurological status stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Breathing irregular SpO₂ 90% | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to pressure on respiratory center as evidenced by irregular respirations and low SpO₂. | SpO₂ 94% or more and breathing regular. | Keep the airway open and give oxygen as prescribed. Continuous monitoring of RR, breathing pattern and SpO₂. Do not let CO₂ build up, because hypercapnia raises ICP. If ordered, prepare for intubation. | SpO₂ on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Not fully conscious Vomiting | **RISK** Risk for aspiration as evidenced by risk factors: decreased consciousness and vomiting. | Airway stays clear, with no aspiration. | Turn him on his side unless there is a reason not to. Have suction ready at the bedside. Nothing by mouth until his swallow has been checked. | No aspiration: goal met. |

## 5.5  Craniotomy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Drowsy after the operation **Objective** GCS 13 Dressing on the head Pupils equal and reactive | **ACTUAL** Decreased intracranial adaptive capacity related to surgical edema as evidenced by reduced GCS after surgery. | Within 24 hours, GCS stable or improving. | At first, check GCS, pupils and limb power every hour. Position as the surgeon has ordered. Watch the drain output and the dressing. Give the anticonvulsant and steroid as prescribed. | GCS improving: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My head hurts." **Objective** Pain 6/10 Frowning, does not want to move | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgical incision as evidenced by reported headache. | Pain 3/10 or less within 1 hour of analgesia. | Give analgesia as prescribed and check again. Keep the room quiet and dim. If he finds it comfortable, put a cold compress on his forehead. A sudden, severe headache must be reported (could be bleeding). | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Surgical wound Drain in situ | **RISK** Risk for surgical site infection as evidenced by risk factors: open cranial surgery and drain. | No signs of infection and no CSF leak. | Aseptic technique for wound care. Watch for clear fluid coming from the wound or the nose. Check temperature and neck stiffness. | Wound clean: goal met. |

## 5.6  Brain Tumour

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have headaches every morning." **Objective** Mass on CT Family say his personality has changed | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to tumour pressure as evidenced by personality change and disorientation. | Stays safe and as oriented as possible. | Every shift, check orientation and behaviour. Reorient him and keep the routine the same each day. Give steroids as prescribed to bring down the edema. | Safe and calm: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Will I recover?" **Objective** Family tearful Asks the same questions again and again | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to diagnosis and treatment as evidenced by worry and tearfulness. | Patient and family share their feelings and understand the plan. | Give information together with the medical team. Leave time for questions and feelings. Refer for psychological and social support. | Less anxious: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Brain tumour Cerebral edema Has had a seizure | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: seizures and impaired judgment. | No injury. | Seizure precautions in place. Give anticonvulsants as prescribed. If his judgment is impaired, supervise him when he moves about. | No injury: goal met. |

## 5.7  Epilepsy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I forget to take my tablets." **Objective** Having more seizures Drug level low | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to irregular use of antiepileptic drugs as evidenced by missed doses and increased seizures. | Takes his medicine every day and has fewer seizures. | Explain why regular doses matter. Suggest reminders and a pill organiser. Teach the triggers: lack of sleep, alcohol, missed doses. Tell him never to stop his drugs suddenly. | Fewer seizures: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "People are afraid of me." **Objective** Withdrawn Stays away from school or work | **ACTUAL** Social isolation related to stigma of seizures as evidenced by withdrawal. | Names people who support him and gets back to one social activity. | Encourage him to talk about how he feels. Teach the family about epilepsy and seizure first aid. Refer to an epilepsy support group. | Names his supports: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Seizure disorder Two seizures this month | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: seizures. | Patient and family can describe seizure first aid. | Teach the family: protect his head, put nothing in his mouth, roll him on his side when the jerking stops, time the seizure. He should not swim alone, and should not drive until he is allowed to. | Family describe first aid: goal met. |

## 5.8  Status Epilepticus

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Seizing non-stop for over 5 minutes SpO₂ 85% Cyanosed | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to continuous seizure activity as evidenced by cyanosis and low SpO₂. | Airway kept open and the seizure stopped as quickly as possible. | Call for urgent help. Turn him on his side, protect his head and move things out of the way. Give high-flow oxygen and suction as needed. Give the benzodiazepine as prescribed and follow the seizure protocol. Check the blood glucose. | Seizure over, SpO₂ back up: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Unresponsive Muscles rigid | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to muscle rigidity as evidenced by irregular breathing. | Ventilation adequate with SpO₂ 94% or more. | Be ready to support the airway or intubate. Continuous monitoring of SpO₂ and RR. Give oxygen. | Ventilation adequate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Seizure going on for a long time | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: uncontrolled motor activity. | No injury during seizures. | Pad the side rails. Never hold his movements down. Write down when the seizure started, how long it lasted and what type it was. | No injury: goal met. |

## 5.9  Meningitis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My neck is stiff and light hurts my eyes." **Objective** Temperature 39.4 °C Neck stiff Light hurts his eyes | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to central nervous system infection as evidenced by fever and neck stiffness. | Temperature below 38 °C within 24–48 hours. | Give IV antibiotics without delay, as prescribed. Give antipyretics. Watch temperature and neurological status. Isolate him as policy requires. | Afebrile: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Headache severe, 8/10 **Objective** Light hurts his eyes Grimacing | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to meningeal irritation as evidenced by headache and photophobia. | Pain 3/10 or less. | Make the room dark. Give analgesia as prescribed. Keep stimulation and visitors to a minimum. Check pain regularly. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Bacterial meningitis Has had close contacts | **RISK** Risk for infection (to others) as evidenced by risk factors: droplet transmission. | Infection does not spread to contacts. | Use droplet precautions as policy says. Inform infection control. Arrange prophylaxis for contacts as ordered. | No secondary cases: goal met. |

## 5.10  Encephalitis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Family say he is confused and feverish **Objective** Disoriented Fever 38.9 °C Behaving differently | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to brain inflammation as evidenced by disorientation and behaviour change. | Stays safe and becomes oriented as he improves. | Check neurological status regularly. Give the antiviral as prescribed. Reorient him and keep the surroundings calm. | More oriented: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family: "He is burning hot." **Objective** Temperature 38.9 °C Skin flushed | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to infection as evidenced by temperature 38.9 °C. | Temperature below 38 °C within 4 hours. | Give the antipyretic as prescribed. Keep the room cool and take off extra covers. Temperature every 2–4 hours. | Afebrile: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Brain inflammation Confused | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: seizures and confusion. | No injury. | Seizure precautions in place. Keep him under close observation. Bed low, side rails as policy allows. | No injury: goal met. |

## 5.11  Spinal Cord Injury

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot feel or move my legs." **Objective** Paraplegia No sensation below T10 | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to spinal cord injury as evidenced by inability to move lower limbs. | Spine kept aligned and the complications of immobility prevented. | Keep the spine aligned and log-roll as ordered. Do ROM exercises. Turn every 2 hours. Start rehabilitation. | No contractures or pressure injury: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Cannot control his bladder Bladder distended | **ACTUAL** Impaired urinary elimination related to neurogenic bladder as evidenced by urinary retention. | Bladder emptied regularly, with no infection. | Intermittent catheterisation as ordered. Record the output. Start bladder training. | Emptying regularly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Immobile No sensation | **RISK** Risk for adult pressure injury as evidenced by risk factors: immobility and sensory loss. | Skin stays intact. | Look at the skin at least twice a day, the sacrum and heels especially. Nurse him on a pressure-redistributing mattress. Turn every 2 hours. Teach him to lift himself to relieve pressure while in the wheelchair. | Skin intact: goal met. |

## 5.12  Autonomic Dysreflexia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have a pounding headache." **Objective** Injury at T6 or higher BP 200/110 mmHg, HR 50/min Flushed and sweating above the level of injury | **ACTUAL** Autonomic dysreflexia related to noxious stimulus below the injury level as evidenced by severe hypertension, bradycardia and headache. | BP back to baseline once the trigger is removed. | Sit him up and lower his legs. Loosen anything tight he is wearing. Check the bladder (kinked catheter, full bladder) and the bowel (impaction). BP every 2–5 minutes. If BP stays high, give the antihypertensive as prescribed. | BP normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "What caused this?" **Objective** First time this has happened Cannot name what sets it off | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to prevention of dysreflexia as evidenced by questions. | Patient and family can name the triggers and what to do first. | Teach the usual triggers: full bladder, constipation, tight clothes, pressure sores. Teach the emergency steps: sit up, loosen clothing, check the bladder. Give him an emergency card. | Explains correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Spinal cord injury at T4 | **RISK** Risk for autonomic dysreflexia as evidenced by risk factors: spinal cord injury at T6 or above. | Episodes prevented, or treated straight away. | Keep bladder and bowel routines going. Check skin and clothing for anything pressing. Teach the family the emergency steps. | No episodes: goal met. |

## 5.13  Guillain-Barré Syndrome

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My legs became weak and now my arms too." **Objective** Weakness spreading upwards Vital capacity falling | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to respiratory muscle weakness as evidenced by reduced vital capacity. | Ventilation adequate, and respiratory failure caught early. | Measure vital capacity as ordered. Watch SpO₂ and how well he can cough. If vital capacity drops, prepare for ventilation. Give immunoglobulin or plasmapheresis as ordered. | Breathing stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Cannot walk Leg muscle strength 2/5 | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to neuromuscular weakness as evidenced by inability to walk. | Joints stay mobile, no contractures. | Passive ROM 3 times a day. Change his position every 2 hours. Refer to physiotherapy. | No contractures: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Immobile On bed rest | **RISK** Risk for thrombosis as evidenced by risk factors: immobility. | No DVT. | Give prophylactic anticoagulant as ordered. Put on compression devices. Check the calves every day. | No DVT: goal met. |

## 5.14  Myasthenia Gravis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My eyelids droop and I get tired chewing." **Objective** Eyelids drooping Chewing and swallowing weak | **ACTUAL** Impaired swallowing related to neuromuscular weakness as evidenced by difficulty chewing and swallowing. | Eats safely, without choking. | Give the anticholinesterase 30–45 minutes before meals, as prescribed. Offer soft food and let her rest during the meal. Keep suction available. | Eating safely: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** More tired as the day goes on Eyelids droop more in the evening | **ACTUAL** Fatigue related to muscle weakness as evidenced by worsening weakness with activity. | Plans activities for the times when she is strongest. | Fit activities in soon after her medication. Encourage rest periods. Teach ways to save energy. | Activities planned: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Breathing muscles weak Cough weak | **RISK** Risk for aspiration as evidenced by risk factors: impaired swallowing and weak cough. | No aspiration, and a crisis is recognised early. | Watch her breathing and swallowing. Teach the signs of myasthenic and cholinergic crisis. | No aspiration: goal met. |

## 5.15  Multiple Sclerosis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel exhausted all the time." **Objective** Tired Walks unsteadily | **ACTUAL** Fatigue related to disease process as evidenced by persistent exhaustion. | Uses ways of saving her energy. | Teach her to pace her activities. Tell her to avoid heat and hot baths. Plan rest periods. Refer to occupational therapy. | Says she has more energy: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel unsteady when I walk." **Objective** Unsteady on her feet Weak | **ACTUAL** Impaired walking related to neuromuscular impairment as evidenced by unsteady gait. | Within 1 week, walks safely with aids. | Give her walking aids and show her how to use them. Refer to physiotherapy. Encourage regular exercise within her limits. | Walking safely: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Unsteady gait Vision disturbed | **RISK** Risk for adult falls as evidenced by risk factors: impaired gait and vision. | No falls. | Clear hazards at home. Use night lights. Teach her to get up slowly. | No falls: goal met. |

## 5.16  Parkinson's Disease

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My hands shake and I move slowly." **Objective** Tremor Moves slowly (bradykinesia) Shuffles when he walks | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to rigidity and bradykinesia as evidenced by slow movement and shuffling gait. | Stays mobile and manages ADL with aids. | Give his medicines on time; any delay makes symptoms worse. Encourage exercise and stretching. Teach him to take big steps and lift his feet when walking. | Mobility kept: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cough while eating." **Objective** Drools Swallows slowly | **ACTUAL** Impaired swallowing related to muscle rigidity as evidenced by drooling and coughing during meals. | Eats safely, without coughing or choking. | Sit him up for meals and for 30 minutes after. Offer small mouthfuls and do not rush him. Refer to the speech therapist. Give his medicines on time before meals. | Eating safely: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Balance poor Shuffles | **RISK** Risk for adult falls as evidenced by risk factors: shuffling gait and postural instability. | No falls. | Fall precautions in place. Teach him to get up slowly and to turn in a wide arc. Get rid of rugs and clutter. | No falls: goal met. |

## 5.17  Dementia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Family say she forgets things that happened recently **Objective** Does not know the date Asks the same thing again and again | **ACTUAL** Chronic confusion related to progressive brain degeneration as evidenced by memory loss and disorientation. | Stays safe and calm in a familiar routine. | Keep the daily routine the same. Give simple instructions and use visual cues. Keep familiar things close by. | Calm and safe: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family: "She puts her clothes on in the wrong order." **Objective** Needs help to dress | **ACTUAL** Dressing self-care deficit related to cognitive impairment as evidenced by inability to dress without help. | Dresses herself with prompts and very little help. | Lay her clothes out in the order she puts them on. Give one instruction at a time. Choose simple clothes with elastic and Velcro. | Takes part: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family: "He left the house at night." **Objective** Wanders | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: wandering and impaired judgment. | No injury. | Make doors and surroundings secure. Have him wear an identification bracelet. Support and teach the caregivers. | No injury: goal met. |

## 5.18  Delirium

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Family say "He is not himself since yesterday." **Objective** Attention comes and goes Disoriented Agitated at night | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to (cause, for example infection, drugs, dehydration) as evidenced by fluctuating consciousness and disorientation. | Back to his usual orientation as the cause is treated. | Find and treat the cause together with the medical team. Reorient him often; keep his glasses and hearing aids on. Encourage family to be with him. Help him sleep at night and stay active in the day. Avoid restraints and any sedative that is not needed. | Oriented: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family: "He sleeps all day and is awake at night." **Objective** Awake at night Sleeps in the day | **ACTUAL** Disturbed sleep pattern related to confusion as evidenced by night waking. | Within 3 days he sleeps at night and is awake in the day. | Keep noise and light down at night. Open the curtains and keep him active in the day. Do not wake him at night unless you need to. | Sleeping better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Agitated Keeps trying to climb out of bed | **RISK** Risk for adult falls as evidenced by risk factors: confusion and agitation. | No falls. | Fall precautions in place. Keep him under close observation. Keep the call bell and anything he needs within reach. | No falls: goal met. |

## 5.19  Herniated Disc and Laminectomy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The pain goes down my leg." **Objective** Pain 8/10 Straight leg raise positive | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to nerve root compression as evidenced by radiating pain. | Pain 3/10 or less. | Give analgesia. After surgery, log-roll as ordered. Teach good posture. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am afraid to move after the operation." **Objective** Moves very little Guarding | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to pain and surgery as evidenced by limited movement. | If ordered, walks with help on day 1. | Help him walk early, as ordered. Teach him not to bend, lift or twist. Teach log-rolling and safe transfers. | Walking: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has had spinal surgery | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: nerve damage or CSF leak after surgery. | Neurological function preserved. | Check leg movement and sensation. Look at the dressing for clear fluid. Make sure he can pass urine. | No deficit: goal met. |

## 5.20  The Unconscious Patient

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** GCS 7 Snoring breaths Secretions pooling | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to decreased level of consciousness as evidenced by snoring and pooled secretions. | Airway stays patent. | Lay him on his side or raise the head of the bed. Put in an airway adjunct if needed. Suction as needed. Watch SpO₂. | Airway clear: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family ask how they can help with his care **Objective** Cannot do any self-care GCS 7 | **ACTUAL** Bathing self-care deficit related to unconsciousness as evidenced by total dependence. | Skin, eyes and mouth stay clean and intact. | Give full hygiene care. Give eye care so the cornea is not damaged. Mouth care every 4 hours. If the family want to, let them help with care. | Skin, eyes and mouth clean: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Immobile Incontinent | **RISK** Risk for adult pressure injury as evidenced by risk factors: immobility and incontinence. | Skin stays intact. | Turn every 2 hours. After incontinence, clean the skin and keep it dry. Nurse him on a pressure-redistributing mattress. | Skin intact: goal met. |

**PART 6**

# Gastrointestinal Care Plans

## 6.1  Gastroesophageal Reflux Disease (GERD)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have burning behind my chest after meals." Worse when he lies down **Objective** Food comes back up Takes antacids every day | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to acid reflux into the esophagus as evidenced by retrosternal burning after meals. | Within 1 week, says the heartburn is less. | Give the proton pump inhibitor before breakfast, as prescribed. Advise small meals and nothing to eat in the 2–3 hours before bed. Raise the head of the bed by 15–20 cm. He should avoid fatty food, coffee, smoking and tight clothes. | Fewer symptoms: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I did not know food affects this." **Objective** Eats late at night Drinks coffee several times a day | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to lifestyle measures as evidenced by unhealthy eating pattern. | Before discharge, names three changes he will make to his lifestyle. | Teach which foods set it off, and weight loss if he is overweight. Give the advice in writing. | Lists his changes: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Reflux at night Coughs at night | **RISK** Risk for aspiration as evidenced by risk factors: reflux of gastric contents at night. | No aspiration and no night cough. | Keep the head of the bed raised 15–20 cm. No meals in the 2–3 hours before lying down. Give the acid-reducing medicine as prescribed. | No cough, no chest infection: goal met. |

## 6.2  Gastritis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My upper stomach hurts and I feel full quickly." **Objective** Tender in the epigastrium Takes NSAIDs | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to gastric mucosal irritation as evidenced by epigastric pain and tenderness. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Give the acid-reducing medicine as prescribed. Stop NSAIDs and alcohol as advised. Offer small, bland meals. | Pain less: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Vomited twice today **Objective** Mouth dry Passing less urine | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to vomiting as evidenced by dry mucosa. | Within 24 hours, mouth moist and urine output adequate. | Keep an I&O chart. Give IV or oral fluids as ordered. Give the antiemetic as prescribed. | Hydrated: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Endoscopy shows erosive gastritis Takes NSAIDs | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: gastric mucosal erosion. | Any bleeding is picked up early. | Watch for vomiting of blood and black stools. Follow the Hb. Teach him to stay off NSAIDs and alcohol. | No bleeding: goal met. |

## 6.3  Peptic Ulcer Disease

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The pain gnaws in my stomach and wakes me at night." **Objective** Epigastric pain eased by eating Positive for H. pylori | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to ulceration of gastric or duodenal mucosa as evidenced by gnawing epigastric pain. | Pain relieved within 48 hours of starting treatment. | Give the eradication therapy and PPI as prescribed. Stress that he must finish the whole antibiotic course. He should avoid NSAIDs, smoking and alcohol. | Pain relieved: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I did not finish the last course of tablets." **Objective** Ulcer has come back Did not finish eradication therapy | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to complex regimen as evidenced by incomplete treatment. | Finishes the prescribed course of treatment. | Go through each drug and when to take it. Give him a written plan. | Course finished: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Ulcer active Smokes | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: ulcer erosion into blood vessel. | No bleeding, or signs reported early. | Teach the signs of bleeding: vomiting blood, black stools, dizziness. Follow the Hb. He should avoid NSAIDs and smoking. | No bleeding: goal met. |

## 6.4  Upper Gastrointestinal Bleeding

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I vomited blood and feel faint." **Objective** Vomiting blood Black stools (melena) BP 88/56 mmHg, HR 124/min Hb 7.5 g/dL | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to acute blood loss as evidenced by hypotension, tachycardia and low Hb. | Within 24 hours, vital signs stable and Hb on target. | Put in two large-bore IV cannulas. Give fluids and blood as prescribed. Vital signs every 15 minutes until stable. Nothing by mouth; get him ready for endoscopy. Give the IV PPI as prescribed. Record urine output every hour. | Vital signs stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Am I going to die?" **Objective** Frightened and shaking Holding tight to a relative's hand | **ACTUAL** Fear related to sight of blood and hospital procedures as evidenced by expressed fear. | Says he is less frightened within 2 hours. | Stay with him. Explain each procedure briefly and calmly. Take away anything stained with blood quickly. Let the family stay. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Still bleeding HR going up | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: hypovolemia. | MAP kept above 65 mmHg. | Vital signs every 15–30 minutes. Watch for signs that he is bleeding again. Keep blood crossmatched. Report any change straight away. | No shock: goal met. |

## 6.5  Esophageal Varices

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Vomiting a lot of fresh blood **Objective** Known to have cirrhosis BP 85/50 mmHg | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to variceal hemorrhage as evidenced by hematemesis and hypotension. | Hemodynamics stable after treatment. | Protect the airway; lie him on his side. Give blood and the vasoactive drug (terlipressin, for example) as prescribed. Get ready for endoscopic band ligation. Give the prophylactic antibiotic as ordered. | Bleeding controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Blood in the airway Not fully conscious | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to blood in airway as evidenced by gurgling respirations. | Airway stays clear. | Keep him on his side and suction as needed. Give oxygen. If ordered, prepare for intubation. | Airway clear: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Portal hypertension Bled from varices recently | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: varices and impaired clotting. | No further bleeding. | Once he has recovered, give the beta-blocker as prescribed. Teach him that he must stop alcohol completely. Book the repeat endoscopy for its scheduled date. | No rebleeding: goal met. |

## 6.6  Gastrectomy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Gives his abdominal pain 7/10 **Objective** Incision in the upper abdomen NG tube in situ | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgical incision as evidenced by pain score 7/10. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Give analgesia regularly as prescribed. Never reposition the NG tube; report it if it comes out of place. Support the incision when he coughs. Get him moving early. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I get dizzy and sweaty after eating." **Objective** Symptoms come 15–30 minutes after eating | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to reduced gastric capacity and dumping syndrome as evidenced by symptoms after meals. | Eats without dumping symptoms. | Advise small meals often, high in protein and low in simple sugar. Drinks go between meals, not with them. Lie down for 20–30 minutes after eating. Teach that he will need vitamin B12 replacement long term, as prescribed. | No dumping symptoms: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Major abdominal surgery Drain in situ | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: surgical wound and anastomosis. | No infection, no anastomotic leak. | Watch temperature, the wound and the drain. Report fever, a fast heart rate or pain that is getting worse. Aseptic technique for wound care. | No infection: goal met. |

## 6.7  Crohn's Disease

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have cramps and diarrhea many times a day." **Objective** 6 stools a day Tender in the right lower quadrant | **ACTUAL** Diarrhea related to bowel inflammation as evidenced by frequent loose stools and cramps. | Within 1 week of treatment, fewer stools. | Chart how often she opens her bowels. Give anti-inflammatory and immunosuppressive drugs as prescribed. Keep her hydrated. Protect the skin around the anus. | Fewer stools: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I avoid eating to avoid pain." **Objective** Lost 5 kg Albumin low | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to malabsorption and reduced intake as evidenced by weight loss. | Weight holds steady within 2 weeks. | Refer to the dietitian. Offer small meals high in calories. Weigh once a week. Give supplements as prescribed. | Weight steady: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On immunosuppressants On steroids | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: immunosuppression. | No infection. | Teach her to report fever quickly. Teach hand hygiene. Check her vaccinations are up to date. | No infection: goal met. |

## 6.8  Ulcerative Colitis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I pass bloody stools 8 times a day." "I rush to the toilet." **Objective** Diarrhea with blood Cramps in the lower abdomen Hyperactive bowel sounds | **ACTUAL** Diarrhea related to inflammation of colonic mucosa as evidenced by frequent bloody stools and urgency. | Within 1 week of treatment, fewer stools with less blood. | Chart how often, how loose, and whether there is blood. Give corticosteroids and aminosalicylates as prescribed. Keep a commode or the toilet close at hand. Protect the skin around the anus. She should avoid foods that make things worse. | Fewer stools: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel dizzy when I stand." **Objective** HR 112/min Hb 9 g/dL Mouth dry | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to fluid and blood loss in stool as evidenced by tachycardia, dry mucosa and low Hb. | Within 24 hours, vital signs stable and urine output adequate. | Give IV fluids and blood as prescribed. Keep an I&O chart. Follow potassium and Hb. | Vital signs stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Colitis severe On high-dose steroids | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: toxic megacolon and perforation in severe colitis. | Complications are picked up early. | Measure her abdominal girth every day. Report sudden distension, fever, a fast heart rate, or fewer stools along with pain. No antidiarrheals or opioids unless ordered. | No complication: goal met. |

## 6.9  Appendicitis and Appendectomy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The pain started around my navel and moved to the right side." Gives the pain 8/10 **Objective** Tender in the right lower quadrant Temperature 38.2 °C WBC up | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to inflammation of the appendix as evidenced by right lower quadrant pain and tenderness. | Pain 3/10 or less before and after surgery. | Nothing by mouth; give IV fluids as ordered. Give analgesia as prescribed. Let him lie with his knees slightly bent. No heat on the abdomen and no laxatives. If the pain suddenly eases and then spreads all over, report it (could be perforation). | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Will it be a big operation?" **Objective** Looks anxious Keeps asking questions | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to emergency surgery as evidenced by worry and repeated questions. | Says he understands the operation and feels less anxious. | Explain the procedure and what recovery will be like. Do the pre-op preparation calmly. Let the family stay. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Appendix inflamed or perforated Surgical incision | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: possible perforation and surgical incision. | No wound infection and no abscess. | Give antibiotics as prescribed. Watch the temperature, the wound and his abdominal pain. Get him moving early. | No infection: goal met. |

## 6.10  Peritonitis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My whole abdomen hurts; I cannot move." **Objective** Abdomen rigid like a board Rebound tenderness Lies very still with knees bent | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to peritoneal inflammation as evidenced by severe generalized pain and rigidity. | Pain 3/10 or less after treatment. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Let him keep his knees bent. Nothing by mouth; pass an NG tube as ordered. Get him ready for surgery. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am very thirsty." **Objective** HR 120/min Urine output 15 mL/hour 800 mL drained from the NG tube | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to fluid shift into the peritoneal cavity and NG losses as evidenced by tachycardia and low urine output. | Within 8 hours, urine output at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour. | Give IV fluids as prescribed. Record urine output and NG losses every hour. Follow electrolytes. | Output adequate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Infection in the peritoneum Fever 39 °C | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: sepsis and hypovolemia. | MAP kept above 65 mmHg. | Give antibiotics on time. Watch vital signs and lactate as ordered. Report low BP, confusion or mottled skin. | Hemodynamics stable: goal met. |

## 6.11  Intestinal Obstruction

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My abdomen is swollen and I cannot pass wind." Colicky pain, 7/10 **Objective** Abdomen distended Vomiting Bowel sounds high-pitched | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to bowel distension as evidenced by colicky pain and distension. | Within 24 hours, less pain and less distension. | Nothing by mouth. Pass an NG tube to decompress the stomach as ordered, and record what drains. Give analgesia as prescribed. Measure abdominal girth every 4–8 hours. | Less pain, girth smaller: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I keep vomiting." **Objective** Mouth dry Urine output low Potassium low | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to vomiting and fluid sequestration in the bowel as evidenced by dry mucosa and low urine output. | Within 24 hours, urine output adequate and electrolytes normal. | Give IV fluids and potassium as prescribed. Keep an I&O chart, counting NG losses. Follow electrolytes. | Balanced: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Bowel completely obstructed HR climbing | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: strangulation, bowel ischemia and fluid loss. | Signs of strangulation are picked up early. | Report constant pain, fever, a rising HR or signs of peritonism. Get him ready for surgery as ordered. | No shock: goal met. |

## 6.12  Hernia Repair

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The groin wound hurts when I move." Gives the pain 5/10 **Objective** Incision in the groin Guards the area when he changes position HR 96/min | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgical incision as evidenced by pain score 5/10 and guarding. | Pain 3/10 or less within 1 hour of analgesia. | Check pain regularly and after each intervention. Give analgesia as prescribed. Show him how to support the wound with a hand or pillow when he coughs or gets up. If ordered for scrotal swelling, use a scrotal support or ice pack. | Pain 3/10 or less: goal met. If not, go over the analgesia with the surgeon. |
| **Subjective** "When can I go back to lifting at work?" **Objective** Does manual work Cannot say what he should avoid doing | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to post-operative activity restrictions as evidenced by questions and inability to state limits. | Before discharge, can explain his lifting limits and how to care for the wound. | He must avoid heavy lifting and straining for as long as the surgeon advises. Teach him to prevent constipation with fluids and fibre. Teach wound care and the signs of infection. Teach him to report a new bulge or severe pain. | States his limits correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I have not passed urine since the operation." **Objective** Had spinal or general anesthesia On opioid analgesia Older man | **RISK** Risk for urinary retention as evidenced by risk factors: anesthesia, opioids and groin surgery. | Passes urine within 6–8 hours of surgery. | Record when he first passes urine and how much. If he has not voided, feel or scan the bladder. If allowed, help him stand up to pass urine. If he still cannot void, catheterise as ordered. | Voids adequately: goal met. |

## 6.13  Colorectal Cancer

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
