| **Subjective** "I have lost weight and do not feel like eating." **Objective** Lost 6 kg in 3 months Hb 9.5 g/dL Albumin low | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to disease process and reduced intake as evidenced by weight loss and low albumin. | Weight holds or goes up before surgery or treatment. | Screen her nutrition on admission. Refer to the dietitian for supplements. Offer small meals often, high in protein. Weigh twice a week. Give iron or blood for the anemia as prescribed. | Weight steady or rising: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am frightened of having a bag." "Will the cancer come back?" **Objective** Tearful Sleeping badly | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to cancer diagnosis and possible stoma as evidenced by expressed fear and insomnia. | Before surgery, shares her worries and says she is less anxious. | Give her time for questions, and listen. Before surgery, arrange for the stoma nurse to visit and mark the stoma site. Give clear information together with the medical team. Bring in the family as she wishes. | Says she feels better prepared: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Bowel resection planned Anemic Albumin low | **RISK** Risk for surgical site infection as evidenced by risk factors: bowel surgery, anemia and poor nutrition. | Wound heals without infection and there are no signs of an anastomotic leak. | Give the prophylactic antibiotic as ordered. Watch the wound, the drain output and the temperature. Report fever, worsening abdominal pain or a fast heart rate (may be a leak). | No infection: goal met. |

## 6.14  Colostomy and Ileostomy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I do not want to look at it." "My wife will be disgusted." **Objective** Looks away during stoma care Will not touch the bag | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to stoma formation as evidenced by refusal to look at or touch the stoma. | Before discharge, looks at the stoma and joins in with its care. | Let him talk about how he feels. Bring him into his care one step at a time. If he agrees, involve his wife. Offer to put him in touch with a stoma support group or someone who has lived with a stoma. | Looks at and handles the stoma: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "How do I change the bag?" **Objective** Stoma is new Cannot explain how to change the bag | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to stoma care as evidenced by questions and inability to describe care. | Before discharge, changes the appliance correctly by himself. | Teach him to measure the stoma and cut the flange to fit. Teach him to clean the skin with water and dry it before putting the bag on. Teach him to empty the pouch when it is one-third full. For an ileostomy: teach him to drink plenty and to know the signs of dehydration. Teach what to report: a dark stoma, no output, bleeding. | Shows you correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Liquid output (ileostomy) Bag does not fit well | **RISK** Risk for impaired skin integrity as evidenced by risk factors: contact of stoma output with skin. | Skin around the stoma stays intact. | Check the flange fits every time it is changed. Look at the skin around the stoma. Use barrier products as recommended. | Skin intact: goal met. |

## 6.15  Hemorrhoids and Hemorrhoidectomy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "It hurts to sit and to pass stool." Gives the pain 7/10 **Objective** Swelling around the anus Shifts his weight when sitting | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to inflamed hemorrhoids or surgical trauma as evidenced by pain score 7/10 and difficulty sitting. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Give analgesia regularly, and especially before the first bowel movement. Warm sitz baths 2–3 times a day, as ordered. Give him a soft cushion and suggest lying on his side. Apply the topical treatment as prescribed. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am afraid to open my bowels." **Objective** No stool for 3 days Usually passes hard stool | **ACTUAL** Constipation related to fear of pain on defecation as evidenced by no stool for 3 days. | Passes soft stool within 48 hours. | Give the stool softener as prescribed. Encourage 2–2.5 L/day of fluid and a high-fibre diet. He should not strain or sit on the toilet for long. | Soft stool passed: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Had anal surgery One pad soaked since the operation | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: surgical wound in vascular tissue. | No significant bleeding. | Check the dressing and pads. Watch vital signs. Tell him to report heavy bleeding or clots once he is home. | No significant bleeding: goal met. |

## 6.16  Cholecystitis and Cholecystectomy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Severe pain under my right ribs after a fatty meal." The pain goes through to her right shoulder **Objective** Tender in the right upper quadrant Murphy sign positive Temperature 38.1 °C | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to gallbladder inflammation and obstruction as evidenced by right upper quadrant pain and positive Murphy sign. | Pain 3/10 or less within 2 hours of treatment. | Give analgesia and an antispasmodic as prescribed. During the acute attack, nothing by mouth as ordered. Give IV antibiotics if prescribed. Get her ready for surgery. Watch for jaundice, dark urine and pale stools. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "What can I eat now?" "Why does my shoulder hurt?" **Objective** Had a laparoscopic cholecystectomy Cannot say what she should eat | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to post-operative care and diet as evidenced by questions. | Before discharge, can repeat the diet advice and the warning signs. | Explain that pain at the shoulder tip comes from the gas used in laparoscopy and settles as she walks about. She can go back to a normal diet, cutting down fatty food at first if it upsets her. Teach wound care. Tell her to report jaundice, fever or worsening pain. | Repeats the advice: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Surgical wounds Inflamed gallbladder taken out | **RISK** Risk for surgical site infection as evidenced by risk factors: surgical incisions and infected gallbladder. | Wounds heal without infection. | Watch the wounds and her temperature. Keep the dressings dry and intact. | No infection: goal met. |

## 6.17  Acute Pancreatitis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Severe pain in the upper abdomen going to my back." Gives the pain 9/10 **Objective** Lipase raised Guarding Vomiting | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to pancreatic inflammation as evidenced by severe epigastric pain radiating to the back. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Give opioid analgesia regularly as prescribed. Sit him up leaning forward, or let him lie with his knees bent. At first, nothing by mouth as ordered; start feeding when it is ordered. If the vomiting does not stop, pass an NG tube as ordered. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am very thirsty." **Objective** HR 118/min, BP 100/60 mmHg Urine output 20 mL/hour | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to fluid shift into tissues and vomiting as evidenced by tachycardia, hypotension and low urine output. | Within 12 hours, urine output at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour. | Give IV fluids as prescribed. Record urine output every hour. Watch calcium (look for tetany) and potassium. Vital signs every hour. | Output adequate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Pancreas inflamed Glucose 190 mg/dL | **RISK** Risk for unstable blood glucose level as evidenced by risk factors: pancreatic injury. | Blood glucose stays on target. | Check blood glucose every 4–6 hours. Give insulin as ordered. | On target: goal met. |

## 6.18  Chronic Pancreatitis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The pain never fully goes away." Drinks alcohol every day **Objective** Epigastric pain for a long time Calcification seen on imaging | **ACTUAL** Chronic pain related to pancreatic damage as evidenced by persistent abdominal pain for months. | Pain at a level that lets him get on with his day. | Work out a regular analgesic plan with the physician. Help him stop drinking and refer him to services. Teach pain measures that do not involve drugs. Watch for dependence on analgesics. | Pain manageable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My stool is greasy and floats." **Objective** Fatty stools (steatorrhea) Lost 5 kg | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to malabsorption as evidenced by steatorrhea and weight loss. | Weight stays steady. | Give pancreatic enzymes with every meal and every snack, as prescribed. Suggest small meals, often. Refer to the dietitian. Weigh once a week. | Weight steady: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Islet cells lost Fasting glucose going up | **RISK** Risk for unstable blood glucose level as evidenced by risk factors: pancreatic endocrine insufficiency. | Blood glucose stays on target. | Check blood glucose. Teach the symptoms of high and low blood sugar. If diabetes develops, refer to the diabetes educator. | Controlled: goal met. |

## 6.19  Viral Hepatitis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel tired all day and my eyes are yellow." **Objective** Jaundice ALT and bilirubin raised Dark urine | **ACTUAL** Fatigue related to liver inflammation as evidenced by persistent tiredness and reduced activity. | Within 2 weeks, says his energy is slowly coming back. | Encourage rest in the acute phase. Build up activity slowly. Follow liver function tests. | Energy improving: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel sick and do not want to eat." **Objective** Nausea Eating less | **ACTUAL** Nausea related to liver dysfunction as evidenced by reported nausea and reduced intake. | Eats and drinks enough. | Offer small meals often, with the bigger meal in the morning when the nausea is less. He must avoid alcohol completely. Unless ordered, avoid drugs that harm the liver, including too much paracetamol. | Intake adequate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Lives with his wife and children **Objective** Hepatitis A, B or C confirmed | **RISK** Risk for infection (to others) as evidenced by risk factors: transmissible virus. | Infection not passed on to contacts. | Explain how his type spreads (fecal-oral for A and E, blood and body fluids for B and C). Teach hand hygiene and not to share razors or toothbrushes. Encourage hepatitis B vaccination for everyone in the household. | States the precautions: goal met. |

## 6.20  Liver Cirrhosis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My abdomen and legs are swollen." **Objective** Ascites Edema in both legs Albumin 25 g/L | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to portal hypertension and low albumin as evidenced by ascites and edema. | Within 1 week, weight and abdominal girth are coming down. | Weigh daily and measure abdominal girth. Limit sodium as prescribed. Give diuretics as prescribed, and watch potassium and sodium. Raise the legs. | Weight and girth falling: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "The itching does not let me sleep." **Objective** Jaundice Scratch marks on the skin | **ACTUAL** Impaired comfort related to pruritus from bile salts as evidenced by itching and scratch marks. | Says the itching is less. | Keep nails short and the skin moisturised. Cool baths, not hot water. Give the antipruritic as ordered. | Less itching: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** INR prolonged Platelets low Varices | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: impaired clotting and portal hypertension. | No bleeding. | Follow INR, platelets and Hb. Soft toothbrush and electric razor only. No IM injections. Teach the signs of GI bleeding. | No bleeding: goal met. |

## 6.21  Hepatic Encephalopathy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Family say he is confused and sleepy **Objective** Flapping tremor (asterixis) Does not know the time or place Ammonia raised | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to accumulation of ammonia as evidenced by disorientation and flapping tremor. | Back to his usual orientation within 72 hours. | Give lactulose as prescribed, aiming for 2–3 soft stools a day. Give rifaximin if ordered. No sedatives. Look for triggers and treat them: GI bleeding, infection, constipation, electrolyte imbalance. Check orientation every shift. | Oriented: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family: "He forgets to eat." **Objective** Cannot feed himself Attention poor | **ACTUAL** Feeding self-care deficit related to confusion as evidenced by inability to feed self. | Gets enough to eat. | Help him at mealtimes. Do not cut protein unless it is ordered. Chart what he eats. | Intake adequate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Confused Weak | **RISK** Risk for adult falls as evidenced by risk factors: confusion and weakness. | No falls. | Fall precautions in place. Bed low and call bell within reach. Help him when he walks. | No falls: goal met. |

## 6.22  Ascites and Paracentesis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot breathe well with this big belly." **Objective** Ascites tense RR 26/min Using accessory muscles | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to pressure of ascites on the diaphragm as evidenced by dyspnea and tachypnea. | After paracentesis, says breathing is easier. | Sit him semi-Fowler. Ask him to empty his bladder before the procedure. Take baseline vital signs and weight. Record how much fluid comes off and what it looks like. After large-volume drainage, give albumin as ordered. | Breathing easier: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My abdomen feels heavy and tight." **Objective** Abdomen distended Keeps changing position | **ACTUAL** Impaired comfort related to abdominal distension as evidenced by reported discomfort. | Says he is more comfortable. | Support him with pillows. Give him loose clothes. Give analgesia if prescribed. | Comfortable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Had large-volume paracentesis | **RISK** Risk for deficient fluid volume as evidenced by risk factors: rapid fluid shift after drainage. | BP stays stable after the procedure. | Check BP and HR during the procedure and afterwards. Check the puncture site for leaking. Weigh him the next day. | Vital signs stable: goal met. |

## 6.23  Bariatric Surgery

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel full after a few spoons." "What can I eat now?" **Objective** Has had a sleeve gastrectomy or bypass Cannot describe the diet stages | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to post-operative diet stages as evidenced by questions. | Before discharge, can describe how her diet will progress. | Teach the diet in stages: clear fluids, then puree, then soft, then normal, as advised. Teach small portions, eating slowly and stopping when full. Drinks go between meals, not with them. Explain she will need vitamin and mineral supplements for life. | Describes the plan correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives the pain 6/10 **Objective** Laparoscopic wounds Pain at the shoulder tip | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgery as evidenced by pain score 6/10. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Get her moving early to ease the shoulder-tip pain. Support the wounds when she coughs. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** BMI above 40 kg/m² Had abdominal surgery | **RISK** Risk for thrombosis as evidenced by risk factors: obesity and surgery. | No DVT, no pulmonary embolism. | Get her moving early. Give the prophylactic anticoagulant as ordered. Put on compression devices. | No DVT: goal met. |

## 6.24  Malnutrition

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I eat very little." **Objective** BMI 16.5 kg/m² Muscles wasted Albumin low | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to inadequate intake as evidenced by low BMI and muscle wasting. | Within 2 weeks, putting on weight steadily. | Screen his nutrition. Refer to the dietitian. Offer oral supplements between meals. Weigh twice a week. Chart what he eats. | Weight going up: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am too weak to walk far." **Objective** Muscles weak Tired out after 20 metres | **ACTUAL** Activity intolerance related to muscle wasting as evidenced by weakness. | Builds up his activity slowly. | Plan activity that increases bit by bit, with rests. Refer to the physiotherapist. Check HR while he is active. | Tolerating more: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Severely malnourished Starting nutritional support | **RISK** Risk for electrolyte imbalance as evidenced by risk factors: refeeding syndrome. | Phosphate, potassium and magnesium stay within normal range. | Start feeding slowly, as prescribed. In the first days, check electrolytes every day. Give thiamine before feeding, as ordered. | Values normal: goal met. |

**PART 7**

# Renal and Urinary Care Plans

## 7.1  Urinary Tract Infection

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "It burns when I pass urine and I go very often." **Objective** Urine cloudy and smells bad Dipstick positive for nitrites and leukocytes Tender above the pubis | **ACTUAL** Impaired urinary elimination related to bladder infection as evidenced by dysuria, frequency and urgency. | Within 48–72 hours of treatment, says the burning and frequency have gone. | Collect a midstream urine sample before the antibiotics, as ordered. Give antibiotics as prescribed and stress finishing the course. Unless fluids are restricted, encourage 2–3 L/day. She should pass urine often and empty her bladder fully each time. | Symptoms gone: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives suprapubic pain 5/10 **Objective** Tender above the pubis Uncomfortable when passing urine | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to bladder mucosal inflammation as evidenced by suprapubic pain and burning. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Give analgesia or a urinary analgesic as prescribed. If she finds it soothing, put warmth over the lower abdomen. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I get this infection every few months." **Objective** Keeps getting UTIs Female, sexually active | **RISK** Risk for infection (recurrent) as evidenced by risk factors: history of recurrent UTI. | Can describe how to prevent another infection. | Teach her to wipe from front to back. Teach her to pass urine after intercourse. Encourage plenty of fluids and not holding on to urine. | Describes the measures: goal met. |

## 7.2  Pyelonephritis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have pain in my side and I feel shivery." **Objective** Temperature 39 °C Tender at the costovertebral angle Pus cells in the urine | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to kidney infection as evidenced by temperature 39 °C and chills. | Afebrile within 72 hours of starting antibiotics. | Send urine and blood cultures as ordered. Give IV antibiotics on time. Give antipyretics. Encourage drinking, or give IV fluids as ordered. | Afebrile: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives flank pain 7/10 **Objective** Guarding Restless | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to renal inflammation as evidenced by flank pain. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Put a warm compress on the flank. Encourage fluids. Check pain regularly. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Kidney infected Heart rate fast | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: possible urosepsis. | Vital signs and urine output stay stable. | Vital signs every 4 hours, and keep track of urine output. Report low BP, a fast heart rate or confusion. Give IV antibiotics on time. | Stable: goal met. |

## 7.3  Acute Glomerulonephritis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My face is puffy in the morning and my urine looks like cola." **Objective** Puffy around the eyes Blood and protein in the urine BP 160/100 mmHg Passing little urine | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to decreased glomerular filtration as evidenced by edema, oliguria and hypertension. | Within 1 week, less edema and BP on target. | Weigh every day. Strict I&O chart. Limit sodium and fluid as prescribed. Give antihypertensives and diuretics as prescribed. Follow urea, creatinine and potassium. | Edema less, BP controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel tired." **Objective** Tired Doing less than usual | **ACTUAL** Fatigue related to disease process as evidenced by tiredness. | Within 1 week, says he has more energy. | Encourage rest in the acute phase. As the edema goes down, build up activity slowly. Plan care so he can rest. | More energy: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Hypertensive Fluid overloaded | **RISK** Risk for ineffective cerebral tissue perfusion as evidenced by risk factors: severe hypertension. | No neurological complications. | BP every 4 hours. Report headache, changes in vision or seizures. Give antihypertensives as prescribed. | No complication: goal met. |

## 7.4  Renal and Ureteric Calculi

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The pain comes in waves from my back to my groin." Gives the pain 10/10 **Objective** Restless, cannot get comfortable Blood in the urine Nauseated | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to obstruction and spasm of the ureter by a calculus as evidenced by severe colicky flank pain. | Pain 3/10 or less within 1 hour of analgesia. | Give analgesia (NSAID or opioid) as prescribed and check again. Unless there is a reason not to, encourage 2–3 L/day of fluid. Strain every drop of urine and keep any stone for analysis. Encourage walking to help the stone pass. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I pass small amounts of urine often." **Objective** Going often Blood in the urine | **ACTUAL** Impaired urinary elimination related to ureteric obstruction as evidenced by frequency and hematuria. | Passes urine normally once the stone has passed or been removed. | Keep an I&O chart. If he passes no urine at all, report it at once. Get him ready for lithotripsy or ureteroscopy as ordered. | Voiding normally: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I drink very little water at work." **Objective** Has had a stone before Works in the heat and drinks little | **RISK** Risk for ineffective health self-management as evidenced by risk factors: low fluid intake and lack of knowledge of prevention. | Can explain how to stop stones coming back. | Teach him to drink enough to pass 2–2.5 L of urine a day. Give diet advice that fits his type of stone. Tell him to come in urgently if he gets fever with flank pain. | Explains prevention: goal met. |

## 7.5  Acute Kidney Injury

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I am passing very little urine." **Objective** Only 15 mL/hour of urine for 6 hours Creatinine going up Crackles and edema | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to reduced renal excretion as evidenced by oliguria, edema and crackles. | Fluid balance achieved and weight stable. | Record urine output every hour and keep a strict I&O chart. Weigh every day. Restrict fluids as prescribed (often the previous day's output plus insensible loss). Go through his drugs with the physician and look for any that harm the kidneys. If ordered, prepare for dialysis. | Fluid balance achieved: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel weak and my heart is jumping." **Objective** Serum K⁺ 6.4 mmol/L Tall, peaked T waves on ECG | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to hyperkalemia as evidenced by ECG changes. | Serum K⁺ below 5.5 mmol/L and ECG back to normal. | Continuous ECG monitoring. Give calcium gluconate, insulin with glucose and any other treatment as prescribed. Cut potassium in his diet. Recheck potassium as ordered. | K⁺ normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Uremia Has a central line and a urinary catheter | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: uremia and invasive devices. | No infection. | Aseptic care of the lines and the catheter. Watch temperature and WBC. Take devices out as soon as they are not needed. | No infection: goal met. |

## 7.6  Chronic Kidney Disease

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My legs swell and I gain weight easily." **Objective** eGFR under 30 mL/min Edema BP 150/95 mmHg | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to impaired renal function as evidenced by edema, weight gain and hypertension. | Keeps his weight gain within the prescribed limits. | Teach him to weigh himself every day. Teach his fluid and sodium limits. Give antihypertensives and diuretics as prescribed. Follow electrolytes and creatinine. | Weight within limits: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I do not know which foods to avoid." **Objective** Potassium and phosphate high Eats dates and bananas every day | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to renal diet as evidenced by questions and abnormal potassium and phosphate. | Plans a renal diet with the dietitian. | Teach which foods are high in potassium (bananas, dates, tomatoes) and in phosphate. Teach him to take phosphate binders with meals. Refer to the dietitian. | Plans his diet correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Anemia from CKD Uremia | **RISK** Risk for activity intolerance as evidenced by risk factors: anemia and uremia. | Keeps up his daily activities. | Follow the Hb. Give erythropoietin and iron as prescribed. Plan rest periods. | Activity kept up: goal met. |

## 7.7  Hemodialysis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel dizzy and cramp during dialysis." **Objective** BP drops to 90/55 mmHg during the session Cramps in the muscles | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to rapid fluid removal during dialysis as evidenced by hypotension and cramps. | Gets through dialysis with a stable BP. | Weigh before and after dialysis. BP every 30 minutes during the session. Report low BP and turn down the ultrafiltration as the protocol says. Teach him to limit how much fluid he puts on between sessions. | Session stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I drink a lot because I am thirsty." **Objective** Puts on 3.5 kg between sessions Ankles swollen | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to difficulty following fluid limit as evidenced by excessive weight gain between sessions. | Weight gain between sessions stays within the prescribed limit. | Teach ways to handle thirst: ice chips, sour sweets, small cups. Cut down salt. Look over his weight records with him. | Within limit: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Vascular access Needled again and again | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: vascular access and immunosuppression from uremia. | Access site stays free of infection. | Aseptic technique for every connection. Look at the access site at every session. Teach him to keep the access clean and report any redness. | No infection: goal met. |

## 7.8  Peritoneal Dialysis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The fluid coming out looks cloudy." **Objective** Effluent cloudy Abdominal pain Fever | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to peritoneal infection (peritonitis) as evidenced by abdominal pain and cloudy effluent. | Within 72 hours of treatment, effluent clear and no pain. | Send the effluent for cell count and culture. Give intraperitoneal antibiotics as prescribed. Record each exchange and what the fluid looks like. | Effluent clear: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "How do I do the exchange at home?" **Objective** Just started peritoneal dialysis Cannot explain the steps of an exchange | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to home dialysis procedure as evidenced by questions. | Does an exchange using aseptic technique. | Teach hand hygiene, wearing a mask and making an aseptic connection. Teach exit-site care. Teach her to record weight, BP and fluid balance. | Shows you correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Peritoneal catheter Exit site a little red | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: indwelling peritoneal catheter. | Exit site stays free of infection. | Exit-site care every day. Teach the signs of exit-site infection and of peritonitis. Hand hygiene before every exchange. | No infection: goal met. |

## 7.9  Arteriovenous Fistula Care

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "What should I do to protect my fistula?" **Objective** New AV fistula, left arm Wears a tight watch on that arm | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to fistula care as evidenced by questions. | Can describe how to protect the fistula. | Teach him to feel for the thrill every day. No BP readings, blood tests or tight clothing on that arm. He should not sleep on that arm or carry heavy bags with it. | Describes the measures: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives pain at the site 4/10 **Objective** Swollen after surgery Guards the arm | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgical creation of fistula as evidenced by reported pain. | Pain 3/10 or less. | Rest the arm up on a pillow. Give analgesia as prescribed. Check the thrill and the circulation in the hand. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Vein now arterialised Needled again and again | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: arterialized vein and needling. | No bleeding or clotting in the fistula. | Teach him to press firmly once the needle is out. Tell him to report if the thrill is gone or if it bleeds. Check the thrill and bruit every shift. | No bleeding: goal met. |

## 7.10  Kidney Transplant

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "How long will I take these medicines?" **Objective** Started on immunosuppressants Cannot name his drugs | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to lifelong immunosuppression as evidenced by questions. | Can name each drug, say when to take it and why it matters. | Teach him never to miss or stop his immunosuppressants. Explain why drug levels are checked. Teach the signs of rejection: fever, pain over the graft, less urine, weight gain. | Explains his regimen: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Is it normal that my urine changes?" **Objective** Urine output up and down Creatinine being followed | **ACTUAL** Impaired urinary elimination related to new graft function as evidenced by variable urine output. | Urine output adequate and creatinine coming down. | In the early period, record urine output every hour. Give fluids as prescribed. Report any sudden drop in output. | Output adequate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Immunosuppressed Transplanted recently | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: immunosuppressive therapy. | No infection. | Teach hand hygiene. Keep away from people who are ill. Teach him to report fever. | No infection: goal met. |

## 7.11  Benign Prostatic Hyperplasia and TURP

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I wake up 4 times at night to pass urine and the stream is weak." **Objective** Stream weak, slow to start Scan shows residual urine | **ACTUAL** Impaired urinary elimination related to bladder outlet obstruction as evidenced by hesitancy, weak stream and nocturia. | Empties his bladder properly after treatment. | Chart his voiding pattern. Give the alpha-blocker as prescribed. Get him ready for TURP as ordered. | Voiding better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Bladder spasms since the operation **Objective** Three-way catheter with continuous irrigation Clots in the drainage | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to bladder spasms after TURP as evidenced by reported spasms. | Says the spasms have eased. | Run continuous bladder irrigation fast enough to keep the urine light pink. Check the catheter is draining; if clots block it, irrigate as ordered. Give the antispasmodic as prescribed. Work out the true urine output (output minus irrigation). | Spasms gone, catheter draining: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Prostate resected Drainage light pink | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: prostatic surgery. | No significant bleeding. | Watch the colour of the drainage. Follow vital signs and Hb. He should not strain. | No bleeding: goal met. |

## 7.12  Bladder Cancer and Urinary Diversion

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I saw blood in my urine but felt no pain." "Is it cancer?" **Objective** Blood in the urine with no pain Has smoked for 30 years Waiting for the cystoscopy results | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to possible cancer diagnosis and treatment as evidenced by worry and repeated questions. | Before treatment, shares his worries and says he is less anxious. | Give him time to talk about his fears. Give clear information together with the medical team. Help him stop smoking. Bring in the family as he wishes. | Says he feels informed and calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "How do I look after the urostomy?" **Objective** New ileal conduit after cystectomy Urine flows from the stoma all the time | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to urostomy care as evidenced by questions and inability to describe care. | Before discharge, changes the appliance and connects the night drainage bag himself. | Teach him to measure the stoma and change the appliance. Show him how to connect a night drainage bag. Teach him to drink 2–2.5 L/day. Explain that mucus in the urine is normal with an ileal conduit. Tell him to report a dark stoma, fever or flank pain. | Shows you correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Urinary diversion Ureteric stents in place | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: urinary diversion and stents. | No UTI. | Aseptic technique with the stents and drainage. Watch his temperature and how the urine looks. Encourage fluids. | No infection: goal met. |

## 7.13  Urinary Incontinence

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I leak urine when I cough, sneeze or laugh." **Objective** Stress incontinence Has had several children BMI 31 kg/m² | **ACTUAL** Stress urinary incontinence related to weak pelvic floor muscles as evidenced by leakage with increased abdominal pressure. | Within 6–12 weeks, leaks less often. | Teach pelvic floor exercises and check she is doing them right. She should practise every day and keep going long term. Advise weight loss, and treating constipation and any chronic cough. Ask her to keep a bladder diary. If there is no improvement, refer her to a continence specialist. | Fewer episodes: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I stopped going to family visits because of the smell." **Objective** Avoids going out Uses a lot of pads | **ACTUAL** Social isolation related to fear of leakage and odour as evidenced by avoiding social activities. | Gets back to at least one social activity. | Talk about which continence products would suit her. Plan a toilet visit before she goes out. Let her talk about how she feels. | Back to an activity: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Skin always damp Wears pads | **RISK** Risk for impaired skin integrity as evidenced by risk factors: moisture from urine. | Perineal skin stays intact. | Teach her to wash with a mild cleanser and dry the skin after each episode. Use barrier cream. Change pads quickly. | Skin intact: goal met. |

## 7.14  Urinary Retention

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I need to pass urine but nothing comes." **Objective** Bladder distended and dull to percussion Bladder scan 700 mL Taking an anticholinergic drug | **ACTUAL** Urinary retention related to bladder outlet obstruction or medication effect as evidenced by distended bladder and inability to void. | Within 24 hours the bladder is emptied and he passes urine on his own. | Try things that help him go: privacy, running water, standing or sitting up. Catheterise as ordered and record how much drains. Go through anticholinergic and opioid drugs with the physician. Do a trial without catheter as ordered and scan for residual. | Voids on his own with a small residual: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives lower abdominal discomfort 7/10 **Objective** Restless Sweaty | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to bladder distension as evidenced by discomfort and restlessness. | Says he feels relief once drained. | Drain the bladder as ordered. Check pain again after drainage. After a large volume drains, watch for blood in the urine. | Relieved: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Catheterised Urine left in the bladder | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: urinary catheter and urinary stasis. | No UTI. | Aseptic technique for insertion. Keep the drainage system closed. Take the catheter out as early as possible. | No infection: goal met. |

## 7.15  Nephrectomy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The wound in my side hurts a lot." Gives the pain 8/10 **Objective** Incision in the flank Guarding HR 104/min | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to flank incision as evidenced by pain score 8/10 and guarding. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Give analgesia regularly (epidural or PCA as ordered). Make him comfortable with pillows for support. Support the incision when he coughs. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "It hurts to take a deep breath." **Objective** Breathing shallow Breath sounds reduced at the base on the operated side | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to incision close to the diaphragm as evidenced by shallow breathing and reduced breath sounds. | Breath sounds clear within 48 hours. | While awake, encourage deep breathing and the incentive spirometer every hour. Get him moving early. Listen to the lungs every 4 hours. | Breath sounds clear: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** A very vascular organ has been removed Only one kidney left | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: surgery on a highly vascular organ. | Vital signs stable and urine output at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour. | Follow vital signs, drain output and hourly urine output. Follow Hb and creatinine. Teach him to avoid drugs that harm the kidneys, such as NSAIDs, in future unless advised. | Stable: goal met. |

**PART 8**

# Endocrine Care Plans

## 8.1  Type 1 Diabetes Mellitus

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I do not know how to inject insulin." Just diagnosed **Objective** Blood glucose 280 mg/dL HbA1c 10% Cannot explain how insulin works | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to new diagnosis and insulin therapy as evidenced by inability to describe or perform insulin injection. | Before discharge, tests her own glucose and injects insulin correctly. | Teach her to use the glucose meter and keep a record. Teach the insulin types, when to give them, and injection technique with site rotation. Teach how to store insulin. Watch her do it herself. Teach the sick-day rules: never stop insulin, and check glucose and ketones more often. | Does it correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My glucose is sometimes very high, sometimes low." **Objective** Glucose 60–320 mg/dL over 3 days | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to difficulty matching insulin, food and activity as evidenced by wide variation in blood glucose. | Within 2 weeks, glucose on target for most of the day. | Go through her glucose log with her. Teach her about carbohydrates and the need for regular meals. Teach her to adjust food or insulin before exercise, as the diabetes team advises. She should check her glucose before driving and at bedtime. | Most readings on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Sometimes I shake and sweat before lunch." **Objective** On insulin Eats at irregular times | **RISK** Risk for unstable blood glucose level as evidenced by risk factors: insulin therapy and irregular food intake. | Recognises a hypo and treats it correctly. | Teach the signs of hypoglycemia. Teach the treatment: 15 g of fast-acting glucose, then recheck after 15 minutes. She should always carry glucose tablets and identification. | Describes the right treatment: goal met. |

## 8.2  Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I eat rice and bread at every meal." "I do not exercise." **Objective** HbA1c 9% BMI 33 kg/m² Fasting glucose 190 mg/dL | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to insufficient knowledge of diet and activity as evidenced by high HbA1c and unhealthy lifestyle. | Can describe a meal plan and an activity goal; HbA1c lower at the next review. | Teach balanced meals, portion control and less refined carbohydrate. As he can manage, aim for 150 minutes of moderate activity a week. Teach him when to take his medicines. Refer to the dietitian and the diabetes educator. | Describes his plan and glucose is improving: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I do not check my feet." **Objective** Monofilament test shows reduced sensation Calluses on both feet | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to foot care as evidenced by not inspecting feet and reduced sensation. | Shows you how he checks his feet each day. | Teach him to look at his feet every day, using a mirror. Teach washing, drying between the toes, and moisturising (but not between the toes). Advise proper footwear and never going barefoot. Book a foot review once a year. | Shows you: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Hyperglycemia Hypertensive Smokes | **RISK** Risk for ineffective peripheral tissue perfusion as evidenced by risk factors: diabetes, hypertension and smoking. | Commits to reducing his risk. | Follow BP and lipids. Help him stop smoking. Encourage him to take his medicines as prescribed. | Describes his plan: goal met. |

## 8.3  Diabetic Ketoacidosis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I am very thirsty and have been vomiting." **Objective** Glucose 480 mg/dL Ketones positive, pH 7.15 Kussmaul breathing Mouth dry, HR 120/min | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to osmotic diuresis and vomiting as evidenced by dry mucosa, tachycardia and hyperglycemia. | Within 6–8 hours, vital signs stable and urine output adequate. | Give IV fluids as the DKA protocol prescribes. Record urine output every hour, and I&O. Vital signs and level of consciousness every hour. Glucose every hour, electrolytes as ordered. | Hydrated and stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel I cannot catch my breath." **Objective** Breathing deep and fast (Kussmaul) pH 7.15, HCO₃⁻ 10 mmol/L Breath smells fruity | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to compensatory response to metabolic acidosis as evidenced by Kussmaul respirations and low pH. | Within 24 hours, breathing pattern back to normal as the acidosis is corrected. | Watch rate and depth of breathing and blood pH as ordered. Give the insulin infusion and fluids according to the protocol. Check potassium before and during insulin therapy and replace it as prescribed. Watch the ECG for signs of a potassium problem. | Breathing and pH normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On an insulin infusion | **RISK** Risk for unstable blood glucose level as evidenced by risk factors: IV insulin infusion. | Glucose comes down gradually, without a hypo. | Check glucose every hour. When glucose falls to the protocol level, add dextrose to the fluids. Keep the insulin infusion running until ketones have cleared and subcutaneous insulin has been given. | No hypoglycemia: goal met. |

## 8.4  Hyperosmolar Hyperglycemic State

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Family say she has been drowsy for 2 days **Objective** Glucose 900 mg/dL Serum osmolality very high Hardly any ketones Mouth dry | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to severe osmotic diuresis as evidenced by dehydration and high osmolality. | Fluid deficit and glucose corrected gradually over 24–48 hours. | Give IV fluids as prescribed; this is corrected more slowly than DKA. Follow sodium and osmolality. Record urine output every hour. Watch neurological status. | Better hydrated and more alert: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family say "She does not recognize us." **Objective** Confused GCS 12 | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to hyperosmolality as evidenced by drowsiness and confusion. | Back to her usual orientation as the osmolality corrects. | GCS every hour. Keep her safe: bed low, side rails as policy allows. Reorient her often. | Oriented: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Dehydrated Immobile Elderly | **RISK** Risk for thrombosis as evidenced by risk factors: hyperviscosity and immobility. | No VTE. | Give the prophylactic anticoagulant as ordered. Get her moving as soon as she can. Check the calves every day. | No VTE: goal met. |

## 8.5  Hypoglycemia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel shaky, sweaty and hungry." **Objective** Glucose 52 mg/dL Shaking and sweating Cannot concentrate HR 110/min | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to low blood glucose as evidenced by glucose 52 mg/dL, tremor, sweating and difficulty concentrating. | Glucose above 70 mg/dL within 15 minutes. | If he is conscious: give 15–20 g of fast-acting glucose, recheck in 15 minutes and repeat if needed. After that, give a carbohydrate snack or his next meal. If he is unconscious: IV dextrose or IM glucagon as prescribed, and nothing by mouth. Work out why it happened. | Glucose normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I did not eat lunch but took my insulin." **Objective** Skipped a meal Cannot say how to prevent a hypo | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to matching food and insulin as evidenced by taking insulin without eating. | Can explain how to prevent hypoglycemia. | Teach him to time his meals with his insulin. Tell him to carry glucose with him. | Explains prevention: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has hypos again and again Drives to work | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: hypoglycemic episodes with confusion. | No injury from hypoglycemia. | He should check his glucose before he drives. Teach the family to spot and treat a hypo, including using glucagon if it is prescribed. He should wear diabetes identification. | No injury: goal met. |

## 8.6  Diabetic Foot Ulcer

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I did not feel the wound until I saw blood on my sock." **Objective** 2 × 2 cm ulcer on the sole Sensation reduced Weak pedal pulses | **ACTUAL** Impaired tissue integrity related to neuropathy and poor circulation as evidenced by ulcer on the sole. | Within 2 weeks the ulcer shows signs of healing. | Measure the wound and record it every week. Do wound care as prescribed. Take the pressure off with special footwear or no weight-bearing, as ordered. Keep blood glucose under control. | Wound smaller: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot walk normally." **Objective** Ordered non-weight-bearing Wobbly on crutches | **ACTUAL** Impaired walking related to offloading requirement as evidenced by prescribed restriction. | Moves about safely with aids, without putting weight on the ulcer. | Give him crutches, a walker or a wheelchair. Teach him to use them safely. Check whether he is at risk of falling at home. | Moving safely: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Open wound Glucose above target | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: open wound and hyperglycemia. | Infection does not spread. | Watch for redness, swelling, smell and fever. Send a wound swab as ordered. Keep blood glucose under control. | No infection: goal met. |

## 8.7  Hyperthyroidism and Thyroidectomy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel hot and nervous and lose weight even though I eat." **Objective** HR 118/min Shaky Lost 5 kg TSH low, T4 high | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to increased metabolic rate as evidenced by weight loss despite good intake. | Within 4 weeks of treatment, weight steady. | Give high-calorie, high-protein meals. Give antithyroid drugs and the beta-blocker as prescribed. Weigh once a week. | Weight steady: goal met. |
| **Subjective** After the thyroidectomy: "It hurts to swallow." **Objective** Incision on the neck Voice hoarse | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to neck incision as evidenced by pain on swallowing. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Sit her semi-Fowler with pillows supporting her neck. Give analgesia as prescribed. Offer cool, soft food. At first, check her voice every hour and report hoarseness that is getting worse. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Neck surgery close to the parathyroids and the airway | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: neck hematoma, laryngeal nerve damage and hypocalcemia after thyroidectomy. | Airway clear and calcium normal. | Keep a tracheostomy set at the bedside. Check the dressing and the back of the neck for bleeding. Report tingling around the mouth or in the fingers (hypocalcemia). Follow calcium. | No complications: goal met. |

## 8.8  Thyroid Storm

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Temperature 40 °C HR 160/min Agitated | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to excessive thyroid hormone as evidenced by temperature 40 °C. | Temperature below 38.5 °C within 6 hours. | Start cooling. Give paracetamol as ordered (not aspirin). Give the antithyroid drug, iodine, beta-blocker and steroid as prescribed. | Temperature coming down: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** HR 160/min Atrial fibrillation | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to tachydysrhythmia as evidenced by HR 160/min and irregular rhythm. | HR under 100/min within 6 hours. | Continuous ECG monitoring. Give the beta-blocker as prescribed. Watch BP and look for signs of heart failure. | Rate controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Agitated Confused | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: agitation and delirium. | No injury. | Keep the room calm and cool. Keep her under close observation. Bed low, side rails as policy allows. | No injury: goal met. |

## 8.9  Hypothyroidism

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel cold, tired and slow." **Objective** TSH high, T4 low Putting on weight Skin dry | **ACTUAL** Fatigue related to decreased metabolic rate as evidenced by tiredness and slowness. | Within weeks of treatment, more energy. | Give levothyroxine in the morning on an empty stomach. Explain that treatment is lifelong and TSH needs checking regularly. Plan rest. | More energy: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I have not opened my bowels for 4 days." **Objective** Bowel sounds reduced Stools hard | **ACTUAL** Constipation related to slowed GI motility as evidenced by infrequent stools. | Within 3 days, soft bowel movements regularly. | Encourage fluids and fibre. Encourage activity as she can manage. Give the laxative as prescribed. | Regular: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** HR 52/min Elderly | **RISK** Risk for decreased cardiac output as evidenced by risk factors: bradycardia and myxedema. | HR and BP stable. | No sedatives. In older or cardiac patients, start thyroxine at a low dose as ordered. Report chest pain. | Stable: goal met. |

## 8.10  Cushing's Syndrome

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My face is round and I bruise easily." **Objective** Round "moon" face Fat around the trunk Skin thin Glucose up | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to changes in appearance as evidenced by distress about face and body. | Talks about her feelings and understands the changes can be treated. | Encourage her to talk. Explain how the changes should improve with treatment. | Shows acceptance: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My ankles are swollen." **Objective** Edema Potassium low | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to sodium retention as evidenced by edema. | Within 1 week, less edema. | Weigh every day. Encourage a low-sodium diet. Follow potassium. | Less edema: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Cortisol high Skin thin, slow to heal | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: immunosuppression from excess cortisol. | No infection. | Teach hand hygiene. Look for subtle signs of infection (there may be no fever). Protect her skin from injury. | No infection: goal met. |

## 8.11  Addison's Disease and Adrenal Crisis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel weak and dizzy, and vomit." **Objective** BP 80/50 mmHg Na⁺ 128 mmol/L, K⁺ 5.8 mmol/L Skin darker than usual | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to aldosterone and cortisol deficiency as evidenced by hypotension and hyponatremia. | BP stable within 6 hours. | Give IV hydrocortisone straight away, as prescribed. Give IV saline and dextrose as ordered. Follow vital signs, glucose and electrolytes. | BP stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Why must I double my tablets when sick?" **Objective** Just diagnosed Cannot explain the sick-day rules | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to steroid replacement as evidenced by questions. | Can explain the sick-day rules. | Teach him never to stop his steroids. Teach him to raise the dose on sick days as instructed. He should carry a steroid card and an emergency injection. | Explains the rules: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Cortisol deficiency Glucose 62 mg/dL on arrival | **RISK** Risk for unstable blood glucose level as evidenced by risk factors: cortisol deficiency. | Blood glucose stays within the normal range. | Check glucose. Give dextrose as ordered. Give hydrocortisone on time. | Normal: goal met. |

## 8.12  Syndrome of Inappropriate ADH (SIADH)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Family say he is confused **Objective** Na⁺ 122 mmol/L Urine concentrated Gaining weight with no edema | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to water retention as evidenced by hyponatremia and weight gain. | Sodium comes back up to normal slowly. | Restrict fluids as prescribed. Daily weight and a strict I&O chart. Correct the sodium slowly, as ordered. | Sodium rising: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family say "He is confused." **Objective** Disoriented | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to hyponatremia as evidenced by disorientation. | Oriented as his sodium corrects. | Neuro checks every 4 hours. Reorient him often. Report any seizure. | Oriented: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Sodium very low Confused | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: seizures and falls from low sodium. | No injury. | Seizure precautions in place. Fall precautions in place. Bed kept low. | No injury: goal met. |

## 8.13  Diabetes Insipidus

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I drink and pass urine all the time." **Objective** Passing 6 L of urine a day Urine specific gravity low Na⁺ 150 mmol/L | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to excessive dilute urine output as evidenced by polyuria and hypernatremia. | I&O balanced and sodium normal. | Record urine output and specific gravity every hour. Give desmopressin as prescribed. Replace fluids as ordered. Weigh daily. | Balanced: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot sleep because I go to the toilet." **Objective** Up at night to pass urine Tired in the day | **ACTUAL** Disturbed sleep pattern related to nocturia as evidenced by waking to void. | Within 1 week of treatment, says he sleeps better. | Give desmopressin at the prescribed time (often at night). Keep a urinal by the bed. Only cut down evening fluids if he is advised to. | Sleeping better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On desmopressin Headache | **RISK** Risk for electrolyte imbalance as evidenced by risk factors: desmopressin causing water retention. | Serum sodium stays within the normal range. | Follow sodium. Teach him to drink only when thirsty. Tell him to report headache, nausea or confusion. | Normal: goal met. |

## 8.14  Pituitary Surgery (Transsphenoidal)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My nose is packed and I breathe through my mouth." **Objective** Nose packed Mouth dry | **ACTUAL** Impaired oral mucous membrane integrity related to mouth breathing as evidenced by dry mouth. | Mouth moist again within 24 hours. | Give mouth care often. Use humidified air. Offer sips of water. | Moist: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Can I blow my nose?" "Is it normal to have drips from my nose?" **Objective** Cannot say what precautions to take after surgery Nasal packing in situ | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to precautions after transsphenoidal surgery as evidenced by questions and inability to state precautions. | Before discharge, can describe how to prevent a CSF leak. | Test any drainage for glucose, following policy. Teach her: no nose blowing, no sneezing with the mouth shut, no bending forward. Clear drainage from the nose or a headache must be reported. | No leak: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has had pituitary surgery Urine output going up | **RISK** Risk for deficient fluid volume as evidenced by risk factors: diabetes insipidus after surgery. | I&O balanced and sodium normal. | Record urine output every hour. Report output above the physician's limit. Follow sodium and urine specific gravity. | Balanced: goal met. |

**PART 9**

# Musculoskeletal Care Plans

## 9.1  Fractures

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My leg hurts badly when anyone touches it." Gives the pain 8/10 **Objective** Limb swollen and out of shape X-ray shows a closed fracture of the tibia Muscles in spasm | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to bone fracture, soft-tissue injury and muscle spasm as evidenced by pain score 8/10 and guarding. | Pain 3/10 or less within 1 hour of the intervention. | Before any movement, splint the limb as ordered. Unless there is a reason not to, raise the limb above heart level. Cold packs for the first 24–48 hours, as ordered. Give analgesia as prescribed and check again. When you move the limb, support it above and below the fracture. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot walk or go to the toilet alone." **Objective** Ordered non-weight-bearing Affected limb moves very little | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to fracture and prescribed immobilization as evidenced by inability to bear weight. | Within 3 days, gets about safely on crutches or a walker. | Teach crutch walking together with the physiotherapist. Encourage active exercise of the limbs that are not injured. As allowed, teach isometric exercises for the injured limb. Help with ADL as needed. | Moving about safely: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My toes feel tight and tingling." **Objective** Swelling under the cast or splint Closed fracture of the lower leg | **RISK** Risk for peripheral neurovascular dysfunction as evidenced by risk factors: fracture, swelling and immobilization device. | Limb stays warm and pink, with normal sensation, movement and capillary refill. | Check the 5 Ps (pain, pallor, pulselessness, paresthesia, paralysis) every hour for the first 24 hours, then as ordered. Pain out of proportion to the injury, or pain on passive stretch, must be reported at once. Keep the limb raised and loosen the bandages if ordered. | Neurovascular status normal: goal met. |

## 9.2  Cast Care

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The cast itches and I want to scratch inside it." **Objective** New plaster cast on the forearm Trying to push things down inside the cast | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to cast care as evidenced by unsafe behaviour and questions. | Before discharge, can tell you how to look after the cast. | Teach him never to push anything inside the cast. Teach him to keep the cast dry and give the plaster time to dry fully (24–48 hours). Teach him to keep the arm raised and keep moving his fingers. Teach the warning signs: pain getting worse, numbness, colour change, a bad smell or discharge. | Repeats the instructions: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "The edge of the cast rubs my skin." **Objective** Edges of the cast rough Skin red at the edge of the cast | **ACTUAL** Impaired comfort related to cast pressure and itching as evidenced by reported discomfort and redness at edges. | Within 24 hours, says it feels more comfortable. | Pad or petal the rough edges. For itching, blow cool air from a fan into the cast. Check the skin at the cast edges every shift. | Comfortable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Cast is rigid Limb swollen after the injury | **RISK** Risk for peripheral neurovascular dysfunction as evidenced by risk factors: constrictive cast and swelling. | Limb stays warm and pink, with normal sensation and movement. | Check circulation, sensation and movement every hour at first, then as ordered. Keep the limb raised. Report signs of compression and be ready to split the cast as ordered. | Normal: goal met. |

## 9.3  Traction

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have been lying in the same position for days." **Objective** Skeletal traction on the femur Cannot move much | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to traction as evidenced by restricted movement. | Muscle strength and joint function kept in the limbs that are not in traction. | Weights must hang freely, with the ropes running in the pulley tracks. Never take off or lift the weights unless it is ordered. Keep his body aligned against the line of pull. Encourage exercise of the free limbs. | Traction working and joints mobile: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "The pin sites are sore." **Objective** Skeletal pins A little serous ooze | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to traction pins and fracture as evidenced by reported soreness. | Pain 3/10 or less. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Make him comfortable within the limits of the traction. Be gentle when doing pin-site care. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Skeletal pins | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: skeletal pins penetrating skin and bone. | No infection at the pin sites. | Pin-site care as policy says. Look for redness, pus and loose pins. Watch his temperature. | Pin sites clean: goal met. |

## 9.4  External Fixator

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I do not like how my leg looks with the frame." **Objective** External fixator on the tibia Will not look at his leg | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to external fixator as evidenced by avoiding looking at the leg. | Looks at the leg and helps with pin care. | Explain what the frame is for and roughly how long it will stay on. Encourage him to help with his care. Suggest loose clothes to cover the frame. | Helps with his care: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "How do I clean the pins at home?" **Objective** Going home with the frame on Cannot explain how to care for the pins | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to pin-site care as evidenced by questions. | Shows you how he cleans the pin sites. | Teach pin-site cleaning according to the protocol. Teach the signs of infection. Tell him never to adjust the clamps himself. | Shows you correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Several pins Serous ooze at two sites | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: pins through skin and bone. | Pin sites stay free of infection. | Look at the pins every day. Pin-site care according to the protocol. Watch his temperature. | No infection: goal met. |

## 9.5  Hip Fracture and Internal Fixation

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My hip hurts after I fell." Gives the pain 8/10 **Objective** Leg shortened and turned outwards Age 80 years Day 1 after fixation | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to fracture and surgery as evidenced by pain score 8/10. | Pain 3/10 or less and joins in with mobilisation. | Give analgesia regularly; consider a nerve block as ordered. Put a pillow between her legs as ordered. Give analgesia before physiotherapy. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Confused at night **Objective** Newly disoriented Elderly, on opioids | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to surgery, pain and medication as evidenced by new disorientation. | Back to her usual orientation. | Screen for delirium every day. Reorient her and bring the family in. Treat pain, dehydration and constipation. | Oriented: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Elderly Immobile Had hip surgery | **RISK** Risk for adult pressure injury as evidenced by risk factors: older age, immobility and surgery. | Skin stays intact. | Turn every 2 hours, as far as allowed. Nurse her on a pressure-relieving mattress. Check heels and sacrum every shift. Get her up early, as ordered. | Skin intact: goal met. |

## 9.6  Total Hip Replacement

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Gives the pain 6/10 **Objective** Incision over the hip Drain in situ | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgical trauma as evidenced by pain score 6/10. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Give analgesia regularly. Cold packs as ordered. Give analgesia before exercise. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "How should I sit and sleep?" **Objective** New prosthesis Crosses his legs in bed | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to hip precautions as evidenced by questions. | Can describe the hip precautions and sticks to them. | Teach the precautions the surgeon orders (for a posterior approach: no bending the hip beyond 90°, no crossing the legs, no turning the leg inwards). Use a raised toilet seat and a high chair. Show him how to use dressing aids. | Follows the precautions: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Had major orthopaedic surgery Moving less | **RISK** Risk for thrombosis as evidenced by risk factors: hip replacement and reduced mobility. | No DVT. | Give the anticoagulant as prescribed. Every hour, encourage ankle exercises. Get him moving early. Check the calves every day. | No DVT: goal met. |

## 9.7  Total Knee Replacement

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Gives the pain 7/10 when bending the knee **Objective** Knee swollen Cannot bend the knee far | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgery as evidenced by pain on movement. | Pain low enough to do her knee exercises (4/10 or less). | Give analgesia regularly, especially before physiotherapy. Cold therapy as ordered. Raise the leg, but do not put a pillow under the knee. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "It is hard to bend my knee." **Objective** Knee bends to 60° | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to pain and stiffness as evidenced by limited knee flexion. | Knee bends to 90° before discharge, or to the physiotherapist's target. | Encourage knee exercises, and continuous passive motion if ordered. Walk with an aid as instructed. Record how far the knee bends every day. | Target reached: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Joint prosthesis in place Wound dressing on | **RISK** Risk for surgical site infection as evidenced by risk factors: joint prosthesis. | Wound heals without infection. | Aseptic wound care. Watch her temperature. Tell her to report redness, swelling or discharge. | No infection: goal met. |

## 9.8  Amputation

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I can still feel my foot even though it is gone." **Objective** Below-knee amputation Phantom limb pain | **ACTUAL** Chronic pain related to phantom limb sensation as evidenced by pain in the absent limb. | Says the phantom pain is less. | Tell him phantom pain is real. Give analgesia and neuropathic agents as prescribed. Encourage massaging the stump and desensitisation. | Less pain: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am not a whole person now." **Objective** Will not look at the stump Tearful | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to loss of limb as evidenced by avoidance and distress. | Looks at the stump and talks about rehabilitation. | Let him grieve and talk about it. Bring him into the care of his stump. Arrange for him to meet another amputee or a prosthetist. | Engages with rehabilitation: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Stump | **RISK** Risk for disuse syndrome as evidenced by risk factors: amputation, pain and prolonged flexed positioning of the stump. | Stump keeps its full extension. | After the first day, no pillows under the stump, as ordered. If allowed, lie prone for 20–30 minutes several times a day. Do the exercises as taught. | No contracture: goal met. |

## 9.9  Osteoporosis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have back pain and I am getting shorter." **Objective** Has lost height Bone density low | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to disease management as evidenced by questions. | Can describe her diet, exercise and medication plan. | Teach calcium and vitamin D intake. Teach weight-bearing exercise. Teach how to take the bisphosphonate (empty stomach, stay upright for 30 minutes). | Describes her plan: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Back pain, 5/10 **Objective** Fractured vertebra Spine curved forward (kyphosis) | **ACTUAL** Chronic pain related to vertebral fractures as evidenced by back pain. | Pain at a level that lets her manage her day. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Refer to physiotherapy. Teach safe posture and lifting. | Manageable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Bones fragile Elderly | **RISK** Risk for adult falls as evidenced by risk factors: older age and fragile bones. | No falls. | Give advice on safety at home. Take up rugs and use night lights. Encourage balance exercises. | No falls: goal met. |

## 9.10  Osteoarthritis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My knees hurt and are stiff in the morning for a short time." **Objective** Grating in the joint (crepitus) Joints enlarged | **ACTUAL** Chronic pain related to joint degeneration as evidenced by knee pain and stiffness. | Pain at a level that lets him manage his day. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Teach weight loss. Teach heat or cold therapy. Encourage low-impact exercise. | Manageable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot climb stairs because of my knees." **Objective** Struggles on the stairs | **ACTUAL** Impaired walking related to joint pain as evidenced by difficulty with stairs. | Walks safely with an aid. | Give him a cane to hold on the good side. Refer to physiotherapy. If he is overweight, encourage weight loss. | Walking safely: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Joints painful Elderly | **RISK** Risk for adult falls as evidenced by risk factors: impaired mobility. | No falls. | Give fall prevention advice. Encourage strengthening exercises. Check his footwear. | No falls: goal met. |
