| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My hand joints are swollen and stiff for hours in the morning." **Objective** Same joints swollen on both sides ESR raised Stiff for more than 1 hour in the morning | **ACTUAL** Chronic pain related to joint inflammation as evidenced by swollen painful joints. | Says pain and stiffness are less. | Give DMARDs and anti-inflammatory drugs as prescribed. A warm shower in the morning helps. Balance rest with exercise. | Less pain: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot button my shirt." **Objective** Hands deformed Takes 30 minutes to get dressed | **ACTUAL** Dressing self-care deficit related to joint deformity as evidenced by inability to fasten buttons. | Gets dressed using adaptive aids. | Refer to occupational therapy. Suggest Velcro fastenings and adapted clothes. | Dresses herself: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On immunosuppressive drugs WBC low | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: immunosuppressive therapy. | No infection. | Follow blood counts as ordered. Teach her to report fever. Check her vaccinations. | No infection: goal met. |

## 9.12  Gout

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My big toe is red, hot and very painful." **Objective** First metatarsophalangeal joint swollen Uric acid raised | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to urate crystal inflammation as evidenced by severe joint pain and swelling. | Pain relieved within 24–48 hours. | Rest the joint and raise it. Give an anti-inflammatory or colchicine as prescribed. Use a bed cradle to keep the sheets off the joint. | Pain relieved: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I eat a lot of red meat." **Objective** Attacks keep coming back Diet high in purines | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to prevention of attacks as evidenced by high-purine diet. | Can explain how to prevent attacks. | Teach him to cut down red meat, organ meat and sugary drinks. Encourage fluids. Explain that urate-lowering therapy is long term. | Explains the measures: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Uric acid high Stops his medicine once the pain settles | **RISK** Risk for ineffective health self-management as evidenced by risk factors: long-term medication and diet changes. | Keeps taking urate-lowering therapy as prescribed. | Explain why treatment is long term. Book follow-up checks of uric acid. Tell him not to stop his medicines during an attack. | Taking them as prescribed: goal met. |

Give analgesia as prescribed. Raise the limb. | Controlled: goal met. |

Check drug levels as ordered. | Afebrile: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Bone weakened Tibia infected | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: pathological fracture of infected bone. | No pathological fracture. | Handle the limb gently. Follow the weight-bearing limits. Give him walking aids. | No fracture: goal met. |

## 9.14  Compartment Syndrome

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The pain is getting worse even with injections." **Objective** Pain when the muscle is passively stretched Compartment tense and swollen Pins and needles | **ACTUAL** Ineffective peripheral tissue perfusion related to increased compartment pressure as evidenced by severe pain, tense swelling and paresthesia. | Circulation restored after fasciotomy. | Report this at once. Keep the limb level with the heart, not raised. Take off or loosen tight dressings or the cast, as ordered. Get him ready for fasciotomy. | Circulation restored: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Pain 10/10 **Objective** Opioids do not touch the pain Pain on passive stretch | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to ischemia as evidenced by pain out of proportion. | Pain relieved after decompression. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Check pain often. Pain that will not settle must be reported at once. | Relieved: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Fasciotomy wound Muscle exposed | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: open fasciotomy wounds. | Wounds stay free of infection. | Use sterile dressings. Watch his temperature and how the wound looks. Give antibiotics as prescribed. | No infection: goal met. |

## 9.15  Fat Embolism Syndrome

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I suddenly feel short of breath." **Objective** Broke a long bone 2 days ago SpO₂ 86% Petechiae across the chest Confused | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to fat emboli in the pulmonary circulation as evidenced by hypoxemia, dyspnea and petechiae. | SpO₂ 94% or more. | Give high-flow oxygen. Sit him upright. Call for urgent review and be ready for ventilation. Move the fractured limb as little as possible. | SpO₂ on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family say "He is confused." **Objective** Disoriented Restless | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to cerebral fat emboli and hypoxemia as evidenced by disorientation. | Oriented as his oxygen levels improve. | Do neuro checks. Put safety measures in place. Give oxygen to target. | Oriented: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Hypoxemic Heart rate fast | **RISK** Risk for decreased cardiac output as evidenced by risk factors: right heart strain. | Vital signs stable. | Continuous ECG and vital signs monitoring. Report low BP. Give oxygen. | Stable: goal met. |

Log-roll him whenever his position is changed. Check pain regularly. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "How do I get out of bed?" **Objective** Brace ordered Cannot explain how to get out of bed safely | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to spinal precautions as evidenced by questions. | Gets in and out of bed safely. | Teach the log-roll and how to get up from lying on his side. Teach him how to wear the brace. He must not bend, lift or twist. | Shows you: goal met. |

**PART 10**

# Hematology, Immunology and Oncology Care Plans

## 10.1  Iron-Deficiency Anemia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I get tired and breathless climbing stairs." **Objective** Hb 8.5 g/dL, ferritin low Conjunctiva pale HR 104/min on exertion | **ACTUAL** Activity intolerance related to reduced oxygen-carrying capacity as evidenced by exertional dyspnea, fatigue and low Hb. | Within 2 weeks of treatment, does her ADL without getting breathless. | Plan activities with rests in between. Check HR and SpO₂ while she is active. Give iron as prescribed; if IV iron is ordered, watch for reactions. Follow Hb and ferritin. | Copes with ADL: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I do not eat meat often." **Objective** Eats little iron Periods are heavy | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to dietary iron and oral iron therapy as evidenced by low-iron diet and questions. | Can name iron-rich foods and explain how to take her iron tablets. | Teach which foods are rich in iron: red meat, liver, legumes, dark green leaves, dates. Teach her to take iron with vitamin C, and not with tea, milk or antacids. Explain that dark stools and constipation are expected. Refer her to find out why she is losing blood. | Repeats the advice: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On oral iron Eats little fibre | **RISK** Risk for constipation as evidenced by risk factors: oral iron therapy. | While on iron, soft stools at least every 2–3 days. | Encourage fluids and fibre. Chart her bowel pattern. Give a stool softener if ordered. | Stools regular: goal met. |

## 10.2  Sickle Cell Crisis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The pain in my bones is unbearable." Gives the pain 10/10 **Objective** Known to have sickle cell disease Guards his limbs HR 120/min | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to vaso-occlusion and tissue ischemia as evidenced by severe bone pain. | Within 1 hour of analgesia, says the pain is bearable. | Give strong analgesia without delay, as prescribed (often opioids by protocol). Check pain again every 30–60 minutes. Put warmth on the painful areas; keep cold away. Believe him when he tells you about his pain. | Pain bearable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am thirsty." **Objective** Mouth dry Urine concentrated | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to inadequate intake and increased loss as evidenced by dry mucosa. | Well hydrated within 24 hours. | Give IV and oral fluids as prescribed. Keep an I&O chart. If SpO₂ is below target, give oxygen. | Hydrated: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I have chest pain and cough." **Objective** In sickle crisis Breathing shallow because of pain | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: functional asplenia and lung involvement (acute chest syndrome). | Chest syndrome and infection picked up early. | Watch SpO₂, RR and temperature. Encourage the incentive spirometer. Report chest pain, fever or a drop in SpO₂ at once. | No complications: goal met. |

## 10.3  Thalassemia in Adults

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I need blood every month and feel tired before it." **Objective** Hb 7.5 g/dL before the transfusion Pale | **ACTUAL** Fatigue related to chronic anemia as evidenced by tiredness before transfusion. | Says she has more energy after the transfusion. | Give the transfusion as prescribed, with all safety checks. Watch for transfusion reactions. Plan rest periods. | More energy: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I miss the iron chelation sometimes." **Objective** Ferritin high Records show missed doses | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to burden of daily chelation therapy as evidenced by missed doses and high ferritin. | Takes her chelation as prescribed. | Explain iron overload and what it does to the heart and liver. Work out a schedule that fits her life. Follow ferritin. | Taking it more regularly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has had many transfusions Spleen removed | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: splenectomy and repeated transfusions. | No serious infection. | Make sure her vaccinations are done. Teach her to report fever urgently. Give the prophylactic antibiotic if ordered. | No infection: goal met. |

## 10.4  Aplastic Anemia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel weak and get breathless quickly." **Objective** Hb 7 g/dL Pale HR 110/min on exertion | **ACTUAL** Activity intolerance related to reduced oxygen-carrying capacity as evidenced by weakness, exertional dyspnea and low Hb. | Within 1 week, manages self-care with rests and without breathlessness. | Plan care so he can rest between activities. Give the red cell transfusion as prescribed, with safety checks. Check HR and SpO₂ while he is active. Give the immunosuppressive therapy as prescribed and watch for side effects. | Copes with ADL: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My gums bleed when I brush." **Objective** Platelets 15 × 10⁹/L Petechiae on the legs Gums bleeding | **ACTUAL** Impaired oral mucous membrane integrity related to thrombocytopenia as evidenced by gum bleeding. | No active bleeding from the mouth within 48 hours. | Use a soft toothbrush or sponge swabs. No alcohol-based mouthwash. Give the platelet transfusion as prescribed. No IM injections and no rectal procedures. | No bleeding from the mouth: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Neutrophils 0.3 × 10⁹/L Central line in situ | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: severe neutropenia and invasive line. | No infection, and any fever treated within 1 hour. | Protective isolation as policy says. Strict hand hygiene. Temperature every 4 hours; treat any fever as neutropenic sepsis. Look at the line site every day. | No infection: goal met. |

## 10.5  Leukemia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have fever and feel very tired." **Objective** Neutrophils 0.4 × 10⁹/L Temperature 38.3 °C Having induction chemotherapy | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to infection during neutropenia as evidenced by temperature 38.3 °C and low neutrophil count. | Gets IV antibiotics within 1 hour of the fever and is afebrile within 72 hours. | Following the neutropenic sepsis protocol, take blood cultures and give IV antibiotics within 1 hour. Vital signs every 1–2 hours. Keep him in protective isolation. No rectal temperatures or rectal procedures. | Afebrile and stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My mouth is sore and I cannot eat." **Objective** Mouth ulcers Eating less Swallowing hurts | **ACTUAL** Impaired oral mucous membrane integrity related to chemotherapy as evidenced by oral ulcers and pain on eating. | Within 5 days, says his mouth is more comfortable and is eating more. | Look in his mouth every day. Mouth care with a soft brush and the prescribed rinse after meals and at bedtime. Give the analgesic mouthwash before meals. Offer soft, cool, bland food. | More comfortable, eating more: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Platelets 18 × 10⁹/L | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: thrombocytopenia from disease and chemotherapy. | No major bleeding. | Bleeding precautions in place. Check platelets every day. Give the platelet transfusion as prescribed. Report headache or confusion (could be bleeding in the head). | No major bleeding: goal met. |

## 10.6  Lymphoma

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I sweat at night and I have lost weight." **Objective** Lost 6 kg in 2 months Lymph nodes in the neck enlarged Albumin low | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to increased metabolic demand and reduced appetite as evidenced by weight loss. | Weight steady through treatment. | Assess his nutrition and refer to the dietitian. Offer high-calorie supplements. Control nausea with the prescribed antiemetics. Weigh once a week. | Weight steady: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "What will chemotherapy do to me?" "Can I continue working?" **Objective** Tense Has slept badly since the diagnosis | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to diagnosis and planned treatment as evidenced by questions and insomnia. | Says he understands the treatment plan and feels less anxious. | Explain the treatment schedule and the usual side effects. Give written information. Invite questions and include the family. Offer psychological support. | Less anxious: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On chemotherapy On steroids | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: immunosuppression from treatment. | No infection. | Teach hand hygiene and food safety. Tell him to report a temperature of 38 °C or more straight away. He should stay away from crowds while his counts are low. | No infection: goal met. |

## 10.7  Disseminated Intravascular Coagulation (DIC)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Oozing from IV and puncture sites Blood in the urine Platelets and fibrinogen low, D-dimer high BP 92/58 mmHg | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to active bleeding from coagulopathy as evidenced by oozing, hematuria and falling BP. | Within 24 hours, bleeding less and vital signs stable. | Help treat whatever caused it (sepsis, an obstetric complication, trauma). Give platelets, plasma and cryoprecipitate as prescribed. Press on puncture sites for longer than usual. Follow vital signs, Hb and clotting results. Avoid any puncture that is not needed. | Less bleeding, stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Fingers and toes cool and mottled Capillary refill slow | **ACTUAL** Ineffective peripheral tissue perfusion related to microvascular thrombi as evidenced by mottled, cool extremities. | Hands and feet warm, with capillary refill under 3 seconds. | Check hands and feet every hour. Keep her warm. Report cyanosis or any new organ failure. | Perfusion better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Clotting problem continuing Septic | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: hemorrhage and sepsis. | MAP above 65 mmHg and urine output at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour. | Follow hemodynamics and hourly urine output. Give fluids and blood products as ordered. | Stable: goal met. |

## 10.8  Thrombocytopenia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Small red spots appeared on my legs." "What should I avoid?" **Objective** Petechiae on the lower legs Platelets 20 × 10⁹/L | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to bleeding precautions as evidenced by questions. | Before discharge, can describe the bleeding precautions. | Teach her to use a soft toothbrush and an electric razor. She should avoid aspirin, NSAIDs and contact sports. Teach her how to prevent constipation and straining. Tell her to report nosebleeds, black stools or headache. | Describes the precautions: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am afraid I will bleed inside." **Objective** Anxious Keeps asking about her results | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to bleeding risk as evidenced by worry and repeated questions. | Says she feels less anxious. | Explain the plan and how she is being monitored. Share her results as appropriate. Let her voice her worries. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Platelets 20 × 10⁹/L | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: thrombocytopenia. | No major bleeding. | No IM injections and no rectal procedures. Watch for any neurological change. Give platelets or other treatment as prescribed. | No major bleeding: goal met. |

## 10.9  Hemophilia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My knee swelled after a small fall." Gives the pain 7/10 **Objective** Bleeding into the knee Factor VIII deficiency Knee movement limited | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to bleeding into the joint as evidenced by swelling, pain and limited movement. | Bleeding stopped and pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Give factor concentrate at once, as prescribed. Rest, ice, compression, elevation. Paracetamol or opioid as prescribed; no aspirin or NSAIDs. No IM injections. | Pain controlled, swelling going down: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "How do I give the factor at home?" **Objective** Home treatment planned Cannot explain reconstitution | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to home factor replacement as evidenced by questions. | Patient or carer prepares and gives the factor IV correctly. | Teach reconstitution and IV injection. Teach keeping a record of doses and bleeds. Teach when to get urgent help (head injury, large bleeds). | Shows you correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Severe factor deficiency | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: clotting factor deficiency. | No major bleeds. | Suggest safe exercise (swimming); no contact sports. Wear medical identification. Teach dental care to prevent gum bleeding. | No major bleeding: goal met. |

## 10.10  The Neutropenic Patient

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Why must I wear a mask and avoid visitors?" **Objective** Neutrophils 0.5 × 10⁹/L Has just had chemotherapy | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to infection prevention during neutropenia as evidenced by questions. | Within 24 hours, can describe the infection precautions. | Explain what neutropenia is and when the counts will be lowest. Teach hand hygiene and food safety (food cooked well, fruit washed). Tell him to report a temperature of 38 °C or more straight away. | Describes the precautions: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel lonely in this room." **Objective** In protective isolation Few visitors | **ACTUAL** Social isolation related to protective isolation as evidenced by expressed loneliness. | Says he feels less lonely. | Drop in regularly, not only when a task needs doing. Encourage phone and video calls. Allow visitors who are well and follow the precautions. | Less lonely: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Neutropenia severe Central line in situ | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: neutropenia and invasive device. | No infection. | Check the mouth, skin, perianal area and line every day. Avoid invasive procedures where you can. Give the growth factor as prescribed. | No infection: goal met. |

## 10.11  HIV Infection and AIDS

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have diarrhea and lost a lot of weight." **Objective** Lost 10% of his weight Oral thrush CD4 count low | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to opportunistic infections and diarrhea as evidenced by weight loss and oral thrush. | Within 2 weeks, weight steady. | Treat the oral thrush as prescribed. Offer small meals often, high in protein. Treat the diarrhea and keep him hydrated. Refer to the dietitian. | Weight steady: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Must I take tablets every day for life?" **Objective** Starting antiretroviral therapy Cannot name his drugs | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to antiretroviral therapy as evidenced by questions. | Before discharge, can explain why he must take his tablets every day. | Explain that missed doses can lead to drug resistance. Help him build a daily routine with reminders. Explain why viral load and CD4 count are checked. Teach how to prevent passing the virus on. | Describes his plan: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** CD4 count low | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: immunodeficiency. | No opportunistic infection. | Give prophylactic medicines as ordered. Tell him to report fever, cough, headache or diarrhea. Keep his information confidential and care for him without judgment. | No infection: goal met. |

## 10.12  Systemic Lupus Erythematosus

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My joints ache and I am always tired." **Objective** Joints painful and swollen Butterfly rash on the face ANA positive | **ACTUAL** Fatigue related to disease activity as evidenced by persistent tiredness and reduced activity. | Within 2 weeks, says she has more energy. | Plan periods of rest and activity. Give anti-inflammatory and immunosuppressive drugs as prescribed. Encourage gentle, regular exercise. | More energy: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I hate the rash on my face." **Objective** Butterfly rash on the face Does not want to go out | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to facial rash as evidenced by distress and avoiding social contact. | Shows acceptance and uses protective measures. | Teach sun protection: sunscreen, a hat, staying out of the midday sun. Talk about cosmetic camouflage. Let her talk about how she feels. | Accepting: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On steroids and immunosuppressants | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: immunosuppressive therapy. | No infection. | Tell her to report a fever early. Follow blood counts. Make sure her vaccinations are done as advised. | No infection: goal met. |

## 10.13  Anaphylaxis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My throat is closing." **Objective** Stridor and wheeze Face and lips swollen Hives | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to airway edema and bronchospasm as evidenced by stridor and wheeze. | Airway open within minutes of treatment. | Call for help and remove the trigger. Give IM adrenaline straight away, following the protocol. Give high-flow oxygen. If the swelling gets worse, prepare for an advanced airway. No improvement? Repeat the adrenaline as the protocol says. | Airway open: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel faint." **Objective** BP 80/40 mmHg HR 130/min | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to vasodilation and capillary leak as evidenced by hypotension and tachycardia. | Systolic BP above 90 mmHg within 30 minutes. | Lay him flat with his legs up (sit him up if lying flat makes the breathing worse). Give an IV fluid bolus as prescribed. Continuous monitoring of BP, HR and SpO₂. After the adrenaline, give the antihistamine and steroid as prescribed. | BP back up: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has a known severe allergy May have a second (biphasic) reaction | **RISK** Risk for allergy reaction as evidenced by risk factors: known severe allergy. | Avoids exposure in future and uses the auto-injector correctly. | Once he recovers, observe him for the time set by policy. Write the allergy clearly in the notes and on his wristband. Teach him how to use the adrenaline auto-injector. Refer to the allergy clinic. | Shows you correctly: goal met. |

## 10.14  Chemotherapy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel sick for days after each cycle." **Objective** Vomits 3 times a day Eating less | **ACTUAL** Nausea related to effects of chemotherapy as evidenced by nausea and vomiting. | Says the nausea is less and keeps eating and drinking. | Give antiemetics before the chemotherapy and regularly afterwards, as prescribed. Offer small, bland, cold meals. Keep strong smells away. Chart what she eats and any vomiting. | Less nausea: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My hair is falling out; I do not want to go out." **Objective** Hair falling out Tearful | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to hair loss as evidenced by distress and avoiding going out. | Uses ways of coping with the hair loss. | Explain that hair usually grows back after treatment. Before the hair falls out, talk about wigs, scarves and head covers. Let her talk about how she feels. | Coping: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Vesicant drugs going through a peripheral line | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: extravasation of vesicant chemotherapy. | Finishes treatment without an extravasation injury. | Check for blood return and look at the IV site before and during the infusion. Ask her to tell you about any pain or burning at the site. If you suspect extravasation: stop the infusion, leave the cannula in, and follow the extravasation protocol. | No injury: goal met. |

## 10.15  Radiotherapy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The skin in the treatment area is red and itchy." **Objective** Treatment field red, with dry peeling skin | **ACTUAL** Impaired skin integrity related to radiation effects as evidenced by erythema and peeling in the treatment field. | Skin heals with no infection. | Teach him to wash with lukewarm water and pat the skin dry. No perfume, deodorant or tight clothes on the area. Keep the area out of the sun. Use only the creams that are prescribed. | Skin healing: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel tired all the time." **Objective** Getting more tired as the course goes on Sleeps in the afternoon | **ACTUAL** Fatigue related to radiotherapy as evidenced by increasing tiredness. | Uses ways of managing the fatigue. | Teach him to pace his activities and plan rests. Encourage light exercise. Make sure he eats well. | Managing the fatigue: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Having radiation to the head and neck | **RISK** Risk for impaired oral mucous membrane integrity as evidenced by risk factors: radiation to the oral cavity. | Lining of the mouth stays intact. | Arrange a dental check before treatment starts. Teach mouth care after meals. He should avoid smoking, alcohol and spicy food. | Mucosa intact: goal met. |

## 10.16  Bone Marrow (Stem Cell) Transplant

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "When will my blood counts recover?" "Will the transplant work?" **Objective** All blood counts low after conditioning Sleeping badly | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to prolonged treatment and uncertain outcome as evidenced by worry and insomnia. | Says she feels less anxious. | Explain roughly how long engraftment is expected to take. Keep her updated on her blood counts. Offer psychological support. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I have a rash and diarrhea." **Objective** Raised red rash on the palms Diarrhea Had an allogeneic transplant | **ACTUAL** Impaired skin integrity related to graft-versus-host disease as evidenced by rash. | Rash gets better with treatment. | Report rash, diarrhea or jaundice quickly. Give immunosuppressive therapy as prescribed. Be gentle with skin care. | Rash improving: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Severely immunosuppressed Central line in situ | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: immunosuppression and invasive device. | No infection. | Keep her in protective isolation. Strict hand hygiene. Watch her temperature and the line site. | No infection: goal met. |

## 10.17  Breast Cancer and Mastectomy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I do not want to see my chest." **Objective** Had a mastectomy 2 days ago Looks away when the dressing is changed | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to loss of a breast as evidenced by refusal to look at the surgical site. | Before discharge, looks at the incision and talks about her options. | Let her talk about how she feels. Offer to be there with her the first time she looks. Talk about a prosthesis and reconstruction. Offer to put her in touch with a support group. | Looks at the site and discusses her options: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives the pain 5/10 **Objective** Incision on the chest Wound drain in situ | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgical tissue trauma as evidenced by pain score 5/10. | Pain 3/10 or less. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Rest her arm on a pillow. Check the drain is working and record the output. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Axillary lymph nodes removed | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: axillary node removal with impaired lymph drainage of the arm. | No infection or lymphedema in the arm. | No BP readings, injections or blood tests on that arm. Teach the arm exercises she has been shown. Teach skin care and how to protect the arm from cuts and burns. Tell her to report any swelling or redness in the arm. | No infection or swelling: goal met. |

**PART 11**

# Integumentary Care Plans

## 11.1  Burns (Acute Phase)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I am very thirsty." **Objective** Partial- and full-thickness burns, 30% TBSA HR 125/min, BP 96/60 mmHg Urine output 20 mL/hour | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to capillary leak and fluid loss through burned skin as evidenced by tachycardia, hypotension and low urine output. | Within 8 hours, urine output 0.5 mL/kg/hour (adult) and vital signs stable. | Put in two large-bore IV cannulas and start fluids by the burn formula, as prescribed. Record urine output every hour through the catheter. Adjust the fluids as ordered, going by the urine output. Vital signs every hour, and follow electrolytes. Weigh every day. | Urine output on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "The pain is unbearable." Gives the pain 9/10 **Objective** Partial-thickness burns Crying and restless | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to burn injury and exposed nerve endings as evidenced by pain score 9/10 and restlessness. | Pain 4/10 or less at rest, and copes with dressing changes. | Give IV opioids as prescribed; do not use the IM route in the acute phase. Give analgesia 30 minutes before each dressing change. Keep the wounds covered so less air reaches them. Distract him and explain every step. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Skin barrier lost Invasive lines in place Burn wound | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: loss of skin integrity and invasive devices. | No wound infection and no systemic infection. | Strict aseptic technique for wound care. Apply the topical antimicrobial dressings as prescribed. Watch how the wound looks and smells, and the temperature. Give tetanus prophylaxis as ordered. | No infection: goal met. |

## 11.2  Burns (Rehabilitation Phase)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot fully straighten my arm." **Objective** Burn over the elbow healing Scar tightening Cannot fully straighten the elbow | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to scar contracture as evidenced by limited joint extension. | Joint ROM kept or improved. | Encourage active ROM exercises several times a day. Put on extension splints as ordered. Work together with the physiotherapist and occupational therapist. Teach him how to wear pressure garments. | ROM kept or better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "People will stare at my scars." **Objective** Scars visible on face and arms Avoids the mirror | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to scarring as evidenced by avoiding mirror and fear of others' reactions. | Talks about his feelings and plans a return to social life. | Let him talk about how he feels. Talk about scar management and reconstructive options. Refer for psychological support and to a burns support group. | Talks about his plans: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** New skin still fragile Itchy | **RISK** Risk for impaired skin integrity as evidenced by risk factors: fragile healed skin and scratching. | Healed skin stays intact. | Teach him to moisturise the healed skin. Teach sun protection. Give the antipruritic as prescribed. | Skin intact: goal met. |

## 11.3  Pressure Injury Management

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My lower back is sore." **Objective** Stage 3 pressure injury on the sacrum, 4 × 3 cm Slough in the wound bed Immobile | **ACTUAL** Impaired tissue integrity related to unrelieved pressure as evidenced by stage 3 sacral pressure injury. | Within 2 weeks the wound is smaller and the granulation looks healthy. | Measure the wound and record it every week. Do wound care according to the wound care plan. Take the pressure off: turn every 2 hours and never let him lie on the wound. Nurse him on a pressure-redistributing mattress. Refer to the tissue viability nurse. | Wound smaller: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am not hungry." **Objective** Albumin low Eats less than half of each meal | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to poor intake as evidenced by low albumin and reduced intake. | Eats at least three-quarters of his meals and supplements, to help healing. | Refer to the dietitian. Offer protein supplements. Chart what he eats. | Eating more: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Open wound close to the anus Incontinent | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: open wound and fecal contamination. | Wound stays free of infection. | Keep the wound covered, and clean it after every episode of incontinence. Watch for a smell, more exudate, and fever. | No infection: goal met. |

## 11.4  Surgical Wound Care

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The wound is wet and smells." **Objective** Redness spreading 2 cm out from the incision Pus coming from the wound Temperature 38.2 °C | **ACTUAL** Impaired tissue integrity related to wound infection as evidenced by redness, purulent discharge and fever. | Within 72 hours, less redness and discharge. | Take a wound swab as ordered. Do aseptic wound care as prescribed. Give antibiotics as prescribed. Record how the wound looks every shift. | Wound improving: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives wound pain 5/10 **Objective** Wound tender Guards it when moving | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to wound inflammation as evidenced by pain score 5/10. | Pain 3/10 or less. | Give analgesia before dressing changes. Be gentle with the wound. Use non-adherent dressings. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Obese Abdominal wound under strain | **RISK** Risk for impaired tissue integrity as evidenced by risk factors: obesity, wound infection and raised abdominal pressure from coughing (wound dehiscence). | Wound edges stay closed. | Teach her to splint the wound when she coughs. Use an abdominal binder if ordered. Report any increase in serous drainage (it can come before dehiscence). | Wound intact: goal met. |

## 11.5  Cellulitis

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

Give antibiotics as prescribed. Raise the leg. Watch his temperature. | Redness going back: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives the pain 6/10 **Objective** Leg tender and swollen Limping | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to tissue inflammation as evidenced by pain score. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Raise the leg. Nothing tight over the area. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Infection spreading Diabetic | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: possible sepsis. | Vital signs stay stable. | Vital signs every 4 hours. Report low BP, confusion or redness that is spreading fast. | Stable: goal met. |

## 11.6  Diabetic and Venous Leg Ulcers

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The wound on my leg has been open for months." **Objective** Venous ulcer above the inner ankle Swelling Skin stained brown | **ACTUAL** Impaired tissue integrity related to venous insufficiency as evidenced by chronic open ulcer. | Within 4 weeks the ulcer is smaller. | Measure the ulcer every week. Do an ABPI before compression, as ordered. Apply compression bandaging as prescribed. Teach her to keep the leg raised. | Ulcer smaller: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "The bandages are uncomfortable." **Objective** Does not want to wear compression Takes the bandages off at night | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to discomfort of compression therapy as evidenced by removing bandages. | Keeps the compression on as prescribed. | Explain how compression helps the ulcer heal. Check the bandage fits well and is comfortable. Teach walking and ankle exercises. | Keeps it on: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Wound has been open a long time | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: chronic open wound. | Ulcer stays free of infection. | Watch for more pain, a smell, more exudate and spreading redness. If infection is suspected, take a swab. | No infection: goal met. |

## 11.7  Skin Grafts

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "When can I move my leg?" **Objective** Split-thickness graft on the leg On ordered bed rest | **ACTUAL** Impaired physical mobility related to immobilization of the graft site as evidenced by prescribed bed rest. | Graft takes, and he gets up when allowed. | Keep the graft site still, as ordered. Do not let anything press on or rub the graft. Raise the leg. Teach exercises for the other limbs. | Graft taking: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "The donor site hurts more than the graft." **Objective** Donor site on the thigh Guards the thigh | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to donor site wound as evidenced by reported pain. | Pain 3/10 or less. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Leave the donor dressing intact as the protocol says. Position him so nothing presses on the donor site. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Graft site and donor site | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: two open wounds. | Graft takes, and neither site becomes infected. | Watch for a smell, discharge and fever. Aseptic technique for dressing changes. Watch the colour of the graft and whether it is sticking. | No infection: goal met. |

## 11.8  Herpes Zoster (Shingles)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Burning pain on one side of my chest with blisters." **Objective** Blistering rash following one dermatome Pain, 7/10 | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to nerve inflammation as evidenced by burning pain along a dermatome. | Pain 3/10 or less. | Start the antiviral early, as prescribed. Give analgesia and neuropathic agents as ordered. Use cool compresses. Loose cotton clothes. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "The itching keeps me awake." **Objective** Scratching Not sleeping well | **ACTUAL** Impaired comfort related to pruritus as evidenced by scratching and sleep disturbance. | Within 48 hours, says the itching is less. | Keep nails short. Apply calamine or the prescribed lotion. Give an antihistamine at night if prescribed. | Less itching: goal met. |
| **Subjective** His daughter-in-law, who lives with him, is pregnant **Objective** Blisters still open | **RISK** Risk for infection (to others) as evidenced by risk factors: contact with vesicle fluid. | Not passed on to anyone without immunity. | Keep the lesions covered. Until the lesions crust over, he should keep away from pregnant women, newborns and anyone immunosuppressed. Hand hygiene. | No one infected: goal met. |

**PART 12**

# Eye and ENT Care Plans

## 12.1  Cataract Surgery

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "How do I put the drops in?" "Can I pray and bend as usual?" **Objective** Day of the cataract operation Cannot explain how to put the drops in Elderly | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to post-operative eye care as evidenced by questions about eye drops and activity. | Before discharge, puts the eye drops in herself and can state the precautions. | Teach hand hygiene and how to put the drops in, waiting 5 minutes between different drops. She should wear the eye shield at night as advised. No rubbing the eye, bending, heavy lifting or straining. Tell her to report sudden pain, worse vision or discharge. | Shows you correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot see well with one eye covered." **Objective** Patch over the operated eye Cannot judge depth well | **ACTUAL** Bathing self-care deficit related to temporary visual limitation as evidenced by difficulty with self-care. | Manages self-care safely, with help when needed. | Show her around the room. Put her things on the side of her good eye. Help with bathing as needed. | Safe self-care: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Vision reduced Elderly | **RISK** Risk for adult falls as evidenced by risk factors: impaired vision and older age. | No falls. | Keep the floor clear and the room well lit. Walk with her at first. Call bell within reach. | No falls: goal met. |

## 12.2  Glaucoma

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I did not know I was losing side vision." **Objective** Pressure inside the eye raised Losing peripheral vision | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to chronic eye disease and lifelong treatment as evidenced by unawareness of condition. | Can explain what the eye drops are for and that they are for life. | Explain that vision already lost will not come back, but further loss can be prevented. Teach how to put the drops in and to press on the inner corner of the eye afterwards. Stress regular checks of the eye pressure. Before starting any new medicine, he should tell the doctor he has glaucoma. | Describes his plan: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Acute angle closure: "My eye hurts badly and I see halos." **Objective** Eye red and painful Vomiting Pressure inside the eye very high | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to sudden rise in intraocular pressure as evidenced by severe eye pain, halos and vomiting. | Within hours, pain relieved and pressure down. | Treat it as an emergency and call the ophthalmologist. Give the prescribed drugs (drops, acetazolamide) without delay. Give an antiemetic and analgesia. Lay him flat as advised. | Pain relieved: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has lost part of his visual field | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: reduced peripheral vision. | No injury. | Teach him to turn his head to scan around him. Clear hazards at home. | No injury: goal met. |

Keep her calm and resting. Let the family stay. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "How long must I keep my head down?" **Objective** Gas bubble in the eye after vitrectomy Ordered to keep a set position | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to post-operative positioning as evidenced by questions. | Keeps her head in the prescribed position. | Teach the exact position and how long the surgeon wants it kept. While the gas is in the eye, no flying and no going to high altitude. Give pillows and supports to keep her comfortable. | Keeps the position: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has to stay in one position Vision reduced | **RISK** Risk for impaired skin integrity as evidenced by risk factors: prolonged fixed position. | Skin over pressure points stays intact. | Check pressure areas on the face and chest. ## 12.4  Tonsillectomy (Adult)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "It hurts a lot to swallow." Gives the pain 7/10 **Objective** Throat pain Does not want to eat | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgical trauma of the throat as evidenced by pain on swallowing. | Pain low enough to eat and drink. | Give analgesia regularly, especially before meals. Encourage cool drinks and soft food. Encourage a normal diet early, because it helps healing. | Eating and drinking: goal met. |

## 12.5  Laryngectomy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Writes "How will I talk?" **Objective** Total laryngectomy Permanent tracheostoma | **ACTUAL** Impaired verbal communication related to removal of the larynx as evidenced by inability to speak. | Gets his needs across by other means. | Give him a writing board or a communication app. Give him extra time. Refer to the speech therapist to discuss ways of restoring his voice. | Gets his needs across: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Points to the stoma and coughs **Objective** Secretions coming from the stoma Breathing noisy | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to increased secretions and altered airway as evidenced by secretions and noisy breathing. | Airway stays clear. | Suction as needed. Humidify the air he breathes. Clean the stoma and teach him to care for it himself. | Airway clear: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Permanent stoma | **RISK** Risk for aspiration as evidenced by risk factors: open stoma (water, foreign bodies). | No aspiration through the stoma. | Teach him to cover the stoma in the shower. He must not go swimming. Use a stoma cover. | States the precautions: goal met. |

## 12.6  Head and Neck Cancer

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Food sticks in my throat; I cannot swallow solids." **Objective** Lost 7 kg Coughs on thin fluids Tumour in the oropharynx | **ACTUAL** Impaired swallowing related to tumour obstruction and treatment effects as evidenced by dysphagia and coughing with fluids. | Gets enough nutrition by a safe route and keeps his weight steady. | Arrange a swallow assessment with the speech therapist. Give a texture-modified diet and thickened fluids as recommended. Give tube feeds if ordered. Weigh once a week and refer to the dietitian. | Weight steady, eating safely: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I do not want people to see my face." **Objective** Face changed after surgery Does not want visitors | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to facial disfigurement as evidenced by avoiding visitors and distress. | Talks about how he feels and lets the family visit. | Let him talk about how he feels. Prepare the family for how he looks. Talk about reconstructive and prosthetic options. Refer for psychological support. | Accepts visits: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Had surgery and radiation to the pharynx Weak cough | **RISK** Risk for aspiration as evidenced by risk factors: impaired swallowing and altered anatomy. | No aspiration. | Sit him up for every meal and for 30 minutes after. Keep suction available. Watch for fever, cough and chest signs. | No aspiration: goal met. |

**PART 13**

# Gynecological and Men's Health Care Plans

## 13.1  Hysterectomy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The lower abdominal pain is strong." Gives the pain 7/10 **Objective** Incision in the abdomen Guarding HR 102/min | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgical tissue trauma as evidenced by pain score 7/10 and guarding. | Pain 3/10 or less within 24 hours. | Give analgesia regularly as prescribed (PCA if ordered). Have her hold a pillow against her abdomen when she coughs or moves. Get her moving early. Watch the wound and any vaginal bleeding. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel I am no longer a complete woman." **Objective** Tearful Young, uterus removed | **ACTUAL** Situational low self-esteem related to loss of reproductive organ as evidenced by negative statements about self. | Talks about how she feels and can name positive things about herself. | Give her privacy to talk about her feelings. Give accurate information about how this affects sexuality and menopause. If she wishes, include her husband. Refer for counselling if needed. | Shows acceptance: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Had pelvic surgery Catheter out today | **RISK** Risk for urinary retention as evidenced by risk factors: pelvic surgery and bladder handling. | Passes enough urine within 6 hours of the catheter coming out. | Record when she voids and how much. If she passes very little or nothing, scan the bladder. Encourage fluids. | Voids adequately: goal met. |

## 13.2  Cervical and Ovarian Cancer

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have abdominal swelling and lost my appetite." **Objective** Ascites Losing weight CA-125 raised | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to early satiety and disease as evidenced by weight loss and poor intake. | Weight steady through treatment. | Offer small meals often, and supplements. Refer to the dietitian. Control the nausea. Weigh once a week. | Weight steady: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Will I survive this?" **Objective** Tearful Sleeping badly | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to cancer diagnosis and treatment as evidenced by fear and insomnia. | Says she feels less anxious. | Give clear information together with the medical team. Invite her questions. Offer psychological and spiritual support. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Pelvic cancer Had major surgery | **RISK** Risk for thrombosis as evidenced by risk factors: cancer, pelvic surgery and reduced mobility. | No DVT. | Give the prophylactic anticoagulant as ordered. Put on compression stockings. Get her moving early. | No DVT: goal met. |

## 13.3  Endometriosis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My periods are extremely painful." Gives the pain 8/10 during her period **Objective** Pelvis tender Pain gets in the way of work | **ACTUAL** Chronic pain related to endometrial tissue outside the uterus as evidenced by severe cyclical pelvic pain. | Pain at a level that lets her manage her daily activities. | Give NSAIDs and hormonal therapy as prescribed. Teach heat application and relaxation. Encourage her to keep a pain diary. | Pain manageable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Will I be able to have children?" **Objective** Married 3 years, not yet pregnant Gets tearful talking about fertility | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to concern about fertility as evidenced by questions and worry. | Says she understands her treatment and her fertility options. | Give her accurate information. Refer to a fertility specialist as appropriate. | Informed: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On hormonal therapy | **RISK** Risk for ineffective health self-management as evidenced by risk factors: long-term hormonal therapy with side effects. | Keeps up her treatment as prescribed. | Explain the side effects and how to manage them. Book follow-up. | Keeps to treatment: goal met. |

## 13.4  Pelvic Inflammatory Disease

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have lower abdominal pain and discharge." **Objective** Fever 38.4 °C Pain when the cervix is moved Purulent vaginal discharge | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to pelvic infection as evidenced by lower abdominal pain and tenderness. | Within 48 hours of treatment, pain controlled. | Give antibiotics as prescribed and stress finishing the course. Give analgesia. Sit her semi-Fowler to help drainage. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "How did I get this?" **Objective** Her partner has not been treated Cannot say how to prevent it | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to transmission and prevention as evidenced by questions. | Can explain why her partner needs treatment and how to prevent this. | Explain why her partner must be treated too. Teach safe sex. No intercourse until treatment is finished. | Describes her plan: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Fallopian tubes inflamed | **RISK** Risk for ineffective health self-management as evidenced by risk factors: incomplete treatment, which may lead to infertility or ectopic pregnancy. | Finishes treatment and follow-up. | Book a review after treatment. Explain the long-term risks if PID is not treated. Tell her to come back if the pain or fever returns. | Treatment and follow-up done: goal met. |

## 13.5  Prostate Cancer and Prostatectomy

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I leak urine since the catheter was removed." **Objective** Dribbling since the catheter came out Uses 3 pads a day | **ACTUAL** Stress urinary incontinence related to sphincter weakness after prostatectomy as evidenced by urine leakage. | Within 6–12 weeks, uses fewer pads. | Teach pelvic floor exercises and check he is doing them right. Give him suitable continence products. He should cut down caffeine and alcohol. Explain that continence often gets better over the months. | Continence improving: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Will I be able to have relations with my wife?" **Objective** Worried about erectile function after surgery Avoids talking about it with his wife | **ACTUAL** Sexual dysfunction related to effects of radical prostatectomy as evidenced by expressed concern about erectile function. | Talks about his worries and the options open to him. | Give him privacy and do not judge. Give accurate information about recovery and the treatments available. If he wishes, include his wife. Refer to urology or a sexual health specialist. | Discusses the options: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Had pelvic surgery Has cancer | **RISK** Risk for thrombosis as evidenced by risk factors: pelvic surgery and cancer. | No DVT. | Give the prophylactic anticoagulant as ordered. Get him moving early. Teach leg exercises and how to check his calves. | No DVT: goal met. |

## 13.6  Sexually Transmitted Infections
