| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have burning on passing urine and a discharge." "Do I need to tell my partner?" **Objective** Urethral discharge Tested positive for gonorrhea or chlamydia | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to treatment and transmission as evidenced by questions. | Before leaving, can explain the treatment plan, partner notification and prevention. | Explain the infection and why the treatment must be finished. Explain why the partner needs testing and treatment. No intercourse until both partners have been treated. Teach safe sex. | Describes the plan: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am ashamed; please do not tell anyone." **Objective** Will not make eye contact Tense | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to diagnosis, stigma and fear of disclosure as evidenced by shame and request for secrecy. | Says he feels less anxious and trusts that care is confidential. | Do not judge. Explain the rules on confidentiality. Give care in private. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Partner not treated Does not always use condoms | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: re-exposure to an untreated partner. | No reinfection. | Help with notifying the partner. Arrange a test of cure where needed. Offer tests for other STIs, including HIV and hepatitis B. | No reinfection: goal met. |

**PART 14**

# Critical Care and Emergency Care Plans

## 14.1  Hypovolemic Shock

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel cold and thirsty." **Objective** BP 80/50 mmHg, HR 132/min Skin cold and clammy; capillary refill 4 seconds Urine output 10 mL/hour Known to have lost blood or fluid | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to acute blood or fluid loss as evidenced by hypotension, tachycardia, poor perfusion and oliguria. | Within 2 hours, MAP 65 mmHg or more and urine output at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour. | Call for help and stop any external bleeding with direct pressure. Put in two large-bore IV cannulas. Give warmed fluids and blood products as prescribed. Check BP, HR, SpO₂ and level of consciousness every 5–15 minutes. Put in a urinary catheter and record the output every hour. | MAP and urine output on target: goal met. If not, get ready for surgery to control the bleeding. |
| **Subjective** Drowsy **Objective** Confused and slow to respond SpO₂ 92% | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to reduced cerebral perfusion as evidenced by drowsiness and slow responses. | Alert and oriented as perfusion improves. | GCS every 15–30 minutes. Give oxygen to keep SpO₂ on target. Unless there is a reason not to, keep him flat with his legs up. | Alert: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Receiving a massive transfusion Given cold fluids Uncovered during resuscitation | **RISK** Risk for hypothermia as evidenced by risk factors: blood loss, cold IV fluids and exposure during resuscitation. | Core temperature stays above 36 °C. | Run blood and fluids through a fluid warmer. Cover him with warm blankets or a forced-air warmer. Temperature every 30–60 minutes. | Temperature kept normal: goal met. |

## 14.2  Sepsis and Septic Shock

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel very unwell and shivery." **Objective** Temperature 39.3 °C or below 36 °C BP 85/45 mmHg, HR 128/min, RR 28/min Lactate 4 mmol/L Newly confused | **ACTUAL** Ineffective peripheral tissue perfusion related to vasodilation and capillary leak as evidenced by hypotension, raised lactate, mottled skin and confusion. | Within 6 hours, MAP 65 mmHg or more and lactate falling. | Follow the sepsis bundle: blood cultures, then IV antibiotics within 1 hour. Give an IV fluid bolus as prescribed, then reassess. Measure lactate and repeat it as ordered. If BP stays low, start vasopressors as prescribed. Record urine output every hour. | MAP on target, lactate falling: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Temperature 39.3 °C Shaking with rigors | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to infection as evidenced by temperature 39.3 °C and rigors. | Temperature below 38 °C within 6 hours. | Give the antipyretic as prescribed. Take off extra covers. Temperature every hour. | Temperature coming down: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** In septic shock BP low | **RISK** Risk for decreased cardiac output as evidenced by risk factors: septic cardiomyopathy and dysrhythmias. | Rhythm and output stable. | Continuous ECG monitoring. Follow electrolytes. Report any new dysrhythmia. | Stable: goal met. |

## 14.3  Cardiogenic Shock

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot breathe and feel faint." **Objective** BP 78/50 mmHg after a large MI Crackles, SpO₂ 88% Skin cold and mottled Urine output 10 mL/hour | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to impaired myocardial contractility as evidenced by hypotension, pulmonary congestion and oliguria. | Within 6 hours, MAP 65 mmHg or more and urine output at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour. | Continuous monitoring of ECG, BP (arterial line if ordered) and urine output. Give inotropes and vasopressors through an infusion pump as prescribed. Get ready for coronary reperfusion or mechanical support as ordered. Give fluids only if ordered, and carefully. | Hemodynamics improving: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Crackles all through the lungs SpO₂ 88% | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to pulmonary congestion as evidenced by crackles and hypoxemia. | SpO₂ 94% or more within 6 hours. | If BP allows, sit him up. Give oxygen and be ready for non-invasive or mechanical ventilation. Give diuretics if ordered. | SpO₂ on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Cardiac output low | **RISK** Risk for electrolyte imbalance as evidenced by risk factors: renal hypoperfusion and diuretic therapy. | Electrolytes normal and creatinine stable. | Follow electrolytes and creatinine. Replace as ordered. | Values normal: goal met. |

## 14.4  Neurogenic Shock

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Injury to the cervical spinal cord BP 82/48 mmHg, HR 48/min Skin warm and dry below the level of injury | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to loss of sympathetic tone as evidenced by hypotension, bradycardia and warm skin. | MAP within the target the physician has set. | Keep the spine immobilised. Give fluids and vasopressors as prescribed. For symptomatic bradycardia, give atropine as ordered. Continuous ECG and BP monitoring. | MAP on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Cough weak Vital capacity reduced | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to respiratory muscle weakness as evidenced by reduced vital capacity. | Ventilation adequate, vital capacity stable and SpO₂ on target. | Follow vital capacity, SpO₂ and ABG. If his breathing gets worse, prepare for ventilation. Assisted coughing, as you were taught. | Ventilation adequate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Cannot control body temperature below the injury | **RISK** Risk for hypothermia as evidenced by risk factors: loss of sympathetic control below the level of injury. | Core temperature stays at 36–37.5 °C. | Temperature every 2–4 hours. Adjust the room temperature and his covers. Keep him covered during procedures. | Temperature normal: goal met. |

## 14.5  Cardiac Arrest and Post-Resuscitation Care

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Spontaneous circulation back after cardiac arrest BP 88/50 mmHg Intubated, GCS 6 | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to post-arrest myocardial dysfunction as evidenced by hypotension. | MAP 65 mmHg or more. | Continuous monitoring of ECG, BP and SpO₂. Give vasopressors as prescribed. Take a 12-lead ECG and, if ordered, prepare for coronary angiography. Keep SpO₂ on target, avoiding both too little and too much oxygen. | MAP on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** GCS 6 May be having seizures | **ACTUAL** Decreased intracranial adaptive capacity related to hypoxic brain injury as evidenced by GCS 6 after cardiac arrest. | No secondary brain injury. | Control temperature as the protocol says, and do not let a fever develop. Keep glucose and CO₂ normal. Watch for seizures. Head of the bed at 30 degrees. | Neurological status stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Family ask "Will he wake up?" **Objective** Outcome uncertain | **RISK** Risk for maladaptive grieving (family) as evidenced by risk factors: sudden critical illness of a family member and uncertain prognosis. | The family get information and support. | Give honest updates regularly. Let the family be with him. Offer spiritual and social support. | Family supported: goal met. |

## 14.6  Multiple Trauma

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel weak and dizzy." **Objective** Fractured pelvis and femur BP 86/54 mmHg, HR 128/min Skin pale and cold | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to hemorrhage from multiple injuries as evidenced by hypotension, tachycardia and cold skin. | MAP 65 mmHg or more and urine output at least 0.5 mL/kg/hour. | Do the primary survey: airway with cervical spine control, breathing, circulation. Stop external bleeding and put on a pelvic binder as ordered. Give warmed blood products as prescribed (massive transfusion protocol). Vital signs every 5–15 minutes and urine output every hour. Get him ready for imaging and surgery. | Hemodynamics stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Everything hurts." Gives the pain 9/10 **Objective** Several fractures Grimacing Guarding | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to multiple tissue injuries as evidenced by pain score 9/10 and guarding. | Within 1 hour, says the pain is bearable. | Give IV analgesia in small doses, titrated, as prescribed. Splint the fractures. Check pain and sedation often. Handle and move him gently. | Pain bearable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Open wounds Has invasive lines and drains | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: open wounds, contamination and invasive devices. | No wound infection. | Clean and dress wounds with aseptic technique. Give tetanus prophylaxis and antibiotics as ordered. Watch his temperature and how the wounds look. | No infection: goal met. |

## 14.7  Chest Trauma (Rib Fractures and Flail Chest)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot take a deep breath because of pain." **Objective** One side of the chest moves the wrong way (paradoxical) RR 30/min and shallow SpO₂ 89% | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to chest wall instability and pain as evidenced by paradoxical movement, tachypnea and hypoxemia. | Within 12 hours, RR 12–24/min and SpO₂ 94% or more. | Give oxygen as prescribed. Sit him upright, or on the injured side if ordered. Make sure analgesia works well enough (nerve block or epidural as ordered) for him to breathe deeply. Follow SpO₂, RR and ABG. If respiratory failure develops, prepare for ventilation. | Breathing effectively: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Pain 8/10 when he breathes **Objective** Ribs tender Holds his chest stiff | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to rib fractures as evidenced by pain on breathing and splinting. | Pain 4/10 or less when he breathes deeply. | Give analgesia regularly. Teach him to hold a pillow against his chest when he coughs. Check pain before and after breathing exercises. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Breathing shallow Secretions building up | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: hypoventilation and retained secretions (pneumonia). | No chest infection. | While awake, encourage the incentive spirometer every hour. Encourage coughing once the analgesia is working. Watch his temperature and sputum. | No infection: goal met. |

## 14.8  Abdominal Trauma

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My abdomen hurts after the accident." **Objective** Abdomen distended and bruised BP 90/56 mmHg, HR 122/min Ultrasound shows free fluid | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to internal bleeding as evidenced by hypotension, tachycardia and free intraperitoneal fluid. | Vital signs stable before and after surgery. | Put in two large-bore IV cannulas. Give fluids and blood as prescribed. Nothing by mouth; get him ready for laparotomy. Follow vital signs, abdominal girth and urine output. | Hemodynamics stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives the pain 8/10 **Objective** Guarding and rebound tenderness | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to abdominal injury as evidenced by pain score 8/10 and guarding. | Pain bearable (4/10 or less) once he has been assessed and treated. | Once the surgeon has assessed him, give analgesia as prescribed. Let him lie with his knees bent. Reassess often. | Pain bearable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Bowel may be injured | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: possible bowel perforation and peritoneal contamination. | No peritonitis and no wound infection. | Give antibiotics as prescribed. Watch his temperature and abdominal signs. Report pain that is getting worse, or rigidity. | No infection: goal met. |

## 14.9  Multiple Organ Dysfunction Syndrome (MODS)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On a ventilator, FiO₂ 0.7 PaO₂ low Bilateral infiltrates | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to alveolar-capillary injury as evidenced by hypoxemia on high oxygen. | Oxygenation within the target the physician has set. | Follow ABG and the ventilator settings. Use lung-protective ventilation as ordered. Position and prone him according to protocol. Suction as needed. | Oxygenation on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Urine output 10 mL/hour Creatinine climbing Fluid balance 4 L positive | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to acute kidney injury as evidenced by oliguria, rising creatinine and positive balance. | Fluid balance on target. | Record urine output and fluid balance every hour. Get ready for renal replacement therapy as ordered. Follow electrolytes. | On target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Sedated Immobile Edematous | **RISK** Risk for adult pressure injury as evidenced by risk factors: immobility, edema and poor perfusion. | Skin stays intact. | Change his position every 2 hours, as he tolerates. Use pressure-redistributing surfaces. Check the skin and the areas under devices every shift. | Skin intact: goal met. |

## 14.10  Poisoning and Drug Overdose

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Family found him with empty tablet bottles **Objective** RR 8/min and shallow SpO₂ 86% Pupils pinpoint | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to central nervous system depression as evidenced by bradypnea and hypoxemia. | RR 12–20/min and SpO₂ 94% or more. | Open the airway and support breathing with a bag-valve-mask if needed. Give the antidote as prescribed (naloxone for opioids, for example). Continuous monitoring of RR, SpO₂ and level of consciousness. Contact the poison information service as policy says. Take blood for drug levels as ordered. | Breathing adequate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** GCS 10 Disoriented when he wakes | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to toxic effects of substance as evidenced by reduced GCS and disorientation. | Back to his usual orientation. | Check GCS often. Keep him safe and under close observation. Once he is alert, ask whether the overdose was intentional and refer to the mental health team. | Oriented and referred: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Not fully conscious Vomiting | **RISK** Risk for aspiration as evidenced by risk factors: decreased level of consciousness and vomiting. | Airway stays clear, with no aspiration. | Put him in the recovery position. Have suction ready. Nothing by mouth until he is alert. | No aspiration: goal met. |

## 14.11  Heat Stroke

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Collapsed while working outside in summer **Objective** Core temperature 41 °C Skin hot and dry Confused | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to exposure to high environmental temperature as evidenced by core temperature 41 °C, hot skin and confusion. | Core temperature below 39 °C within 30 minutes. | Get him somewhere cool and take off his clothes. Cool him fast: cold water immersion, or evaporative cooling with fans plus ice packs to the neck, armpits and groin. Monitor core temperature continuously and stop active cooling at about 39 °C, as the protocol says. Watch for seizures. | Temperature on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** HR 130/min BP 90/60 mmHg Passing small amounts of dark urine | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to excessive sweating and heat exposure as evidenced by tachycardia, hypotension and low urine output. | Urine output adequate and vital signs stable. | Give IV fluids as prescribed. Record urine output every hour and look for dark urine (rhabdomyolysis). Follow electrolytes, creatinine and CK. | Stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Brain affected by the heat Confused | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: seizures and confusion from heat stroke. | No injury. | Seizure precautions in place. If he has a seizure, give the anticonvulsant as prescribed. Keep him under close observation. | No injury: goal met. |

## 14.12  Accidental Hypothermia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Found outside in the cold, drowsy **Objective** Core temperature 31 °C Not shivering Speech slurred | **ACTUAL** Hypothermia related to prolonged exposure to cold as evidenced by core temperature 31 °C and drowsiness. | Core temperature rises by about 1–2 °C an hour, as the protocol says. | Take off wet clothes and handle him gently. Use active external rewarming (warm blankets, a forced-air warmer). Give warmed IV fluids as prescribed. Monitor core temperature continuously. | Warming safely: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** HR 42/min J waves on the ECG BP 90/50 mmHg | **ACTUAL** Decreased cardiac output related to bradycardia and dysrhythmias caused by hypothermia as evidenced by slow HR and low BP. | Rhythm and BP stable as he warms up. | Continuous ECG monitoring. Have the defibrillator ready. Move him gently; rough handling can set off ventricular fibrillation. | Rhythm stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Being rewarmed Fluid shifting | **RISK** Risk for electrolyte imbalance as evidenced by risk factors: rewarming and fluid shifts (potassium changes). | Potassium and glucose stay normal while he is rewarmed. | Check potassium and glucose during rewarming. Replace as ordered. | Values normal: goal met. |

## 14.13  Drowning

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Pulled from the water after several minutes **Objective** Coughing up frothy sputum SpO₂ 84% Crackles | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to aspiration of water and surfactant loss as evidenced by hypoxemia and crackles. | SpO₂ 94% or more within 6 hours. | Give high-flow oxygen and be ready for CPAP or intubation. Follow SpO₂, RR and ABG. If conscious, sit him up. Observe for at least as long as policy says, because the lung injury can get worse. | SpO₂ on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Temperature 34.5 °C Clothes wet | **ACTUAL** Hypothermia related to immersion in cold water as evidenced by temperature 34.5 °C. | Temperature normal within 2 hours. | Take off the wet clothes. Warm blankets and warmed fluids. Watch his temperature. | Temperature normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Breathed in water | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: aspiration of contaminated water (pneumonia). | No chest infection. | Watch temperature and sputum, and chest X-ray as ordered. Give antibiotics if prescribed. | No infection: goal met. |

**PART 15**

# Maternity Care Plans

## 15.1  Antenatal Care

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "This is my first pregnancy; I do not know what is normal." **Objective** First pregnancy, 12 weeks Cannot name any danger signs First antenatal visit today | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to first pregnancy as evidenced by questions and inability to state danger signs. | By her next visit, she can name at least five danger signs and her visit schedule. | Explain when her antenatal visits are and which tests she will have. Teach the danger signs: vaginal bleeding, severe headache, blurred vision, swollen face and hands, baby moving less, leaking fluid, fever. Teach about nutrition, and folic acid and iron supplements as prescribed. She should avoid smoking, alcohol and any drug that has not been prescribed. | Names the danger signs and her schedule: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel sick every morning." **Objective** Vomits once a day Weight steady Not dehydrated | **ACTUAL** Nausea related to hormonal changes of early pregnancy as evidenced by morning nausea and vomiting. | Says the nausea is less and keeps eating and drinking. | Suggest small, dry meals often, and a few crackers before getting up. She should avoid fatty food and food with strong smells. Tell her to come in if the vomiting stops her keeping fluids down. | Nausea manageable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Hb 10 g/dL Missed her last visits | **RISK** Risk for disturbed maternal–fetal dyad as evidenced by risk factors: anemia and inadequate antenatal care. | Attends her scheduled visits and Hb goes up. | Give iron as prescribed and teach her how to take it properly. Set up reminders for her visits. Check the baby's growth at every visit. | Coming to visits, Hb improving: goal met. |

## 15.2  Hyperemesis Gravidarum

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot keep even water down." **Objective** Vomiting more than 5 times a day Ketones in the urine HR 110/min, mouth dry | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to persistent vomiting as evidenced by ketonuria, tachycardia and dry mucosa. | Within 24–48 hours, no ketones in the urine and urine output adequate. | Give IV fluids with electrolytes as prescribed. Give antiemetics as prescribed. Keep an I&O chart and test urine for ketones every day. Before any dextrose-containing fluid, give thiamine as ordered. | Hydrated, no ketones: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I have lost weight." **Objective** Lost 4 kg Has hardly eaten for 1 week | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to vomiting and reduced intake as evidenced by weight loss. | Tolerates food by mouth and stops losing weight. | Bring back small, dry meals bit by bit. Refer to the dietitian. Weigh twice a week. | Weight steady: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has been vomiting for a long time | **RISK** Risk for electrolyte imbalance as evidenced by risk factors: loss of gastric fluids. | Potassium and sodium stay normal. | Check electrolytes every day. Add replacement to the IV fluids as prescribed. Report muscle weakness or palpitations. | Values normal: goal met. |

## 15.3  Miscarriage (Spontaneous Abortion)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I lost my baby. Was it my fault?" **Objective** Crying Incomplete miscarriage at 10 weeks | **ACTUAL** Grieving related to pregnancy loss as evidenced by crying and self-blame. | Talks about how she feels and can say that she did not cause the loss. | Give her privacy and time. Explain that most early miscarriages are not caused by anything the mother did. Include her husband and family as she wishes. Offer follow-up and counselling. | Shares her feelings: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives the cramping pain 6/10 **Objective** Bleeding vaginally, with clots Cramps in the lower abdomen | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to uterine contractions as evidenced by cramping pain score 6/10. | Pain 3/10 or less. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Get her ready for medical or surgical management as ordered. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Products of conception retained | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: retained products of conception. | Vital signs stable and bleeding getting less. | Count her pads and watch vital signs. Give the uterotonic as ordered. If she is Rh-negative, give anti-D as prescribed. | Bleeding less: goal met. |

## 15.4  Ectopic Pregnancy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have sharp pain on one side and spotting." The pain goes up to her shoulder **Objective** Pregnancy test positive, but the scan shows an empty uterus Tender over one adnexa Pain, 8/10 | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to tubal distension or rupture as evidenced by unilateral pelvic pain and shoulder-tip pain. | Pain controlled before and after treatment. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Nothing by mouth; get her ready for surgery or methotrexate as ordered. Keep checking what the pain is like and report any sudden worsening. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Will I be able to get pregnant again?" **Objective** Tearful Frightened | **ACTUAL** Fear related to loss of pregnancy and future fertility as evidenced by expressed fear and tearfulness. | Talks about her feelings and understands what this means for future pregnancies. | Give her honest information together with the doctor. Give her space to grieve. Book follow-up. | Shows she understands: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Pregnancy in the tube | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: possible tubal rupture with intra-abdominal bleeding. | Rupture picked up early. | Vital signs every 15–30 minutes. Put in large-bore IV access. Report low BP, a fast heart rate or fainting at once. | Stable: goal met. |

## 15.5  Placenta Previa

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I am bleeding but there is no pain." **Objective** Bright red vaginal bleeding with no pain, at 32 weeks Scan shows the placenta covering the cervix HR 104/min | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to placental bleeding as evidenced by vaginal bleeding and tachycardia. | Vital signs stable and bleeding less. | Never do a vaginal examination. Put in IV access and send blood for crossmatch. Watch the bleeding, her vital signs and the fetal heart. Bed rest, lying on her side. | Bleeding settled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Will my baby be all right?" **Objective** Anxious and tearful | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to bleeding and concern for the fetus as evidenced by worry and tearfulness. | Within 24 hours, says she feels less anxious. | Explain the monitoring and the plan. Tell her what the fetal heart shows. Let a support person stay with her. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Preterm and bleeding | **RISK** Risk for disturbed maternal–fetal dyad as evidenced by risk factors: placental bleeding and possible preterm birth. | Baby stays well. | Monitor the fetal heart rate. Give corticosteroids for the baby's lungs as prescribed. Get her ready for cesarean birth as ordered. | Fetal heart normal: goal met. |

## 15.6  Placental Abruption

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Severe constant pain in my abdomen." **Objective** Uterus hard and tender Dark vaginal bleeding Pain, 9/10 | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to bleeding behind the placenta as evidenced by constant abdominal pain and a hard uterus. | Pain controlled. | Give analgesia as prescribed. Get her ready for urgent delivery. Watch the tone of the uterus. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** BP 90/60 mmHg, HR 120/min May be bleeding behind the placenta (concealed) | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to hemorrhage as evidenced by hypotension and tachycardia. | Vital signs stable and urine output adequate. | Put in two large-bore IV cannulas. Give fluids and blood as prescribed. Check clotting for DIC. Record urine output every hour. | Stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Fetal heart pattern abnormal | **RISK** Risk for disturbed maternal–fetal dyad as evidenced by risk factors: placental separation. | Baby delivered safely with no delay. | Continuous fetal heart monitoring. Call the obstetric and neonatal teams straight away. Get her ready for urgent cesarean birth. | Safe delivery: goal met. |

## 15.7  Gestational Hypertension and Pre-eclampsia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My face and hands are swollen." **Objective** BP 156/102 mmHg Protein in the urine Face and hands swollen | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to reduced renal perfusion and capillary leak as evidenced by edema and proteinuria. | BP on the prescribed target and edema not getting worse. | BP every 4 hours, or as ordered. Test urine for protein and keep an I&O chart. Give antihypertensives as prescribed. Follow liver enzymes, platelets and creatinine. Keep checking on the baby. | BP controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am afraid for my baby." **Objective** Anxious Keeps asking about the baby | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to threat to self and fetus as evidenced by worry. | Says she feels less anxious and can name the warning signs. | Explain the plan of care and the monitoring. Teach the warning signs: severe headache, problems with vision, pain in the upper abdomen, baby moving less. Let a support person stay. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Pre-eclampsia is severe | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: possible eclamptic seizure. | No seizures. | Give magnesium sulfate as prescribed. While she is on magnesium, check reflexes, RR and urine output. Keep calcium gluconate at hand. Keep the room quiet and dim. | No seizure: goal met. |

## 15.8  Eclampsia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Generalised seizure BP 170/110 mmHg Frothy secretions | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to seizure activity as evidenced by frothy secretions. | Airway clear and seizure controlled. | Call for help and turn her onto her left side. Keep the airway open; suction and give oxygen. Give magnesium sulfate as prescribed. Do not hold her down; protect her from injury. | Seizure controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Her husband says "She does not know where she is." **Objective** Drowsy after the seizure | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to postictal state as evidenced by drowsiness and disorientation. | Oriented within a few hours of the seizure. | Neuro checks every 15–30 minutes. Reorient her gently. Keep her under close observation with the side rails up. | Oriented: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Mother has had a seizure | **RISK** Risk for disturbed maternal–fetal dyad as evidenced by risk factors: maternal hypoxia during seizures. | Baby stays well until it can be delivered safely. | Continuous fetal heart monitoring. Give the mother oxygen. Once the mother is stable, get her ready for delivery. | Safe outcome: goal met. |

## 15.9  HELLP Syndrome

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have pain under my right ribs." **Objective** Pain in the right upper quadrant Liver enzymes raised Platelets low | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to liver distension as evidenced by right upper quadrant pain. | Pain 3/10 or less within 2 hours. | Give analgesia, but no NSAIDs. Follow liver enzymes and platelets. Sudden severe pain must be reported (could be a liver hematoma). Get her ready for delivery. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "What is happening to me?" **Objective** Anxious Shaking | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to sudden serious illness as evidenced by questions and fear. | Says she feels less anxious. | Explain the plan of care in simple words. Let a support person stay. Keep her told about how the baby is doing. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Platelets 60 × 10⁹/L | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: thrombocytopenia and liver dysfunction. | No major bleeding. | Follow platelets and clotting. Watch the puncture sites. Give platelets as prescribed. | No bleeding: goal met. |

## 15.10  Gestational Diabetes

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I did not have diabetes before." **Objective** Glucose tolerance test abnormal at 26 weeks Cannot say what her glucose targets are | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to new diagnosis of gestational diabetes as evidenced by questions. | Checks her own glucose and can describe her diet plan. | Teach glucose monitoring and her targets. Refer to the dietitian. If insulin is prescribed, teach her how to use it. Encourage light exercise regularly. | Shows you: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Will diabetes harm my baby?" **Objective** Worried Asks about how big the baby is | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to effect of diabetes on the fetus as evidenced by worry. | Says she feels less anxious. | Explain that good glucose control lowers the risks. Explain how the baby's growth will be checked. Invite her questions. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Gestational diabetes Fasting glucose above target | **RISK** Risk for unstable blood glucose level as evidenced by risk factors: insulin resistance of pregnancy. | Blood glucose stays on target. | Look over her glucose record at every visit. If she is on insulin, teach how to treat a hypo. Book a glucose test after the birth. | On target: goal met. |

## 15.11  Anemia in Pregnancy

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel tired and dizzy." **Objective** Hb 9 g/dL Pale | **ACTUAL** Fatigue related to anemia as evidenced by tiredness and dizziness. | Within 4 weeks of treatment, says she has more energy. | Give iron as prescribed. Plan rest periods. Follow the Hb. Teach which foods are rich in iron. | Better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I stopped iron because of constipation." **Objective** Not taking her iron Hb not going up | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to side effects of iron as evidenced by stopping treatment. | Starts taking her iron again as prescribed. | Deal with the side effects using fluids and fibre. Explain why iron matters for her and the baby. Ask the doctor about a different preparation. | Taking it: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Hb 9 g/dL at 36 weeks | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: reduced reserve to tolerate blood loss at birth. | Hb better before delivery. | Follow the Hb. Plan the delivery care with the obstetric team. Make sure blood is available at delivery if needed. | Better: goal met. |

Use comfort measures and lay her on her left side. Time how often the contractions come. | Controlled: goal met. |

Give magnesium sulfate for neuroprotection as prescribed. Monitor the fetal heart. Let the neonatal unit know. | Ready: goal met. |

Tell her to report if the baby moves less. Show her how to check her pads. | Names them: goal met. |

## 15.14  Normal Labor and Birth

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The pain comes every 3 minutes and is very strong." **Objective** Contracting regularly every 3 minutes, each lasting 60 seconds Cervix 6 cm dilated Moaning and gripping the bed | **ACTUAL** Labor pain related to uterine contractions and cervical dilation as evidenced by strong regular contractions and pain behaviour. | Says she is coping with the contractions using the pain relief she has chosen. | With each contraction, check her pain and how she is coping. Encourage upright positions and walking as she can. Teach breathing techniques and use massage. Offer drug pain relief as available and ordered. Encourage her to pass urine every 2 hours. | Coping with labor: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am frightened of the birth." **Objective** Tense First baby | **ACTUAL** Fear related to unknown experience of birth as evidenced by expressed fear and tension. | Says she feels supported and informed. | After every examination, tell her how things are going. Encourage her to have a companion she chooses. Stay with her and support her throughout. | Says she feels supported: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** In active labor | **RISK** Risk for disturbed maternal–fetal dyad as evidenced by risk factors: uterine contractions reducing placental blood flow. | Fetal heart pattern normal right through labor. | Monitor the fetal heart rate as the protocol says (intermittent or continuous). Record findings on the partograph. Report an abnormal fetal heart pattern or meconium-stained liquor. | Fetal heart normal: goal met. |

## 15.15  Induction of Labor

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Why do I need induction?" "Will it hurt more?" **Objective** Overdue, at 41 weeks Cannot explain what will happen | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to the induction procedure as evidenced by questions. | Can say why she is being induced and what to expect. | Explain the reason and the methods (prostaglandin, rupturing the membranes, oxytocin). Explain the monitoring and how long it might take. Get consent as policy requires. | Shows she understands: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Gives contraction pain 7/10 **Objective** Oxytocin infusion running Contracting every 3 minutes | **ACTUAL** Labor pain related to induced uterine contractions as evidenced by pain score and contractions. | Copes with the contractions using the pain relief she has chosen. | Go through the pain relief options with her. Help her with breathing and positions. Check her pain regularly. | Coping: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On an oxytocin infusion | **RISK** Risk for disturbed maternal–fetal dyad as evidenced by risk factors: uterine hyperstimulation from oxytocin. | No more than 4–5 contractions in 10 minutes, and a normal fetal heart. | Run oxytocin through an infusion pump and only increase it as the protocol allows. Continuous monitoring of contractions and fetal heart. If there is hyperstimulation or an abnormal fetal heart, stop the oxytocin and call the doctor. | Contractions and fetal heart normal: goal met. |

## 15.16  Cesarean Section

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The wound hurts when I move or feed the baby." Gives the pain 7/10 **Objective** Low transverse incision on the abdomen Guarding Does not want to move | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to surgical incision as evidenced by pain score 7/10 and reluctance to move. | Within 24 hours, pain 3/10 or less and up and about. | Give analgesia regularly as prescribed. Teach her to hold a pillow over the wound when she coughs or moves. Help her the first time she walks. Encourage her to get moving early. | Pain controlled, walking: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot find a comfortable way to feed." **Objective** Baby not latching well Wound hurts when she holds the baby | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breastfeeding related to post-operative pain and positioning difficulty as evidenced by poor latch. | Within 48 hours, breastfeeding well. | Teach side-lying and the football hold so the baby does not press on the wound. Give analgesia before feeds. Watch a feed and help with the latch. | Feeding well: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Delivered by surgery Uterus may not contract well | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: surgical birth and possible uterine atony. | Fundus firm and lochia a normal amount. | Check the fundus, lochia, wound and vital signs as the protocol says. Give the uterotonic as prescribed. Report heavy bleeding straight away. | No hemorrhage: goal met. |

## 15.17  Normal Postpartum Care

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My stitches hurt when I sit." Gives the pain 5/10 **Objective** Perineal tear repaired A little swollen | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to perineal trauma as evidenced by pain on sitting and swelling. | Pain 3/10 or less. | In the first 24 hours, put a cold pack on the perineum. Give analgesia as prescribed. Teach perineal hygiene and to change pads often. | Pain controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "How do I care for the baby's cord and bath?" **Objective** First baby Not sure how to bathe the baby | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to newborn care and self-care as evidenced by questions. | Before discharge, shows you basic baby care. | Teach cord care, bathing, feeding cues and safe sleep. Teach the danger signs in a newborn: feeding poorly, jaundice, fever, difficulty breathing. Teach her own warning signs: heavy bleeding, fever, pain in the leg, low mood. | Shows you: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Just given birth | **RISK** Risk for bleeding as evidenced by risk factors: postpartum uterus. | Fundus firm, lochia normal. | In the first 24 hours, check the fundus and lochia regularly. Encourage her to pass urine (a full bladder stops the uterus contracting). Teach her fundal massage. | No hemorrhage: goal met. |

## 15.18  Postpartum Hemorrhage

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I feel faint and wet." **Objective** Has lost more than 500 mL of blood Uterus soft and boggy HR 120/min, BP 90/55 mmHg | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to excessive blood loss from uterine atony as evidenced by heavy bleeding, soft uterus, tachycardia and hypotension. | Within 1 hour, bleeding controlled and vital signs stable. | Call for help. Rub up the uterus and empty the bladder. Give uterotonics as prescribed. Put in two large-bore IV cannulas and give fluids and blood as ordered. Measure the blood loss and check vital signs every 5–15 minutes. | Bleeding controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Am I going to die?" **Objective** Frightened Crying | **ACTUAL** Fear related to heavy bleeding and emergency interventions as evidenced by expressed fear. | Says she feels safer as the bleeding stops. | Stay with her. Explain what you are doing in a few words. Keep her husband informed. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Still losing blood | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: hemorrhage. | MAP 65 mmHg or more and urine output adequate. | Put in a urinary catheter and record the output. Check clotting. If the bleeding goes on, get her ready for theatre. | Stable: goal met. |
