## 15.20  Mastitis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My breast is red, hard and painful." **Objective** Red, warm, wedge-shaped area on the breast Nipple cracked | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to breast inflammation as evidenced by pain, redness and hardness. | Pain relieved within 48 hours. | Encourage her to keep feeding or expressing from the affected breast. Warm compress before feeds, cold after. Give analgesia and antibiotics as prescribed. Check the latch. | Pain relieved: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel feverish." **Objective** Temperature 38.5 °C Flushed | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to breast infection as evidenced by temperature 38.5 °C. | Afebrile within 48 hours. | Give the antipyretic as prescribed. Encourage fluids and rest. Keep giving the antibiotics as prescribed. | Afebrile: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Milk not draining Nipple cracked | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: milk stasis and nipple trauma (breast abscess). | No breast abscess. | Make sure the milk is being removed well. Report a fluctuant lump or a fever that gets worse. Teach a correct latch so the nipple is not damaged. | No abscess: goal met. |

## 15.21  Postpartum Depression

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cry all the time and feel I am a bad mother." **Objective** Tearful and withdrawn 3 weeks after the birth Scores high on depression screening Not looking after herself | **ACTUAL** Ineffective coping related to postpartum mood changes and demands of infant care as evidenced by tearfulness, withdrawal and negative self-statements. | Talks about her feelings and accepts treatment and support. | Screen with a validated tool (the Edinburgh scale, for example). Ask her directly about any thoughts of harming herself or the baby, and refer urgently if she has them. Refer to mental health services. Bring the family in to support her and help with the baby. | Accepts support and treatment: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot sleep even when the baby sleeps." **Objective** Cannot sleep Exhausted | **ACTUAL** Disturbed sleep pattern related to low mood and anxiety as evidenced by inability to sleep. | Within 1 week, says she sleeps better. | Arrange for family to help with night feeds. Encourage her to rest when the baby sleeps. Give the prescribed medication if ordered. | Sleeping better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Mood low Little support around her | **RISK** Risk for impaired parenting as evidenced by risk factors: maternal depression and lack of support. | Baby gets safe and adequate care. | Watch how mother and baby interact. Arrange home visits. Bring in family and community support. | Baby cared for safely: goal met. |

## 15.22  Breastfeeding Support

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My baby does not latch and keeps crying." **Objective** Latch shallow Clicking sound while feeding Baby unsettled after feeds | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breastfeeding related to poor latch technique as evidenced by shallow latch and unsettled infant. | Within 48 hours, latching well and the baby is satisfied. | Watch a whole feed. Teach positioning: baby's body held close, nose to nipple, mouth wide open. Encourage feeding on demand, 8–12 times in 24 hours. Teach the signs of a good feed: wet diapers, hearing the baby swallow. | Feeding well: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My nipples are sore." **Objective** Nipples red Winces when the baby latches | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to nipple trauma from poor latch as evidenced by sore nipples. | Within 48 hours, says her nipples are less sore. | Correct the latch. After feeds, rub a little expressed breast milk on the nipples. Change feeding positions. | Less pain: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Latch poor | **RISK** Risk for impaired skin integrity as evidenced by risk factors: repeated nipple trauma. | Nipple skin stays intact. | Check the latch at every feed. Look at the nipples every day. Let the nipples air-dry after feeds. | Intact: goal met. |

**PART 16**

# Newborn Care Plans

## 16.1  Normal Newborn

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "The baby feels cold." **Objective** Axillary temperature 36.2 °C Born 1 hour ago Skin still wet from birth | **ACTUAL** Ineffective thermoregulation related to immature temperature control and heat loss after birth as evidenced by temperature 36.2 °C. | Axillary temperature kept at 36.5–37.5 °C. | Dry the baby straight away and take the wet towels off. Put the baby skin-to-skin on the mother and cover with a warm blanket and a hat. Hold off the first bath as policy says. Check the temperature as the protocol says. | Temperature normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "I do not know if the baby is feeding enough." **Objective** Had first feed within 1 hour Mother not sure of the feeding cues | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge (parent) related to newborn feeding cues and care as evidenced by questions. | Before discharge, the mother can describe feeding cues and how to tell the baby is getting enough. | Teach the early feeding cues: rooting, hand to mouth. Teach the signs of enough intake: 6 or more wet diapers a day after day 5, and weight gain. Teach cord care and safe sleep (on the back, in the baby's own cot). | Describes the signs: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Newborn Umbilical stump exposed | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: immature immune system and umbilical stump. | Baby stays free of infection. | Hand hygiene before touching the baby. Keep the cord clean and dry. Give vitamin K and vaccines on the national schedule. Teach the parents the signs of infection. | No infection: goal met. |

## 16.2  Preterm and Low Birth Weight Infant

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Born at 32 weeks, weight 1.5 kg Temperature 36.0 °C Very little fat under the skin | **ACTUAL** Ineffective thermoregulation related to immaturity and little subcutaneous fat as evidenced by low temperature. | Temperature kept at 36.5–37.5 °C. | Nurse in an incubator or under a radiant warmer as ordered. Once stable, use kangaroo mother care. Keep the baby covered as much as possible during procedures. Temperature every 3–4 hours. | Temperature normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Suck and swallow weak Cannot coordinate feeding well | **ACTUAL** Ineffective infant feeding dynamics related to prematurity as evidenced by weak suck and poor coordination. | Gains weight steadily. | Give expressed breast milk by tube or cup as ordered. Help the mother to express. Weigh every day and chart intake. Move on to breastfeeding as the baby can manage. | Gaining weight: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Born early Having invasive procedures | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: immature immunity and invasive procedures. | Stays free of infection. | Strict hand hygiene. Aseptic care of all lines. Watch for unstable temperature, poor feeding and lethargy. | No infection: goal met. |

## 16.3  Neonatal Jaundice and Phototherapy

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "The baby looks yellow." **Objective** Yellow down to the chest on day 3 Serum bilirubin above the treatment line Sleepy at feeds | **ACTUAL** Neonatal hyperbilirubinemia related to immature liver function and delayed feeding as evidenced by jaundice and raised bilirubin. | Bilirubin falls below the treatment threshold. | Start phototherapy as ordered, with the eyes covered. Expose as much skin as possible and change position as policy says. Feed often, at least every 3 hours. Check bilirubin as ordered. | Bilirubin falling: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother asks "Can I hold my baby?" **Objective** Baby under phototherapy Mother tearful | **ACTUAL** Anxiety (parent) related to infant treatment and separation as evidenced by questions and worry. | Mother understands the treatment and joins in with care. | Explain what phototherapy is for and roughly how long it will take. Let her hold the baby for feeds as policy allows. Encourage her to keep breastfeeding. | Mother joining in: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Under phototherapy | **RISK** Risk for deficient fluid volume as evidenced by risk factors: increased insensible losses under phototherapy. | Baby stays well hydrated. | Count wet diapers. Weigh every day. Watch the temperature. Feed at least every 3 hours. | Hydrated: goal met. |

## 16.4  Respiratory Distress Syndrome

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Grunting, nasal flaring, chest recession RR 70/min SpO₂ 85% | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to surfactant deficiency as evidenced by grunting, retractions and hypoxemia. | SpO₂ within the target range for the baby's gestation. | Give CPAP or oxygen as prescribed. Assist with giving surfactant. Follow SpO₂, RR and blood gases. Handle the baby as little as possible. | SpO₂ on target: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Working hard to breathe Grunting | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to immature lungs as evidenced by tachypnea and retractions. | Within 24–48 hours, the baby is working less hard to breathe. | Position the baby so the airway stays open. Suction only when needed. Group care together so the baby uses less oxygen. | Better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Cannot feed by mouth On IV fluids | **RISK** Risk for unstable blood glucose level as evidenced by risk factors: stress and inability to feed. | Blood glucose stays normal. | Give IV dextrose as prescribed. Check glucose as the protocol says. Start enteral feeds when ordered. | Glucose normal: goal met. |

## 16.5  Neonatal Sepsis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Temperature unstable Feeding poorly, lethargic CRP raised | **ACTUAL** Ineffective thermoregulation related to infection as evidenced by temperature instability. | Temperature stable within 48 hours of treatment. | Take blood cultures and give IV antibiotics without delay, as prescribed. Keep the baby in a neutral thermal environment. Watch vital signs. | Stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Sucks weakly Lethargic | **ACTUAL** Ineffective infant feeding dynamics related to lethargy as evidenced by poor suck. | Gets enough fluid and nutrition. | Give tube feeds or IV fluids as ordered. Chart intake and output. Weigh every day. | Intake adequate: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Septic Capillary refill 3 seconds | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: sepsis. | Perfusion stays stable. | Watch capillary refill, HR, BP and urine output. Report any deterioration at once. Give a fluid bolus as prescribed. | Stable: goal met. |

## 16.6  Birth Asphyxia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Apgar 3 at 1 minute Breathing weak | **ACTUAL** Impaired spontaneous ventilation related to hypoxic insult at birth as evidenced by poor respiratory effort. | Starts breathing effectively on its own. | Start neonatal resuscitation according to protocol. Watch HR and SpO₂. Move the baby to the neonatal unit for ongoing support. | Breathing on its own: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Seizures Tone abnormal | **ACTUAL** Decreased intracranial adaptive capacity related to hypoxic brain injury as evidenced by seizures and abnormal tone. | Seizures controlled. | Give anticonvulsants as prescribed. If the baby is eligible, start therapeutic cooling according to protocol. Watch neurological status and blood glucose. | Controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Baby in the neonatal unit Parents afraid to touch the baby | **RISK** Risk for impaired attachment as evidenced by risk factors: separation of parents and infant. | Parents visit and join in with care. | Encourage them to visit and to touch the baby. Keep the parents informed. Let them do simple care tasks. | Parents involved: goal met. |

## 16.7  Infant of a Diabetic Mother

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** RR 72/min Grunting a little, with some recession Born at 37 weeks, 4.3 kg | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to delayed lung maturity as evidenced by tachypnea, grunting and retractions. | Within 24 hours, RR 40–60/min and SpO₂ on target. | Watch RR, SpO₂ and work of breathing. Give oxygen or CPAP as prescribed. Keep the baby in a neutral thermal environment. Report any increase in distress. | Breathing normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "The baby is sleepy and does not feed well." **Objective** Jittery Sleepy at feeds Big for gestational age | **ACTUAL** Ineffective infant feeding dynamics related to lethargy and jitteriness as evidenced by poor feeding. | Feeds at least every 3 hours and takes enough. | Wake the baby for feeds every 2–3 hours. Help the mother breastfeed; give expressed milk if needed. Chart intake and output. | Feeding well: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Mother is diabetic Glucose 35 mg/dL at 2 hours old | **RISK** Risk for unstable blood glucose level as evidenced by risk factors: maternal diabetes and neonatal hyperinsulinism. | Blood glucose stays normal for age. | Feed within 1 hour of birth and often after that. Check blood glucose before feeds as the protocol says. If glucose stays low, give IV dextrose as prescribed. Watch for jitteriness, poor feeding and seizures. | Glucose normal: goal met. |

## 16.8  Neonatal Hypothermia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Temperature 35.5 °C Hands and feet cool Feeding poorly | **ACTUAL** Hypothermia related to heat loss and immature thermoregulation as evidenced by temperature 35.5 °C. | Temperature 36.5–37.5 °C within 2 hours. | Warm the baby up skin-to-skin or in an incubator. Take off any wet clothes. Check the temperature every 30 minutes until it is normal. Check blood glucose. | Temperature normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Lethargic Sucks weakly | **ACTUAL** Ineffective infant feeding dynamics related to cold stress as evidenced by poor feeding. | Feeds well again once warm. | Feed once the temperature is normal. Help with breastfeeding or give expressed milk. Chart intake. | Feeding: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Cold-stressed Lethargic | **RISK** Risk for unstable blood glucose level as evidenced by risk factors: increased glucose use in cold stress. | Blood glucose stays normal. | Check glucose. Feed early. Give IV dextrose if ordered. | Normal: goal met. |

## 16.9  Cleft Lip and Palate

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "The baby cannot suck well." **Objective** Cleft lip and palate Milk comes out of the nose during feeds | **ACTUAL** Ineffective infant feeding dynamics related to anatomical defect as evidenced by poor suck and nasal regurgitation. | Takes enough and gains weight. | Use a special feeding bottle and teat. Feed the baby sitting up. Burp often. Weigh regularly. | Gaining weight: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother asks "Why did this happen to my baby?" **Objective** Parents upset Mother does not want to hold the baby | **ACTUAL** Anxiety (parent) related to infant's defect as evidenced by distress and questions. | Parents share their feelings and can describe the plan for repair. | Explain when the operations will be done. If you have them, show photos of babies after cleft repair. Encourage them to hold the baby and bond. | Parents understand: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Gap in the palate | **RISK** Risk for aspiration as evidenced by risk factors: cleft palate. | No aspiration. | Feed the baby sitting up. Give small amounts and burp often. Watch for coughing or colour change. | No aspiration: goal met. |

**PART 17**

# Pediatric Care Plans

## 17.1  Bronchiolitis

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He is breathing fast and not feeding." **Objective** 4-month-old, RR 64/min Nasal flaring, subcostal recession Wheeze and crackles, SpO₂ 90% | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to excessive mucus and small airway inflammation as evidenced by retractions, crackles and low SpO₂. | Within 48 hours, SpO₂ 92% or more (or the local target) and less work of breathing. | Before feeds, clear the nose with saline drops and gentle suction. Give oxygen to keep SpO₂ on target. Lay him with his head slightly raised. Watch RR, SpO₂ and any sign of exhaustion or apnea. | SpO₂ on target, less distressed: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He takes only a little milk." **Objective** Taking less than half his usual feeds Fewer wet diapers than usual | **ACTUAL** Ineffective infant feeding dynamics related to breathlessness during feeds as evidenced by reduced intake and fewer wet diapers. | Stays well hydrated. | Offer small feeds, often. If he is not taking enough, give NG feeds or IV fluids as ordered. Count wet diapers and weigh every day. | Hydrated: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Young baby Chest infection | **RISK** Risk for infection (to others) as evidenced by risk factors: contagious viral infection. | Infection not passed to other babies. | Contact and droplet precautions as policy says. Strict hand hygiene. Teach the parents hand hygiene. | No cross-infection: goal met. |

## 17.2  Croup (Laryngotracheobronchitis)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He has a barking cough and noisy breathing at night." **Objective** Barking cough Stridor on breathing in, even at rest Suprasternal recession | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to laryngeal edema as evidenced by stridor and retractions. | Within 4 hours, no stridor at rest. | Keep him calm on his parent's lap and avoid anything that will upset him. Give oral or nebulised steroid and nebulised adrenaline as prescribed. If SpO₂ is low, give oxygen. Never look in his throat. Watch RR, stridor, SpO₂ and level of consciousness. | No stridor at rest: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "I am scared he will stop breathing." **Objective** Mother tearful Child crying | **ACTUAL** Anxiety (parent and child) related to breathing difficulty as evidenced by crying and expressed fear. | Parent and child look calmer. | Explain the treatment. Keep the parent with the child. Cut down noise and stimulation. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Upper airway swollen | **RISK** Risk for suffocation as evidenced by risk factors: progressive upper airway swelling. | Any worsening of the obstruction is spotted and treated early. | Report agitation, drowsiness, cyanosis, or stridor getting quieter while distress gets worse. Have emergency airway equipment ready. Never leave him alone. | No deterioration: goal met. |

## 17.3  Pneumonia in Children

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "She coughs and breathes fast." **Objective** 3-year-old, RR 48/min Crackles at the right base SpO₂ 91%, chest indrawing | **ACTUAL** Ineffective airway clearance related to inflammation and secretions as evidenced by crackles, tachypnea and chest indrawing. | Within 48 hours, SpO₂ 94% or more and less work of breathing. | Give oxygen as prescribed. Sit her up or semi-Fowler. Give antibiotics on time. Encourage fluids. Check RR, SpO₂ and recession every 2–4 hours. | Breathing better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "She feels hot." **Objective** Temperature 39 °C Flushed and irritable | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to infection as evidenced by temperature 39 °C. | Temperature below 38 °C within 4 hours. | Give the antipyretic by weight, as prescribed. Dress her lightly. Recheck the temperature after 1 hour. | Temperature down: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Fever Breathing fast Drinking less | **RISK** Risk for deficient fluid volume as evidenced by risk factors: fever, rapid breathing and reduced intake. | Stays well hydrated. | Offer drinks often. Count wet diapers or record urine output. Give IV fluids if ordered. | Hydrated: goal met. |

## 17.4  Asthma in Children

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Child says "My chest feels tight." **Objective** Wheeze, RR 40/min Can only say a few words at a time SpO₂ 92% | **ACTUAL** Ineffective breathing pattern related to bronchospasm as evidenced by wheeze, tachypnea and difficulty speaking. | Within 2 hours, talks in full sentences and SpO₂ is 94% or more. | Give the inhaled bronchodilator by spacer or nebuliser, as prescribed. Give oral or IV steroid as prescribed. Give oxygen to the SpO₂ target. Keep the child sitting up and calm, with the parent. | Breathing better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "I am not sure how to use the spacer." **Objective** Uses the spacer the wrong way Has missed preventer doses | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge (parent) related to inhaler and spacer use as evidenced by incorrect technique. | Before discharge, the parent uses the spacer correctly. | Demonstrate the spacer with a mask or mouthpiece that suits the child's age. Watch the parent do it. Give a written asthma action plan. | Technique correct: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Father smokes in the house **Objective** Exposed to dust and smoke | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: exposure to asthma triggers at home. | Parents describe how they will cut down the triggers. | No smoking in the house or the car. Teach how to keep dust down. Teach the early signs of an attack. | Describe their plan: goal met. |

## 17.5  Gastroenteritis and Dehydration

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He has had diarrhea and vomiting since yesterday." **Objective** Eyes sunken, mouth dry Skin turgor reduced Hardly any wet diapers Lost 5% of body weight | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to fluid loss from diarrhea and vomiting as evidenced by sunken eyes, dry mucosa and weight loss. | Within 8 hours, mouth moist and urine output normal. | Work out how dehydrated he is. Give oral rehydration solution in small amounts, often, as prescribed. If he is severely dehydrated, give IV fluids as ordered. Record intake, output and weight. Keep breastfeeding. | Rehydrated: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He has watery stools all day." **Objective** 8 watery stools a day Hyperactive bowel sounds | **ACTUAL** Diarrhea related to intestinal infection as evidenced by frequent watery stools. | Within 48 hours, fewer stools. | Chart his stools. Give zinc as prescribed. Keep feeding him normally for his age. Teach hand hygiene. | Fewer stools: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Many stools in the diaper | **RISK** Risk for impaired skin integrity as evidenced by risk factors: contact of stool with skin. | Skin in the diaper area stays intact. | Change diapers quickly. Clean gently and put on barrier cream. Leave the skin open to the air when you can. | Skin intact: goal met. |

## 17.6  Malnutrition in Children

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "She eats very little." **Objective** Weight-for-height below −3 SD Wasting easy to see Mid-upper arm circumference low | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to inadequate intake as evidenced by severe wasting. | Gains weight steadily in the rehabilitation phase. | Follow the therapeutic feeding protocol (start slowly). Weigh every day. Give micronutrients as prescribed. Treat any infection as ordered. | Gaining weight: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "I did not know what to feed her." **Objective** Eats mostly tea and bread No meat or eggs at all | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge (parent) related to child nutrition as evidenced by inadequate diet. | Mother plans balanced meals. | Teach which nutritious foods are available locally. Teach small meals, often. Book follow-up at the nutrition clinic. | Plans meals: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Severely malnourished Temperature 36.0 °C | **RISK** Risk for hypothermia as evidenced by risk factors: low body fat. | Temperature stays at 36.5–37.5 °C. | Keep her warm, covered and close to her mother. Watch the temperature. Check glucose. | Temperature normal: goal met. |

## 17.7  Febrile Seizures

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He shook all over for 2 minutes." **Objective** Temperature 39.5 °C 2 years old Alert now | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to infection as evidenced by temperature 39.5 °C. | Temperature below 38 °C within 2 hours. | Give the antipyretic by weight, as prescribed. Take off extra clothes. Look for the cause of the fever as ordered. | Afebrile: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "I thought he was dying." **Objective** Mother crying Asks whether he will have epilepsy | **ACTUAL** Anxiety (parent) related to child's seizure as evidenced by crying and fear. | Parent understands that febrile seizures are usually harmless and knows the first aid. | Explain what a febrile seizure is. Teach first aid: lay him on his side, put nothing in his mouth, time it, get help if it lasts more than 5 minutes. | Describes first aid: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Fever Has had a febrile seizure before | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: possible further seizure. | No injury. | Seizure precautions in place. Keep the parent informed. Keep him in a safe cot with padded sides. | No injury: goal met. |

## 17.8  Meningitis in Children

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He is very irritable and vomiting." **Objective** Baby: fontanelle bulging, high-pitched cry Temperature 39 °C Feeding poorly | **ACTUAL** Decreased intracranial adaptive capacity related to meningeal inflammation as evidenced by bulging fontanelle and high-pitched cry. | Neurological status stable. | Give IV antibiotics without delay, as prescribed. Watch level of consciousness, the fontanelle and head circumference. Keep the room quiet and dim. | Stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He is very hot." **Objective** Temperature 39 °C Flushed | **ACTUAL** Hyperthermia related to infection as evidenced by fever. | Afebrile within 48 hours. | Give antipyretics by weight. Watch the temperature. Keep him hydrated. | Afebrile: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Bacterial meningitis | **RISK** Risk for infection (to others) as evidenced by risk factors: droplet transmission. | Infection not passed on to contacts. | Droplet precautions as policy says. Inform infection control. Arrange prophylaxis for contacts as ordered. | No spread: goal met. |

## 17.9  Hydrocephalus and VP Shunt

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Head circumference growing Fontanelle bulging Eyes looking down ("sunset eyes") | **ACTUAL** Decreased intracranial adaptive capacity related to accumulation of CSF as evidenced by enlarging head and bulging fontanelle. | No signs of raised ICP after the shunt. | Measure head circumference every day. After surgery, position the baby as ordered. Watch for vomiting, irritability and a tense fontanelle. | Stable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother asks "Will my baby be normal?" **Objective** Parents anxious Lots of questions about the future | **ACTUAL** Anxiety (parent) related to infant's condition and surgery as evidenced by worry. | Parents show they understand the shunt and the plan. | Explain how the shunt works and the signs that it is not working. Encourage them to join in with care. Refer to support services. | Understand: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Shunt implanted | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: implanted shunt. | Shunt stays free of infection. | Watch the wound and the temperature. Teach the parents the signs of infection and blockage: vomiting, irritability, bulging fontanelle. Aseptic wound care. | No infection: goal met. |

## 17.10  Congenital Heart Disease (VSD and Tetralogy of Fallot)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He turns blue when he cries." **Objective** Cyanosed, SpO₂ 78% Has "tet" spells (hypercyanotic spells) Fingers clubbed | **ACTUAL** Impaired gas exchange related to right-to-left shunting as evidenced by cyanosis and low SpO₂. | Spells happen less often and do not last as long. | During a spell: put him in the knee-chest position and give oxygen and medication as prescribed. Keep him calm. Do not let him get dehydrated. Get him ready for surgical repair. | Spells controlled: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He gets tired and sweaty when feeding." **Objective** Not gaining weight well Sweats during feeds | **ACTUAL** Ineffective infant feeding dynamics related to fatigue during feeding as evidenced by sweating and poor weight gain. | Gains weight steadily. | Small feeds, often. High-calorie feeds as prescribed. Let him rest before feeds. Weigh regularly. | Gaining weight: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Heart defect Gets chest infections often | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: congenital heart defect (endocarditis, respiratory infections). | No infection. | Make sure his immunisations are done. Teach dental care. Tell the parents to report fever. | No infection: goal met. |

## 17.11  Rheumatic Fever

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Child says "My knees and ankles hurt." **Objective** Arthritis moving from joint to joint ASO titre raised | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to joint inflammation as evidenced by painful swollen joints. | Pain relieved within 48 hours. | Give the anti-inflammatory as prescribed. Support the joints with pillows. Bed rest in the acute phase. | Pain relieved: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Child says "I get tired quickly." **Objective** Heart rate fast New murmur heard | **ACTUAL** Activity intolerance related to carditis as evidenced by tachycardia on minimal activity. | Takes part in activities within his limits. | Make sure he rests as ordered. Give him quiet games to play. Watch the HR. | Copes with activity: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Needs prophylaxis long term Has missed one injection | **RISK** Risk for ineffective health self-management as evidenced by risk factors: long-term penicillin prophylaxis. | Prophylaxis given on schedule. | Teach the parents why every dose matters. Give them a card with the schedule. | On schedule: goal met. |

## 17.12  Nephrotic Syndrome in Children

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "His eyes and belly are swollen." **Objective** Swollen around the eyes and all over A lot of protein in the urine Albumin low | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to protein loss and low plasma oncotic pressure as evidenced by edema and proteinuria. | Within 2–4 weeks of steroids, less edema and no protein in the urine. | Weigh every day and measure abdominal girth. Test the urine for protein every day. Give steroids as prescribed. Limit salt as ordered. | Less edema: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Child says "Other children laugh at my face." **Objective** Round "moon" face from the steroids Will not go to school | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to edema and steroid effects as evidenced by distress about appearance. | Talks about how he feels and keeps up his social activities. | Explain that the changes will not last. When he is well, encourage him to go to school. | Coping: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On steroids Losing immunoglobulins | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: steroid therapy and protein loss. | No infection. | Tell the parents to report fever and abdominal pain (peritonitis). Keep him away from chickenpox and measles. Hand hygiene. | No infection: goal met. |

## 17.13  Acute Glomerulonephritis in Children

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "His urine is dark like tea." **Objective** Blood in the urine Puffy around the eyes BP 130/90 mmHg Passing little urine | **ACTUAL** Excess fluid volume related to reduced glomerular filtration as evidenced by edema, hypertension and oliguria. | Within 1 week, BP and urine output normal. | Daily weight and a strict I&O chart. Limit fluid and salt as prescribed. BP every 4 hours. Give the antihypertensive as ordered. | BP and output normal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother asks "Can he eat normally?" **Objective** Has questions about his diet Cannot say what he should avoid | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge (parent) related to dietary restrictions as evidenced by questions. | Parent can describe the salt and fluid limits. | Explain the salt and fluid limits. Explain how recovery is expected to go. | Describes them: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Hypertensive BP 140/95 mmHg | **RISK** Risk for ineffective cerebral tissue perfusion as evidenced by risk factors: severe hypertension (encephalopathy). | No neurological complications. | Report headache, vomiting, changes in vision or seizures. Watch the BP. | No complication: goal met. |

## 17.14  Type 1 Diabetes in Children

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother asks "How will we manage injections at school?" **Objective** Just diagnosed, 8 years old Parents cannot explain how to use insulin | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge (parent and child) related to new diagnosis of diabetes as evidenced by questions. | Parents test glucose and give insulin correctly; the child can name the signs of a hypo. | Teach glucose testing and insulin injection. Teach how to recognise and treat a hypo. Talk to the school nurse or teacher. Teach the sick-day rules. | Show you correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Child says "I do not want the needle." **Objective** Cries at injections Hides when the insulin comes out | **ACTUAL** Fear related to repeated injections as evidenced by crying and refusal. | Accepts injections with support. | Explain things at his level and use play. Give him some control (let him choose the site). Praise him when he cooperates. | Accepts injections: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On insulin Activity varies from day to day | **RISK** Risk for unstable blood glucose level as evidenced by risk factors: insulin therapy and variable activity and intake. | Blood glucose on target most of the time. | Go over the glucose record with the family. Adjust the plan with the diabetes team. | On target: goal met. |

| **Subjective** Mother says "She gets tired and pale before her transfusion." **Objective** Hb 7 g/dL Pale Spleen enlarged | **ACTUAL** Activity intolerance related to chronic anemia as evidenced by fatigue and low Hb. | Keeps up her usual activity after the transfusion. | Give the transfusion as prescribed, with all safety checks. Watch for reactions. Plan rest. | Activity kept up: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "The chelation pump is hard to use every night." **Objective** Ferritin high Has missed doses | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to burden of chelation therapy as evidenced by missed doses and high ferritin. | Chelation given regularly. | Talk to the team about oral chelation. Teach the family and support them. | Taking it more regularly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Spleen removed Has had many transfusions | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: splenectomy and repeated transfusions. | No serious infection. | Make sure her vaccinations are done. Distract him with play. Keep the burns covered. | Comfortable: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He has not passed urine much." **Objective** Urine output low Heart rate fast | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to fluid loss through burns as evidenced by low urine output. | Urine output 1 mL/kg/hour within 8 hours. | Give fluids by the burn formula as prescribed. Record urine output every hour. | Output on target: goal met. |

## 17.18  Accidental Poisoning in Children

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He drank something from the cupboard." **Objective** Drank an unknown liquid Medicines and chemicals within the child's reach at home | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge (parent) related to safe storage of medicines and chemicals as evidenced by hazardous home storage. | Before discharge, the parent can explain how to store things safely. | Teach her to keep medicines and chemicals locked away and out of reach. Keep products in their original containers. Give her the poison information number. | Explains the measures: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "It is my fault." **Objective** Mother crying Blames herself | **ACTUAL** Anxiety (parent) related to child's poisoning and guilt as evidenced by crying and self-blame. | Mother talks about her feelings and feels supported. | Support her without judging. Keep her informed. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Toddler who explores everything Things stored unsafely | **RISK** Risk for poisoning as evidenced by risk factors: accessible medicines and chemicals at home. | No further poisoning. | Teach safety at home. Book follow-up. Teach the use of child-resistant containers. | No repeat: goal met. |

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He vomits forcefully after every feed." **Objective** Projectile vomiting, not bile-stained Olive-shaped mass felt Fontanelle sunken, few wet diapers | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to persistent vomiting as evidenced by sunken fontanelle and reduced wet diapers. | Rehydrated, with normal electrolytes, before surgery. | Nothing by mouth; give IV fluids as prescribed. Follow electrolytes and acid–base status. Record vomiting, I&O and weight. | Rehydrated: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Losing weight Hungry straight after vomiting | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to vomiting as evidenced by weight loss. | Tolerates feeds after pyloromyotomy. | After surgery, bring feeds back as ordered. Burp him often. Weigh every day. | Tolerating feeds: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Losing gastric acid | **RISK** Risk for electrolyte imbalance as evidenced by risk factors: vomiting of gastric contents (metabolic alkalosis, hypokalemia). | Electrolytes and acid–base balance back to normal before surgery. | Follow electrolytes. Replace as prescribed. | Normal: goal met. |

Nothing by mouth. Get him ready for air or contrast enema reduction, or surgery. | Pain relieved: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "He keeps vomiting." **Objective** Vomiting Lethargic | **ACTUAL** Deficient fluid volume related to vomiting and bowel fluid sequestration as evidenced by lethargy and dry mucosa. | Rehydrated within 8 hours. | Give IV fluids as prescribed. Keep an I&O chart. Watch vital signs. | Rehydrated: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Bowel ischemic Abdomen distended | **RISK** Risk for shock as evidenced by risk factors: bowel ischemia and perforation. | Vital signs stay stable. | Watch vital signs. Report abdominal distension, fever or blood in the stool. If reduction does not work, get him ready for surgery. | Stable: goal met. |

Weigh regularly. Refer to the dietitian. | Gaining: goal met. |
