**PART 18**

# Mental Health Care Plans

## 18.1  Generalized Anxiety Disorder

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I worry about everything all the time." "I cannot relax." **Objective** Restless, muscles tense HR 100/min Finds it hard to concentrate during the interview | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to persistent excessive worry as evidenced by restlessness, muscle tension and poor concentration. | Within 1 week, can name two things that trigger her anxiety and uses one relaxation technique. | Build trust with her; keep your manner calm and clear. Help her spot her triggers and the first signs that anxiety is building. Teach slow breathing and progressive muscle relaxation. Give medication as prescribed and watch how it works. Refer for psychological therapy (CBT, for example). | Uses the technique and says she is less anxious: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I lie awake thinking." **Objective** Sleeps 4 hours a night Tired | **ACTUAL** Insomnia related to anxious thoughts as evidenced by difficulty falling asleep and fatigue. | Sleeps at least 6 hours. | Teach good sleep habits: regular bedtimes, no caffeine after the afternoon, no screens before bed. Teach a relaxation routine for bedtime. No naps in the day. | Sleeping better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Only coffee and cigarettes calm me." **Objective** Smoking more and drinking more caffeine | **RISK** Risk for ineffective health self-management as evidenced by risk factors: use of caffeine and nicotine to manage anxiety. | Can name healthier ways of coping. | Talk about how caffeine and nicotine make anxiety worse. Support her plan to cut down. Encourage regular physical activity. | Coping in healthier ways: goal met. |

## 18.2  Panic Disorder

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My heart races and I feel I am dying." **Objective** Attacks come on suddenly and last 10 minutes Overbreathing, sweating, shaking ECG normal | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to panic attacks as evidenced by palpitations, hyperventilation and fear of dying. | Within 1 week, uses breathing techniques to cut the attacks short. | Stay with him through an attack and speak calmly, in short sentences. Breathe slowly with him. Cut down stimulation. Once the attack is over, talk about what set it off. Give medication as prescribed. | Uses the techniques: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I no longer go to the market alone." **Objective** Stays away from crowded places At home most days | **ACTUAL** Ineffective coping related to fear of attacks as evidenced by avoidance of public places. | Slowly goes back to the situations he has been avoiding. | Support graded exposure as the therapist has planned it. Praise every step forward. | Back to his activities: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Having attacks often Avoiding places | **RISK** Risk for loneliness as evidenced by risk factors: avoidance of social situations. | Keeps in regular touch with family and friends. | Encourage him to keep in touch with family and friends. Bring the family in to support him. Suggest a support group. | Keeps in touch: goal met. |

## 18.3  Obsessive-Compulsive Disorder

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I must wash my hands 50 times or something bad will happen." **Objective** Hands red and cracked Spends hours washing every day | **ACTUAL** Ineffective coping related to obsessive thoughts as evidenced by repetitive hand washing that interferes with daily life. | Slowly cuts down the time spent on rituals. | Do not stop the rituals suddenly; agree limits that come down bit by bit. Support exposure and response prevention therapy. Give medication as prescribed. Praise progress. | Fewer rituals: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My hands burn." **Objective** Skin on the hands cracked Red | **ACTUAL** Impaired skin integrity related to repeated washing as evidenced by cracked, red skin. | Skin on the hands heals within 2 weeks. | Give an emollient after every wash. Suggest a mild soap and drying gently. Look at the skin every day. | Healing: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Rituals take up a lot of time Late for appointments | **RISK** Risk for ineffective health self-management as evidenced by risk factors: rituals interfering with daily tasks and treatment. | Gets through her daily tasks. | Give the day a structure with set times. Support her goals at work or study. | Tasks done: goal met. |

## 18.4  Post-Traumatic Stress Disorder

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I keep seeing the explosion in my dreams." "I jump at any loud sound." **Objective** Nightmares and flashbacks Hypervigilance Jumps at sudden noise | **ACTUAL** Post-trauma syndrome related to exposure to a traumatic event as evidenced by flashbacks, nightmares and hypervigilance. | Within 2 weeks, fewer intrusive symptoms and uses grounding techniques. | Keep his surroundings safe and predictable. Listen, and never push him for details of the trauma. Teach grounding and breathing techniques. Give medication as prescribed. Refer for trauma-focused therapy. | Uses the techniques, fewer symptoms: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I sleep 2 or 3 hours only." **Objective** Exhausted Irritable | **ACTUAL** Disturbed sleep pattern related to nightmares as evidenced by short sleep and irritability. | Sleeping better within 2 weeks. | Good sleep habits. A relaxation routine before bed. If ordered, give the prescribed medicine for nightmares. | Better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Sometimes I drink to forget." **Objective** Drinking more | **RISK** Risk for ineffective health self-management as evidenced by risk factors: use of alcohol to cope with symptoms. | Can name safer ways of coping. | Talk about how alcohol makes the symptoms worse. Offer a referral to addiction services. Bring in the people who support him. | Names his strategies: goal met. |

## 18.5  Major Depressive Disorder

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Nothing will ever get better." "I am a burden." **Objective** Flat affect, speaks slowly Withdrawn, stays in bed Hardly makes eye contact | **ACTUAL** Hopelessness related to depressive illness as evidenced by negative statements about the future and withdrawal. | Within 1 week, names one thing she hopes for in the future. | Assess her mood and her suicide risk on admission and regularly after that. Spend short periods with her, often. Encourage small activities she can manage. Give antidepressants as prescribed and watch how they work. Refer for psychotherapy. | Names a goal: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I do not feel like eating or washing." **Objective** Lost 4 kg Not washing | **ACTUAL** Self-neglect related to low mood and lack of energy as evidenced by poor hygiene and reduced intake. | Washes every day and eats at least half of each meal. | Set up a simple daily routine. Offer small meals and foods she likes. Help and encourage her to wash, without doing it all for her. | Looking after herself better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Sometimes I wish I would not wake up." **Objective** Feels hopeless Cut off from others | **RISK** Risk for suicidal behavior as evidenced by risk factors: depression, hopelessness and expressed wish to die. | Stays safe and tells staff when the thoughts get stronger. | Ask her directly about suicidal thoughts and plans. Remove dangerous items as policy says. Observe her at the level ordered. Agree with her how she will ask for help. | Safe: goal met. |

## 18.6  Bipolar Disorder (Manic Episode)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I do not need sleep; I have great plans." **Objective** Speech pressured Sleeps 2 hours a night Constantly on the go | **ACTUAL** Insomnia related to manic hyperactivity as evidenced by sleep of 2 hours and continuous activity. | Within 1 week, sleeps at least 6 hours. | Cut down stimulation, especially in the evening. Give the mood stabiliser and other drugs as prescribed. Encourage quiet activities. Record how many hours he sleeps. | Sleeping better: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I have no time to eat." **Objective** Too busy to eat Lost 3 kg | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to hyperactivity and poor attention to meals as evidenced by weight loss. | Weight steady. | Offer high-calorie finger foods and drinks he can take on the move. Chart what he eats. Weigh twice a week. | Weight steady: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Impulsive Judgment poor On lithium | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: impulsive behaviour, exhaustion and possible lithium toxicity. | No injury, and lithium level within the therapeutic range. | Set clear limits and keep them the same. Check lithium levels and watch for signs of toxicity (vomiting, tremor, confusion). Keep his fluid and salt intake steady. | No injury: goal met. |

## 18.7  Schizophrenia

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "The voices tell me people are poisoning my food." **Objective** Hearing voices Suspicious, refuses meals Talks to people no one else can see | **ACTUAL** Impaired verbal communication related to disordered thinking and hallucinations as evidenced by responding to voices and incoherent speech. | Gets his needs across clearly and says the distressing voices are less. | Speak in short, clear sentences. Do not argue with the delusions; acknowledge how he feels and gently present reality. Ask what the voices are saying, especially any commands to harm. Give antipsychotics as prescribed and watch for side effects. If he is suspicious, offer sealed food or let him serve himself. | Gets his needs across: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I prefer to stay alone." **Objective** Keeps away from others Stays in his room | **ACTUAL** Social isolation related to withdrawal and suspiciousness as evidenced by avoiding others. | Joins one group activity a day. | Start with one-to-one contact. Bring him into small groups bit by bit. Praise him for joining in. | Joins an activity: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Hears voices telling him what to do Agitated | **RISK** Risk for other-directed violence as evidenced by risk factors: command hallucinations and suspiciousness. | Does not harm anyone. | Watch for early signs of agitation. Use de-escalation. If ordered, follow the policy for rapid tranquillisation. | No violence: goal met. |

## 18.8  Suicide Risk

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have been thinking about ending my life." **Objective** Has had a recent loss Feels hopeless Giving his things away | **ACTUAL** Hopelessness related to recent loss and depression as evidenced by expressed wish to die and giving away belongings. | Can name reasons for living and engages with treatment. | Ask directly and calmly about thoughts, plans and means. Listen without judging. Bring in the mental health team straight away. Explore his reasons for living and who supports him. | Engages with treatment: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "No one would miss me." **Objective** Lives on his own No contact with his family | **ACTUAL** Social isolation related to withdrawal from others as evidenced by living alone and lack of contact. | Within 48 hours, names at least one person who can support him. | With his consent, bring in family or friends. Arrange follow-up contacts. | Names support: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has said he intends to Feels hopeless | **RISK** Risk for suicidal behavior as evidenced by risk factors: expressed suicidal thoughts and hopelessness. | Stays safe. | Observe him at the level ordered. Remove dangerous items as policy says. Work out a safety plan with him. Make sure follow-up is in place after discharge. | Safe: goal met. |

## 18.9  Aggressive Behaviour

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Leave me alone or I will hit you." **Objective** Shouting, fists clenched Pacing up and down | **ACTUAL** Ineffective impulse control related to anger and poor coping as evidenced by threats and agitation. | Expresses his anger in words. | Approach calmly, with an open posture, and keep a safe distance. De-escalate: listen, acknowledge his feelings, give him choices. Cut down stimulation and move other people away. Give medication as prescribed if it is needed. | Expresses anger safely: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Nobody listens to me." **Objective** Frustrated Shouts when he does not get what he asks for | **ACTUAL** Ineffective coping related to frustration as evidenced by aggressive outbursts. | Can name two other ways to deal with his anger. | Once he is calm, talk about what set him off. Teach anger management techniques. Praise him when he uses them. | Uses the alternatives: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has been violent before | **RISK** Risk for other-directed violence as evidenced by risk factors: history of violence and current agitation. | No harm to himself or anyone else. | Use restraint or seclusion only as a last resort, and only as policy allows. Document his behaviour and what was done. Debrief after any incident. | No harm: goal met. |

## 18.10  Opioid and Drug Withdrawal

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My whole body aches and I cannot stop yawning." **Objective** Sweating, pupils dilated, goosebumps Vomiting, diarrhea Last used 18 hours ago | **ACTUAL** Acute substance withdrawal syndrome related to abrupt cessation of opioids as evidenced by sweating, dilated pupils, vomiting and aches. | Gets through withdrawal safely, with symptoms he can tolerate. | Score the withdrawal with a validated scale. Give substitution or symptom-relief medication as prescribed. Keep him hydrated. Keep his surroundings calm. | Withdrawal safe: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I want to stop but I am afraid I will use again." **Objective** Has relapsed before Unsure about treatment | **ACTUAL** Ineffective coping related to substance dependence as evidenced by repeated relapse. | Can name what triggers a relapse and who will support him. | Use motivational interviewing. Refer to addiction services. Warn him that the risk of overdose is higher after a period without using. | Describes his plan: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Vomiting and diarrhea | **RISK** Risk for deficient fluid volume as evidenced by risk factors: vomiting and diarrhea during withdrawal. | Stays well hydrated. | Keep an I&O chart. Encourage drinking; give IV fluids if ordered. Give the antiemetic and antidiarrheal as prescribed. | Hydrated: goal met. |

## 18.11  Alcohol Withdrawal

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "My hands shake and I see insects on the wall." **Objective** Shaking, sweating, HR 118/min Seeing things that are not there Last drink 48 hours ago | **ACTUAL** Acute substance withdrawal syndrome related to abrupt cessation of alcohol as evidenced by tremor, tachycardia and hallucinations. | Gets through withdrawal with no seizures and no delirium. | Score the withdrawal with a validated scale (CIWA-Ar, for example). Give benzodiazepines as prescribed, according to the score. Give thiamine before any glucose, as prescribed. Watch vital signs and level of consciousness. | Withdrawal safe: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "There are people in my room." **Objective** Disoriented at night Hallucinating | **ACTUAL** Acute confusion related to alcohol withdrawal as evidenced by disorientation and hallucinations. | Oriented within 72 hours. | Keep his room well lit and quiet. Reorient him often. Keep him under close observation. | Oriented: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Drinks heavily Has had seizures in withdrawal before | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: withdrawal seizures and confusion. | No injury. | Seizure precautions in place. Fall precautions in place. Give the benzodiazepine according to the score, as prescribed. | No injury: goal met. |

## 18.12  Eating Disorders

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I am still too fat." **Objective** BMI well below normal Restricts what she eats Exercises far too much | **ACTUAL** Imbalanced nutrition: less than body requirements related to restriction of intake as evidenced by very low BMI and food restriction. | Follows the meal plan and gains weight as the treatment team has set. | Follow the specialist meal plan; supervise meals and the time after them. Weigh her according to protocol, the same way each time. Do not argue about food; keep your approach calm and the same every time. Work with the dietitian and the mental health team. | Following the plan: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I hate my body." **Objective** Sees her body as bigger than it is Checks herself in the mirror often | **ACTUAL** Disturbed body image related to distorted perception of body size as evidenced by negative statements despite low weight. | Talks about how she feels and engages with therapy. | Talk about her feelings, not her weight. Support her psychological therapy. | Engages: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has restricted severely Starting nutritional rehabilitation | **RISK** Risk for electrolyte imbalance as evidenced by risk factors: malnutrition and refeeding syndrome. | Electrolytes stay normal. | At the start, check phosphate, potassium and magnesium every day. Check the ECG as ordered. Give supplements as prescribed. | Values normal: goal met. |

## 18.13  Borderline Personality Disorder

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Everyone abandons me." "I cut myself when I feel empty." **Objective** Old and new superficial cuts on the forearm Mood changes quickly | **ACTUAL** Ineffective impulse control related to intense emotions as evidenced by self-injury and rapid mood changes. | Uses other ways of coping when distressed. | Keep boundaries clear and the same. Help her recognise her triggers and early warning signs. Support the skills she learns in dialectical behaviour therapy. If she self-injures, respond calmly and without punishment. | Uses alternatives: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "The day staff are good, the night staff are bad." **Objective** Relationships unstable Plays staff off against each other (splitting) | **ACTUAL** Ineffective coping related to emotional instability as evidenced by unstable relationships. | Keeps steady relationships with staff. | The whole team takes the same approach. Agree one care plan that every member of staff follows. Set clear boundaries, kindly. | Consistent: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has self-injured before | **RISK** Risk for self-mutilation as evidenced by risk factors: history of self-injury and emotional distress. | Stays safe. | Assess her risk regularly. Remove dangerous items as policy says. Agree a safety plan with her. | Safe: goal met. |

## 18.14  Electroconvulsive Therapy (ECT)

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "Will the electricity hurt my brain?" **Objective** First ECT booked Restless | **ACTUAL** Anxiety related to ECT procedure as evidenced by fear and questions. | Says he is less anxious before treatment. | Explain the procedure, the anaesthetic and what effects to expect. Answer his questions honestly. Make sure consent is obtained as the law and policy require. | Calmer: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Will I lose my memory?" **Objective** Asks about side effects Cannot say how to prepare | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to ECT preparation and side effects as evidenced by questions. | Can describe how to prepare and what effects to expect. | Teach fasting and taking out dentures and jewellery. Explain that he may have short-term memory loss and a headache. | Describes them: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has an anaesthetic Confused after treatment | **RISK** Risk for acute confusion as evidenced by risk factors: anesthesia and seizure induction. | Oriented once he has recovered from each treatment. | Watch vital signs and the airway after treatment. Reorient him. Stay with him until he is fully alert. | Oriented: goal met. |

## 18.15  ADHD and Autism Spectrum Disorder in Children

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Teacher says "He cannot sit still and interrupts others." **Objective** Cannot pay attention Overactive Acts on impulse | **ACTUAL** Ineffective impulse control related to neurodevelopmental disorder as evidenced by interrupting, hyperactivity and acting without thinking. | Follows a simple routine and has fewer disruptive episodes. | Give simple instructions, one step at a time. Keep the daily routine the same. Praise the behaviour you want straight away. Give medication as prescribed and watch his appetite and sleep. | Fewer episodes: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Mother says "She does not look at people or play with others." **Objective** Hardly makes eye contact Plays the same way over and over Upset by change | **ACTUAL** Impaired social interaction related to autism spectrum disorder as evidenced by poor eye contact and limited play with others. | Tolerates structured interaction with someone she knows. | Keep her surroundings predictable. Use visual schedules. Refer to speech and behavioural therapy. | Tolerates interaction: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Acts on impulse Does not see danger | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: impulsivity and limited awareness of danger. | No injury. | Teach the parents about safety at home. Watch closely near roads and water. | No injury: goal met. |

**PART 19**

# Community and Older Adult Care Plans

## 19.1  Home Care After Discharge

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** His wife says "I do not know how to change his dressing or give his injections." **Objective** Sent home with a wound and on insulin His wife does most of the caring Medicines kept the wrong way | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge (caregiver) related to home care procedures as evidenced by questions and incorrect storage of medicines. | Within 3 home visits, his wife does the wound care and injections correctly. | Look at the home and at what the caregiver can manage. Teach each procedure and watch her do it back. Write out a medication schedule. Set up regular home visits and give contact numbers. | Does it correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "The house is not suitable for a wheelchair." **Objective** Steps at the front door Bathroom too small for a wheelchair | **ACTUAL** Ineffective home maintenance behaviors related to environmental barriers and reduced mobility as evidenced by unsuitable home layout. | Within 2 weeks the family has made basic changes to the home. | Suggest ramps and grab bars, and taking up rugs. Refer to occupational therapy and social services. | Changes made: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Patient depends on others for care Only one person caring for him | **RISK** Risk for caregiver role strain as evidenced by risk factors: high care demands and lack of support. | Caregiver names where she can get respite. | Ask about the caregiver's own health and stress. Bring other family members in. Put her in touch with community services. | Respite found: goal met. |

## 19.2  Family Health Assessment

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Mother says "Since my husband lost his job, we argue all the time." **Objective** Money worries Children doing worse at school | **ACTUAL** Interrupted family processes related to unemployment and financial stress as evidenced by frequent conflict and changes in children's behaviour. | Family agree on one plan together for managing stress. | Do a family assessment (genogram, ecomap). Encourage family members to talk openly with each other. Refer to social support services. Follow up at the times agreed. | Shared plan agreed: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "We eat fast food most days." **Objective** Two family members overweight Hardly any exercise | **ACTUAL** Ineffective family health self-management related to limited knowledge of healthy lifestyle as evidenced by poor diet and inactivity. | Family agree on two healthy changes. | Teach balanced meals using local foods. Encourage the family to be active together. Set small goals with them. | Changes agreed: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Diabetes and hypertension run in the family No one has been screened | **RISK** Risk for ineffective health self-management as evidenced by risk factors: family history and lifestyle. | Adults in the family go for screening within 1 month. | Arrange BP and glucose screening for the adults. Teach the early signs of disease. | Screening done: goal met. |

## 19.3  School Health

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Teacher says "Many children come to school without breakfast and complain of headaches." **Objective** Screened pupils have low hemoglobin Few places to wash hands | **ACTUAL** Deficient community health related to limited health programmes in the school as evidenced by anemia and poor hygiene facilities. | Within one term, the school starts a health education and screening programme. | Work out the school's health needs with the staff. Organise screening for vision, hearing, growth and anemia. Teach hygiene and nutrition in sessions suited to each age. Work with parents and the school administration. | Programme started: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Pupils say "We do not know how to wash hands properly." **Objective** Washing hands the wrong way No soap in the toilets | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to hand hygiene as evidenced by incorrect technique. | Pupils wash their hands correctly. | Show them the hand washing steps. Use songs or posters. Push for soap and water to be available. | Shown correctly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Classrooms crowded Poor sanitation | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: crowding and poor sanitation. | School reports fewer absences because of infection. | Check immunisation status. Teach how to cough and sneeze safely. Report any outbreak to the health authorities. | Fewer absences: goal met. |

## 19.4  Occupational Health

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Workers say "We do not wear masks; it is too hot." **Objective** Factory air is dusty Workers coughing | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to occupational hazards and protective equipment as evidenced by not using masks. | Workers wear their protective equipment every time. | Teach the dangers of dust and chemicals. Show how to wear masks and protective equipment properly. Work with management to provide equipment that suits the job. | Equipment worn: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "My back hurts after lifting." **Objective** Lifting heavy loads by hand No lifting aids | **ACTUAL** Acute pain related to poor lifting technique as evidenced by back pain after work. | Within 4 weeks, workers report less back pain. | Teach safe lifting and how to use aids. Encourage breaks and stretching. Refer anyone whose pain does not settle. | Less pain: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Machinery Long shifts | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: unsafe machinery and fatigue. | No injuries at work during the monitoring period. | Promote safety training. Report hazards and get them fixed. Provide first aid training. | No injuries: goal met. |

## 19.5  Communicable Disease Control

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Residents say "Many children have diarrhea this week." **Objective** A cluster of diarrhea cases Water supply unsafe | **ACTUAL** Deficient community health related to unsafe water and sanitation as evidenced by a cluster of diarrhea cases. | Fewer new cases within 2 weeks. | Inform the public health authorities. Teach people to boil or chlorinate their water. Hand out oral rehydration salts and teach how to make them up. Promote hand hygiene and safe food handling. | Cases falling: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "Vaccines are not necessary." **Objective** Few children immunised Rumours that vaccines are harmful | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to benefits of immunization as evidenced by refusal of vaccines. | More people vaccinated within 3 months. | Give clear information. Work with community leaders. Organise outreach sessions. | More vaccinated: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Outbreak in the community | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: ongoing transmission. | Outbreak under control within 4 weeks. | Find cases and trace contacts. Give advice on isolation. Report cases to the public health authorities. | Controlled: goal met. |

## 19.6  Chronic Disease Management in the Community

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I take my blood pressure tablets only when I have a headache." **Objective** BP 162/98 mmHg Does not come to clinic regularly | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to misunderstanding of chronic illness as evidenced by irregular medication use and uncontrolled BP. | Takes his medicines every day and comes to follow-up. | Explain that this is a long-term disease. Use a pill organiser and reminders. Book regular clinic visits. Bring in the family. | Taking medicines, BP improving: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot afford the medicines every month." **Objective** Misses medicines because of the cost BP not controlled | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health maintenance behaviors related to limited resources as evidenced by missed medicines due to cost. | Can get medicines he can afford. | Link him to free or subsidised programmes. Ask the prescriber about cheaper options. | Access arranged: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Diabetic, treatment irregular Fasting glucose 180 mg/dL | **RISK** Risk for unstable blood glucose level as evidenced by risk factors: diabetes with irregular treatment. | Blood glucose stays on target. | Arrange regular glucose checks. Give diet advice. Go over his medicines at every visit. | On target: goal met. |

## 19.7  Disaster Nursing

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Survivors say "We lost our homes; we do not know where to go." **Objective** Families displaced into shelters Crowded, not enough water | **ACTUAL** Ineffective community coping related to sudden disaster as evidenced by displacement and lack of organized resources. | Within days, basic needs met and services organised. | Take part in triage and first aid. Work with relief agencies on shelter, water and food. Set up health surveillance. Identify the most vulnerable: children, pregnant women, older adults, disabled people. | Services organised: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I cannot stop thinking about what happened." **Objective** Survivors distressed and not sleeping Children clinging to their parents | **ACTUAL** Post-trauma syndrome related to disaster experience as evidenced by intrusive thoughts and insomnia. | Within days, survivors receive psychological first aid. | Give psychological first aid. Bring families back together. Refer severe cases to mental health services. | Support given: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Shelters crowded Water unsafe | **RISK** Risk for infection as evidenced by risk factors: crowding and poor sanitation. | No outbreaks in the shelters. | Promote safe water and sanitation. Keep immunisation services running. Watch for outbreaks. | No outbreaks: goal met. |

## 19.8  Falls in Older Adults at Home

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I fell twice this year in the bathroom." **Objective** 78 years old Walks slowly, holds on to furniture Home poorly lit | **ACTUAL** Impaired walking related to muscle weakness and poor balance as evidenced by slow, unsteady gait. | Within 2 weeks, walks safely with an aid. | Check her gait, her balance and her home. Give her a walking aid and show her how to use it. Encourage balance and strength exercises. Go through any medicines that make her dizzy with the doctor. | Walking safely: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I did not know my slippers were dangerous." **Objective** Slippers loose Rugs on the floor | **ACTUAL** Deficient knowledge related to home fall hazards as evidenced by unsafe footwear and rugs. | Can describe how to make her home safer. | Teach the need for footwear that fits well. Take up rugs; put in grab bars and night lights. Check the home again at the next visit. | Describes the measures: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Has fallen before Elderly | **RISK** Risk for adult falls as evidenced by risk factors: history of falls, older age and home hazards. | No more falls. | Teach her how to get up after a fall and how to call for help. Book follow-up visits. Suggest a personal alarm. | No falls: goal met. |

## 19.9  Polypharmacy in Older Adults

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I take 11 tablets and I am not sure what each is for." **Objective** Several doctors prescribing Duplicate medicines found | **ACTUAL** Ineffective health self-management related to complex medication regimen as evidenced by confusion about medicines and duplicates. | Regimen made simpler and the patient understands it. | Review his medicines with the doctor or pharmacist. Write out a schedule. Use a pill organiser. Get rid of expired and duplicate medicines. | Understands the regimen: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel dizzy in the morning." **Objective** BP drops when he stands Unsteady when he gets up | **ACTUAL** Activity intolerance related to medication side effects as evidenced by dizziness on rising. | Within 1 week, no dizziness when he gets up. | Report the side effects to the prescriber. Teach him to get up slowly. Check BP lying and standing. | No dizziness: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** On many drugs Kidney function reduced | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: drug interactions and adverse effects. | No adverse drug events. | Arrange regular medication reviews. Tell him to report any new symptom. Keep one up-to-date list of all his medicines. | No events: goal met. |

## 19.10  Cognitive Decline in Older Adults at Home

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** Her daughter says "She forgets to eat and leaves the stove on." **Objective** Forgetful Does not know the date | **ACTUAL** Chronic confusion related to progressive cognitive impairment as evidenced by memory loss and disorientation. | A safe daily routine at home. | Set up a fixed routine. Use calendars, labels and reminders. Arrange for someone to supervise cooking and medicines. | Routine safe: goal met. |
| **Subjective** Her daughter says "She forgets to eat." **Objective** Losing weight Skipping meals | **ACTUAL** Self-neglect related to cognitive impairment as evidenced by missed meals and weight loss. | Eats regular meals. | Arrange help with meals or meal delivery. Watch her weight. Use reminders at mealtimes. | Eating regularly: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Leaves the stove on Wanders off | **RISK** Risk for injury as evidenced by risk factors: impaired judgment and wandering. | No injury. | Fit safety devices (stove cut-off, door alarms). Have her wear an identification bracelet. Teach the family how to supervise her. | No injury: goal met. |

## 19.11  Incontinence in Older Adults at Home

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I cannot reach the toilet in time at night." **Objective** Urge incontinence Up twice a night to pass urine | **ACTUAL** Urge urinary incontinence related to overactive bladder as evidenced by leakage before reaching the toilet. | Within 4 weeks, fewer episodes. | Keep a bladder diary. Bladder training with voiding at set times. Cut down caffeine. A commode by the bed at night. | Fewer episodes: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I am ashamed." **Objective** Avoids visitors Uses a lot of pads | **ACTUAL** Situational low self-esteem related to incontinence as evidenced by shame and avoiding visitors. | Talks about how she feels and accepts help. | Talk about discreet continence products. Talk with her supportively. Reassure her that treatment can help. | Accepts help: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Skin damp Perineum red | **RISK** Risk for impaired skin integrity as evidenced by risk factors: moisture. | Skin stays intact. | Teach skin care and the use of barrier cream. Change pads quickly. Look at the skin every day. | Intact: goal met. |

## 19.12  Caregiver Role Strain

| **Assessment** | **Nursing Diagnosis** | **Plan / Expected Outcome** | **Nursing Interventions** | **Evaluation** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Subjective** "I have not slept well for months caring for my father." **Objective** Caregiver worn out Not looking after her own health | **ACTUAL** Caregiver role strain related to continuous care demands as evidenced by exhaustion and neglect of own health. | Caregiver finds one respite option and uses it. | Ask about the caregiver's stress. Share the caring among the family. Put her in touch with respite and community services. Encourage her to look after her own health. | Using respite: goal met. |
| **Subjective** "I feel guilty when I get angry." **Objective** Tearful Irritable | **ACTUAL** Ineffective coping related to prolonged caregiving stress as evidenced by guilt and tearfulness. | Caregiver uses two healthy ways of coping. | Listen and let her know her feelings are normal. Suggest support groups. Teach ways to reduce stress. | Coping: goal met. |
| **Subjective** *No specific complaint* **Objective** Cut off from others Has stopped visiting friends | **RISK** Risk for loneliness as evidenced by risk factors: social isolation of the caregiver. | Keeps in regular touch with other people. | Encourage her to see her friends. Plan regular breaks. Put her in touch with caregiver support services. | Keeps in touch: goal met. |

**APPENDICES**

# Reference Material

## Appendix A  Assessment Scales

**Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS)**

| **Component** | **Response** | **Score** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Eye opening | Spontaneous | 4 |
|  | To sound | 3 |
|  | To pressure | 2 |
|  | None | 1 |
| Verbal response | Oriented | 5 |
|  | Confused | 4 |
|  | Words | 3 |
|  | Sounds | 2 |
|  | None | 1 |
| Motor response | Obeys commands | 6 |
|  | Localizing | 5 |
|  | Normal flexion | 4 |
|  | Abnormal flexion | 3 |
|  | Extension | 2 |
|  | None | 1 |

The total runs from 3 to 15. At 8 or below, the patient is usually severely impaired and the airway is at risk. Write each part down separately (for example E3 V4 M6).

**Morse Fall Scale**

| **Item** | **Finding** | **Points** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| History of falling (within 3 months) | No / Yes | 0 / 25 |
| Secondary diagnosis | No / Yes | 0 / 15 |
| Ambulatory aid | None, bed rest or nurse assist | 0 |
|  | Crutches, cane or walker | 15 |
|  | Holds on to furniture | 30 |
| IV therapy or saline lock | No / Yes | 0 / 20 |
| Gait | Normal, bed rest or wheelchair | 0 |
|  | Weak | 10 |
|  | Impaired | 20 |
| Mental status | Oriented to own ability | 0 |
|  | Overestimates or forgets limitations | 15 |

The total runs from 0 to 125. Many hospitals call 0–24 low risk, 25–44 moderate risk and 45 or more high risk, but go by your own local policy.

## Assessment Scales (continued)

**Braden Scale for Pressure Injury Risk**

| **Subscale** | **What is assessed** | **Score range** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Sensory perception | Ability to respond to pressure-related discomfort | 1–4 |
| Moisture | Degree to which skin is exposed to moisture | 1–4 |
| Activity | Degree of physical activity | 1–4 |
| Mobility | Ability to change and control body position | 1–4 |
| Nutrition | Usual food intake pattern | 1–4 |
| Friction and shear | Sliding and dragging of skin against surfaces | 1–3 |

The total runs from 6 to 23, and the lower the score, the higher the risk. Bands in common use: 15–18 mild risk, 13–14 moderate risk, 10–12 high risk, 9 or less very high risk.

**Pain Assessment Scales**

- Numeric Rating Scale (NRS): the patient gives the pain a number from 0 (no pain) to 10 (worst pain imaginable). Works for adults and older children.

- Faces pain scales: the child points to the face that looks like their pain. Works from about 3–4 years of age.

- FLACC scale: for babies and children who cannot tell you about their pain. There are five items, each scored 0–2, so the total is 0–10.

| **FLACC item** | **0** | **1** | **2** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Face | No particular expression or smile | Occasional grimace or frown, withdrawn | Frequent to constant frown, clenched jaw, quivering chin |
| Legs | Normal position or relaxed | Uneasy, restless, tense | Kicking or legs drawn up |
| Activity | Lying quietly, moves easily | Squirming, shifting, tense | Arched, rigid or jerking |
| Cry | No cry | Moans or whimpers, occasional complaint | Crying steadily, screams or sobs |
| Consolability | Content, relaxed | Reassured by touch or talking | Difficult to console or comfort |

## Appendix B  Normal Laboratory Values (Adults)

Every laboratory has its own reference ranges, depending on its methods, so always go by the range your local lab prints. The figures below are typical adult values, given here for study.

| **Test** | **Typical adult range** | **Conventional units** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Hemoglobin (Hb) | Male 13.5–17.5 g/dL; female 12.0–15.5 g/dL |  |
| White blood cells (WBC) | 4.0–11.0 × 10⁹/L |  |
| Platelets | 150–400 × 10⁹/L |  |
| Sodium (Na⁺) | 135–145 mmol/L |  |
| Potassium (K⁺) | 3.5–5.0 mmol/L |  |
| Chloride | 98–106 mmol/L |  |
| Bicarbonate (HCO₃⁻) | 22–26 mmol/L |  |
| Calcium (total) | 2.2–2.6 mmol/L | 8.5–10.5 mg/dL |
| Magnesium | 0.7–1.0 mmol/L | 1.7–2.4 mg/dL |
| Urea / BUN | 2.5–7.8 mmol/L | BUN 7–20 mg/dL |
| Creatinine | Male 60–110 µmol/L; female 45–90 µmol/L | About 0.6–1.2 mg/dL |
| Fasting blood glucose | 3.9–5.5 mmol/L | 70–99 mg/dL |
| HbA1c | Below 5.7% (non-diabetic range) |  |
| Albumin | 35–50 g/L | 3.5–5.0 g/dL |
| Total bilirubin | Below 21 µmol/L | About 0.3–1.2 mg/dL |
| INR (not on anticoagulant) | 0.8–1.2 |  |
| Lactate | Below 2 mmol/L |  |
| CRP | Below 5 mg/L |  |
| Arterial pH | 7.35–7.45 |  |
| PaCO₂ | 35–45 mmHg (4.7–6.0 kPa) |  |
| PaO₂ (room air) | 80–100 mmHg (10.7–13.3 kPa) |  |
| SpO₂ | 95–100% (88–92% target in some COPD patients) |  |

## Appendix C  Drug Calculations

**Basic formulas**

| **Purpose** | **Formula** |
| --- | --- |
| Tablets or liquid dose | Dose required ÷ dose available × volume (or quantity) available |
| IV infusion rate (mL/hour) | Total volume (mL) ÷ time (hours) |
| Drip rate (drops/minute) | Volume (mL) × drop factor (drops/mL) ÷ time (minutes) |
| Weight-based dose | Dose per kg × body weight (kg) |
| Infusion in mcg/kg/min to mL/hour | Dose (mcg/kg/min) × weight (kg) × 60 ÷ concentration (mcg/mL) |

**Worked examples**

- Paracetamol 750 mg is ordered. Available: 500 mg in 5 mL. 750 ÷ 500 × 5 = 7.5 mL.

- 1000 mL of normal saline over 8 hours: 1000 ÷ 8 = 125 mL/hour.

- 1000 mL over 8 hours with a giving set of 20 drops/mL: 1000 × 20 ÷ 480 = 41.7, so about 42 drops/minute.

- Child weighing 18 kg, paracetamol 15 mg/kg per dose: 15 × 18 = 270 mg. Suspension 120 mg in 5 mL: 270 ÷ 120 × 5 = 11.25 mL.

- Heparin 25,000 units in 500 mL (50 units/mL); order 1,000 units/hour: 1,000 ÷ 50 = 20 mL/hour.

- Dopamine 5 mcg/kg/min for a 70 kg patient; 400 mg in 250 mL (1,600 mcg/mL): 5 × 70 × 60 ÷ 1,600 = 13.1 mL/hour.

For high-alert medicines such as insulin, heparin, potassium and opioids, have a second nurse check your calculation independently, and follow local policy.
